as follows, where
Z^{\prime}
denotes the appropriate normalization constant:

\text{ for all }B\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}\,,\,G_{C}(B)=\frac{1}{Z^{\prime}}\int\mathbf{1}\big\{B\cap C\cap\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\big\}\exp\Big(U(\theta)-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}\theta\,.

Assume that for some
a>0
we have

Z^{\prime}\geq\exp(-ad)\,.

Then for
K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\geq 2
depending on
a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},K_{\textsc{signal}},\beta_{\textsc{signal}}
,
K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\geq 2
depending on
a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},K_{\textsc{signal}}
, and
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\geq 1
depending on
a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},K_{\textsc{signal}},\beta_{\textsc{signal}}
, we have the following.
Consider
s\sim\mathcal{D}
and
\bar{g}\sim N(0,I_{d-1})
such that
s,\bar{g}
are independent.
If
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\frac{1}{K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}}
, then
G_{C}
satisfies the following implication:

\displaystyle\text{If }\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}}\Big)\leq t\leq\frac{1}{4}\exp\Big(-K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\max\{1,\kappa\}^{2}\Big)\,,

(B.28)

\displaystyle\text{ then }\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(G_{C}\Big(\Big\{\theta\,:\,\frac{\theta_{1}s+\langle\bar{\theta},\bar{g}\rangle}{\sqrt{d}}\geq\kappa\Big\}\Big)\leq t\Bigg)\leq 16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}t^{1/K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\,.

Proof.

We define for all
\theta
,

\displaystyle X(\theta):=\frac{s\theta_{1}+\langle\bar{\theta},\bar{g}\rangle}{\sqrt{d}}\,,

and we let
X_{\ell}:=X(\theta^{\ell})
for all replicas
1\leq\ell\leq L
.

First, we prove that the conditions of Lemma
B.7
apply with high enough probability over
G_{C}
. By Theorem
B.8
applied with the convex set
\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C
,
for some
0\leq c^{\prime}_{1}<c^{\prime}_{2}<c^{\prime}_{3}=c^{\prime}_{3}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1})
with
c^{\prime}_{3}\geq\frac{1}{c^{\prime}_{2}-c^{\prime}_{1}}
, we have (
B.26
), (
B.27
). (Crucially
c^{\prime}_{3}
does not explicitly depend on
I
, in particular it does not depend on
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, which will be very important in the following arguments.) Since
G_{C}\big(\{\theta\,:\,\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\not\in I\}\big)=0
(recall the constraint
\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I
is present in the definition of
G_{C}
from this Theorem), it follows that

\displaystyle G_{C}\Bigl(\big\{\theta:c^{\prime}_{2}d\leq\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}\leq c^{\prime}_{3}d\,,\,\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\big\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\exp\Bigl(-\frac{d}{c^{\prime}_{3}}\Bigr)\,,

(B.29)

\displaystyle G_{C}^{\otimes 2}\Bigl(\big\{(\theta^{1},\theta^{2}):|\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},\bar{\theta}^{2}\rangle|\leq c^{\prime}_{1}d\,,\,\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\big\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\exp\Bigl(-\frac{d}{c^{\prime}_{3}}\Bigr)\,.

(B.30)

Now note for any
\theta^{\ell},\theta^{\ell_{1}},\theta^{\ell_{2}}
, we have as
\bar{g}\sim N(0,I_{d-1})
independent of
s
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[X^{2}_{\ell}\big]=\frac{1}{d}\Big((\theta_{1}^{\ell})^{2}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[s^{2}]+\|\bar{\theta}^{\ell}\|^{2}\Big)\quad,\quad\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[X_{\ell_{1}}X_{\ell_{2}}\big]=\frac{1}{d}\Big(\theta_{1}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{1}^{\ell_{2}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[s^{2}]+\langle\bar{\theta}^{\ell_{1}},\bar{\theta}^{\ell_{2}}\rangle\Big)\,.

Consequently for
\theta^{\ell}
such that
c^{\prime}_{2}d\leq\|\bar{\theta}^{\ell}\|^{2}\leq c^{\prime}_{3}d\,,\,(\theta^{\ell})_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[X^{2}_{\ell}\big]

\displaystyle\leq c^{\prime}_{3}+\max\big\{|x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}|,|x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}|\big\}^{2}K_{\textsc{signal}})\leq c^{\prime}_{3}+4K_{\textsc{signal}}\,,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[X^{2}_{\ell}\big]

\displaystyle\geq c^{\prime}_{2}+(x^{2}-3\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[s^{2}]\,.

Similarly for
(\theta^{\ell_{1}},\theta^{\ell_{2}})
such that
|\langle\theta^{\ell_{1}},\theta^{\ell_{2}}\rangle|\leq c^{\prime}_{1}d\,,\,(\theta^{\ell_{1}})_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\,,\,(\theta^{\ell_{2}})_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[X_{\ell_{1}}X_{\ell_{2}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq c^{\prime}_{1}+(x^{2}+3\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[s^{2}]\,.

Define

\displaystyle c_{3}=2c^{\prime}_{3}+4K_{\textsc{signal}}\,,\,c_{1}:=c^{\prime}_{1}+x^{2}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[s^{2}]+\frac{1}{4c^{\prime}_{3}}\,,\,c_{2}:=c^{\prime}_{2}+x^{2}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[s^{2}]-\frac{1}{4c^{\prime}_{3}}\,.

(B.31)

Let

\displaystyle K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},K_{\textsc{signal}})=K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}(c_{3},K_{\textsc{signal}})\geq 12c_{3}K_{\textsc{signal}}\,.

(B.32)

Hence since
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\frac{1}{K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},K_{\textsc{signal}})}\leq\frac{1}{12c_{3}K_{\textsc{signal}}}
, it follows that for such
\theta
satisfying the conditions from (
B.29
) we have
c_{3}\geq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[X^{2}_{\ell}]\geq c_{2}
, and similarly for
\theta^{1},\theta^{2}
satisfying the conditions from (
B.30
) we have
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[X_{\ell_{1}}X_{\ell_{2}}]\leq c_{1}
.
As
c_{3}\geq c^{\prime}_{3}
, and as
G_{C}\big(\{\theta\,:\,\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\not\in I\}\big)=0
, it follows that

\displaystyle G_{C}\Bigl(\big\{\theta:c_{2}\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[X^{2}_{\ell}]\leq c_{3}\big\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\exp\Bigl(-\frac{d}{c_{3}}\Bigr)\,,

(B.33)

\displaystyle G_{C}^{\otimes 2}\Bigl(\big\{(\theta^{1},\theta^{2}):\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[X_{\ell_{1}}X_{\ell_{2}}]\leq c_{1}\big\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\exp\Bigl(-\frac{d}{c_{3}}\Bigr)\,.

(B.34)

Consider
L
such that
2L^{2}\leq\exp(d/c_{3})
and define

\displaystyle E^{L}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{(\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\,:\,c_{2}\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[X^{2}_{\ell}]\leq c_{3}\,\forall\,\ell\,,\,\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[X_{\ell_{1}}X_{\ell_{2}}]\leq c_{1}\,\forall\,\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}\,,\,\theta^{\ell}/\sqrt{d}\in I\,\forall\,\ell\Big\}\,.

(B.35)

Now observe that the conditions of Lemma
B.7
apply with
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}
chosen in (
B.31
) because we have
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\frac{1}{K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}(c_{3},K_{\textsc{signal}})}
, and because
c^{\prime}_{1}\geq 0
and therefore

\displaystyle c_{2}-c_{1}=c^{\prime}_{2}-c^{\prime}_{1}-\frac{1}{2c^{\prime}_{3}}\geq\frac{1}{2c^{\prime}_{3}}\geq\frac{1}{c_{3}}\quad,\quad c_{1}>c^{\prime}_{1}+x^{2}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[s^{2}]\geq x^{2}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[s^{2}]\,.

Applying Lemma
B.7
, we obtain the following. For any
(\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}
and
t
satisfying

\displaystyle L^{-1/K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\leq t\leq\exp\big(-K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\max\{1,\kappa\}^{2}\big)\,,

(B.36)

where
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}=K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}(c_{3},K_{\textsc{signal}},\beta_{\textsc{signal}})\geq 1
comes from Lemma
B.7
with
c_{3}
defined in (
B.31
), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\leq tL\Big)\leq K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\,t^{1/K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}^{2}}\,,

(B.37)

where probability in the above is over
\bar{g},s
.

Consider the event
G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\geq L\,:\,X_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\geq tL\Big)
. Observe that when
G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\geq tL\Big)\geq\frac{1}{4}
, we have by Linearity of Expectation,

\displaystyle G_{C}\big(\big\{\theta\,:\,X(\theta)\geq\kappa\big\}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L}\int\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X(\theta^{\ell})\geq\kappa\big\}\,\mathrm{d}G^{\otimes L}_{C}(\theta)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{L}\cdot tL\cdot G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\geq tL\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{t}{4}\,.

(B.38)

Next observe that

\displaystyle G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\geq tL\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=G_{C}^{\otimes L}(E^{L})-G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\leq tL\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\geq 1-L^{2}\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{c_{3}}\Big)-G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\leq tL\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\frac{1}{2}-G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\leq tL\Big)\,,

(B.39)

where we apply the condition on
L
and use (
B.33
), (
B.34
) and that
G_{C}\big(\{\theta:\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\not\in I\}\big)=0
.

Combining (
B.1.1
), (
B.1.1
), it follows that as events we have

\displaystyle\Bigg\{G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\leq tL\Big)\leq\frac{1}{4}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\subseteq\Bigg\{G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\geq tL\Big)\geq\frac{1}{4}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\quad\subseteq\Big\{G_{C}\big(\big\{\theta\,:\,X(\theta)\geq\kappa\big\}\big)\geq\frac{t}{4}\Big\}\,.

(B.40)

Now by Markov’s Inequality and (
B.37
), we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\leq tL\Big)\geq\frac{1}{4}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 4\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\leq tL\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=4\int\mathbf{1}\big\{(\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\big\}\,\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\leq tL\Big\}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}G_{C}^{\otimes L}(\theta)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 4K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}t^{1/K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\,.

Therefore by the contrapositive of (
B.40
), combining with the above display yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Big(G_{C}\big(\big\{\theta\,:\,X(\theta)\geq\kappa\big\}\big)\leq\frac{t}{4}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(G_{C}^{\otimes L}\Big((\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\in E^{L}\,:\,\#\big\{\ell\leq L\,:\,X^{\prime}_{\ell}\geq\kappa\big\}\leq tL\Big)\geq\frac{1}{4}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 4K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}t^{1/K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\,.

(B.41)

Hence, (
B.36
) implies (
B.41
). The result follows by taking
4t
in place of
t
in this implication, noting
4^{1/K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\leq 4
as
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\geq 1
, and recalling this holds for any
L
with
2L^{2}\leq\exp(d/c_{3})
.
∎

Now we find a generic way to upper bound random variables satisfying the implication in Theorem
B.9
. This is analogous to Lemma 8.3.8 of
[
Tal11
]
, but we require some more generality.

Lemma B.10
.

Consider a random variable
V\geq 0
and assume that for certain numbers
C_{0},C_{1},C_{2},D_{0}\geq 1
and some
a\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq\exp(ad))=0\,,

\displaystyle D_{0}\leq t\leq\exp(d/C_{0})\implies\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq t)\leq C_{1}t^{-1/C_{2}}\,.

Then we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V^{\gamma}]\leq K_{\textup{\ref{lem:momentsofRV838tal}}}=D_{0}+2C_{1}C_{2}+C_{1}\quad\text{ for }\quad\gamma=\max\Big\{1,\frac{1}{2C_{2}},\frac{1}{aC_{0}C_{2}}\Big\}\in(0,1)\,.

Proof.

As
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq\exp(ad))=0
, we may write

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[V^{\gamma}]

\displaystyle=\gamma\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\gamma-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\gamma\int_{0}^{D_{0}}t^{\gamma-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq t)\,\mathrm{d}t+\gamma\int_{D_{0}}^{\exp(d/C_{0})}t^{\gamma-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq t)\,\mathrm{d}t+\gamma\int_{\exp(d/C_{0})}^{\exp(ad)}t^{\gamma-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq t)\,\mathrm{d}t\,.

We upper bound these three integrals as follows:

•

First, we have that

\displaystyle\gamma\int_{0}^{D_{0}}t^{\gamma-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq t)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq t^{\gamma}\Big|_{0}^{D_{0}}=D_{0}^{\gamma}\leq D_{0}\,.

•

Second, as
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2C_{2}}\leq 1
we have by the upper bound on
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq t)
that

\displaystyle\gamma\int_{D_{0}}^{\exp(d/C_{0})}t^{\gamma-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\gamma C_{1}\int_{D_{0}}^{\exp(d/C_{0})}t^{\gamma-1-1/C_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\cdot 2C_{2}D_{0}^{-1/2C_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{1}C_{2}\,,

where we used that
D_{0}\geq 1
.

•

Third, we have by the upper bound on
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq t)
that

\displaystyle\gamma\int_{\exp(d/C_{0})}^{\exp(ad)}t^{\gamma-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(V\geq t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\gamma C_{1}\int_{\exp(d/C_{0})}^{\exp(ad)}t^{\gamma-1-1/C_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\gamma C_{1}\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{C_{0}C_{2}}\Big)\int_{\exp(d/C_{0})}^{\exp(ad)}t^{\gamma-1}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma C_{1}\exp\big(-\frac{d}{C_{0}C_{2}}\big)}{\gamma}\cdot\exp(a\gamma d)\leq C_{1}\,,

as
t\geq\exp(d/C_{0})
and
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{aC_{0}C_{2}}
.

The Lemma is proved.
∎

We will also need the following useful fact:

Lemma B.11
.

Consider any
U\leq 0
defined on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, an interval
I\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, and a convex set
C\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
, define a measure
G_{C}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
as follows, where
Z^{\prime}
denotes the appropriate normalization constant:

\text{ for all }B\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}\,,\,G_{C}(B)=\frac{1}{Z^{\prime}}\int_{B\cap C}\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\,\exp\Big(U(\theta)-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}\theta\,.

Assume that for some
a>0
we have

Z^{\prime}\geq\exp(-ad)\,.

Then for
r\leq r_{\textup{\ref{lem:upperboundintegral}}}
where
r_{\textup{\ref{lem:upperboundintegral}}}
depends on
a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1}
, we have

G_{C}\Big(\big\{\|\bar{\theta}\|\leq r\sqrt{d}\big\}\Big)\leq\exp(-d)\,.

Proof.

By the condition on
Z^{\prime}
and as
U\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle G_{C}\big(\big\{\|\bar{\theta}\|\leq r\sqrt{d}\big\}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(ad)\int_{\|\bar{\theta}\|\leq r\sqrt{d}}\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\,\exp\big(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle=\exp(ad)\Big(\frac{\beta}{\pi}\Big)^{d/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\,\exp(-\beta\theta_{1}^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta\cdot\int_{\|\bar{\theta}\|\leq r\sqrt{d}}\exp\big(-\beta\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\bar{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(d(a+K(\beta))\big)\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{Z}\big(\|Z\|\leq r\sqrt{d}\big)\,,

where
Z\sim N\big(0,\frac{1}{\beta}I_{d-1}\big)
. Standard bounds on the concentration of the norm of multivariate Guassians (see e.g. Theorem 3.1.1,
[
Ver18
]
) now give that for suitable
r_{\textup{\ref{lem:upperboundintegral}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1})
and for all
r\leq r_{\textup{\ref{lem:upperboundintegral}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1})
, we have
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{Z}\big(\|Z\|\leq r\sqrt{d}\big)\leq\exp\big(-d(a+K(\beta)+1)\big)
. This yields the desired conclusion.
∎

B.1.2
Proof of Theorem
B.1

We now have established the technical ingredients necessary to prove Theorem
B.1
. As discussed earlier, the proof proceeds through two key Lemmas. In Lemma
B.12
we show concentration of the normalized free energy around its expectation for quite general mollified Hamiltonians. This generality is needed as we apply this result for both
u(\cdot)
from Theorem
A.1
and with
\exp u(x)=\mathbf{1}\{x\geq\kappa\}
. In Lemma
B.14
we show the impact of the mollifier on the expectation is small.

Since these arguments will involve changing the mollifier
u(\cdot)
, overloading notation, we make the following definitions for only Appendix
B.1
.
For any function
u(\cdot)
and interval
I\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, let

\displaystyle H_{u}(\theta):=\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}u(S_{i})\quad\text{and}\quad Z_{u}(I):=\int\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\Big(H_{u}(\theta)-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,.

(B.42)

We do not vary
\beta
in this proof, so there will be no ambiguity in the above definitions.

Next, for
A\geq 0
, we define the truncated logarithm

\displaystyle\log_{A}x:=\max(\log x,-A)\,.

(B.43)

Several useful properties of the truncated logarithm, Lemmas
F.2
,
F.3
,
F.4
, are presented in Appendix
F.1
. The reason we introduce the truncated logarithm is to truncate various partition functions
Z
by
\log_{ad}(Z)
, allowing us to use Theorem
B.9
. Later in the proof when we conclude Theorem
B.1
, we will choose
a
large enough so the truncation is of no impact with high probability using the concentration result Lemma
B.12
.

Our first step is to show strong concentration properties of
\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)
. From here on out, following the convention of this paper excluding Appendix
B.1.1
, we express dependence on the quantities
K_{\textsc{signal}},\beta_{\textsc{signal}}
through the signal-to-noise ratio
\lambda
governing them.

Lemma B.12
.

Consider any
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
,
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
, and
a>0
. Then whenever
u\leq 0
is concave with
u(x)=0
for all
x\geq\kappa
, we have the following. Consider any interval
I=[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]
with
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\frac{1}{K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}}
where
K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}
depends on
a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
. Then for all
d
large enough in terms of
a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
and all
n\leq 2d
, we have for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{d}\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)\Big]\Big|\geq t\Bigr)\leq 2\exp\Bigl(-\frac{d}{K(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}\min\{t^{2},t\}\Bigr)\,.

(B.44)

Note Lemma
B.12
applies for any concave
u\leq 0
with
u(x)=0
for all
x\geq\kappa
, and does not impose further regularity assumptions on
u
. In particular, it applies for
u
given by
\exp u(x)=\mathbf{1}\{x\geq\kappa\}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{F}_{i}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the randomness of the first
i
samples. Let
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}
denote conditional expectation w.r.t.
\mathfrak{F}_{i}
. Let

\displaystyle X_{i}(I):=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}\Big[\frac{1}{d}\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)\Big]-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\Big[\frac{1}{d}\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)\Big]\,.

(B.45)

Note
X_{i}(I)
is
\mathfrak{F}_{i}
-measurable and
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}[X_{i}(I)]=0
.
First, we claim it suffices to prove for all
i
that:

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\Big[\exp\Big(\frac{d\,|X_{i}(I)|}{K(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}\Big)\Big]\leq 2\,,

(B.46)

To this end, note that upon establishing (
B.46
) for all
1\leq i\leq n
, applying Bernstein’s Inequality on the
X_{i}(I)
and recalling
n\leq 2d
yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Biggl(\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log_{ad}Z_{\beta}(I)-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{d}\log_{ad}Z_{\beta}(I)\Big]\Big|\geq t\Biggr)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Biggl(\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}(I)\Big|\geq t\leq 2\exp\Bigl(-\frac{d\min\{t^{2},t\}}{K(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}\Bigr)\Bigg)\,.

The rest of the proof of Lemma
B.12
is now devoted to showing (
B.46
).
We define

\displaystyle W_{i}(I)

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i,1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n}u(S_{i^{\prime}})-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,,

(B.47)

\displaystyle Y_{1}(I)

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{1}\Big\{W_{i}(I)\geq\exp(-ad)\Big\}\Big|\log_{ad}\Big(\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}\Big)\Big|\,,

(B.48)

\displaystyle Y_{2}(I)

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{1}\Big\{\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}<\exp(-ad),W_{i}(I)>1\Big\}\Big|\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)-\log_{ad}W_{i}(I)\Big|\,,

(B.49)

\displaystyle Y(I)

\displaystyle:=Y_{1}(I)+Y_{2}(I)\,.

(B.50)

Now, we begin with some preparatory work to show (
B.46
).

Lemma B.13
.

We have the pointwise upper bound

\displaystyle\Big|\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)-\log_{ad}W_{i}(I)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq Y(I)\,.

Proof of Lemma
B.13
.

Notice
Z_{u}(I)\leq W_{i}(I)
as
u\leq 0
. We break into the three possible cases:

•

If
W_{i}(I)\leq\exp(-ad)
, then the left hand side is 0 so the above holds.

•

If
Z_{u}(I)\leq\exp(-ad)<W_{i}(I)
: If
W_{i}(I)\leq 1
, the above follows as a consequence of Lemma
F.2
on truncated logarithms. Else if
W_{i}(I)>1
, we must have
Z_{u}(I)/W_{i}(I)\leq\exp(-ad)
in this case, and therefore the left hand side above is upper bounded by
Y_{2}(I)
.

•

If
Z_{u}(I),W_{i}(I)>\exp(-ad)
: First suppose
W_{i}(I)\leq 1
. Then this is again a consequence of Lemma
F.2
. Else if
W_{i}(I)>1
and
\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}\geq\exp(-ad)
, then
Z_{u}(I)\geq\exp(-ad)
, and the Lemma follows by definition of logarithms. Finally if
W_{i}(I)>1
and
\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}<\exp(-ad)
, the left hand side above is upper bounded by
Y_{2}(I)
.

This proves Lemma
B.13
.
∎

Notice
W_{i}(I)
does not depend on the randomness defining the
i
-th sample. Therefore we have that
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}\Big[\log_{ad}W_{i}(I)\Big]=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\Big[\log_{ad}W_{i}(I)\Big]
.
Lemma
B.13
thus gives

\displaystyle d\big|X_{i}(I)\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)\big]-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\beta}(I)\big]\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)-\log_{ad}W_{i}(I)\big]-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)-\log_{ad}W_{i}(I)\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)-\log_{ad}W_{i}(I)\big|\Big]+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)-\log_{ad}W_{i}(I)\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}\big[|Y(I)|\big]+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\big[|Y(I)|\big]\,.

Thus for any
\gamma>0
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma d\big|X_{i}(I)\big|\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}|Y(I)|\big)\exp\big(\gamma\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}|Y(I)|\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\gamma\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\big[|Y(I)|\big]\Big)\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}|Y(I)|\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma|Y(I)|\big)\Big]\cdot\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma|Y(I)|\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma|Y(I)|\big)\Big]^{2}\,.

(B.51)

Let
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
denote expectation in the randomness defining the
i
-th sample, thus
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
. Hence

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i-1}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma Y(I)\big)\Big]=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}^{i}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma Y(I)\big)\Big]\,.

Now, note it suffices to show that for
\gamma=\gamma(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)>0
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma Y(I)\big)\Big]\leq K_{\textup{\ref{eq:secondgoal}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,.

(B.52)

This is because Jensen’s Inequality then implies

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\exp\Big(\frac{\gamma}{K_{\textup{\ref{eq:secondgoal}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}Y(I)\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma Y(I)\big)\Big]^{\frac{1}{K_{\textup{\ref{eq:secondgoal}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}}

\displaystyle\leq K_{\textup{\ref{eq:secondgoal}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)^{1/K_{\textup{\ref{eq:secondgoal}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}\leq 2\,,

hence implying (
B.46
) when combined with the above.

If
W_{i}(I)\leq\exp(-ad)
then
Y(I)=0
, and (
B.52
) immediately follows. Thus suppose from now on that
W_{i}(I)\geq\exp(-ad)
; we will establish (
B.52
). Note this step is permitted because
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
is only with respect to the randomness defining the
i
-th sample, while
W_{i}(I)
does not depend on this randomness, and hence the randomness defining
W_{i}(I)
and
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
are independent.

By AM-GM, to show (
B.52
), it suffices to show for some
\gamma=\gamma(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa)>0
that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma Y_{1}(I)\big)\Big]\,,\,\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma Y_{2}(I)\big)\Big]\leq K(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,.

(B.53)

To this end, define the measure
G_{i}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by

G_{i}(\theta)\propto\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\,\exp\Big(\sum_{1\leq i^{\prime}\leq n,i^{\prime}\neq i}u(S_{i^{\prime}})-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\,.

Note as the normalizing constant of
G_{i}(\theta)
is exactly
W_{i}(I)
by (
B.47
), and as
u(x)=0
for all
x\geq\kappa
and
u(x)\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}=\int\exp u(S_{i})\,\mathrm{d}G_{i}(\theta)\quad,\quad 1\geq\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}=\int\exp u(S_{i})\,\mathrm{d}G_{i}(\theta)\geq G_{i}(S_{i}\geq\kappa)\,.

(B.54)

We apply Theorem
B.9
with
U(\theta)
defined by
\exp(U(\theta))=\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\big(\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}u(S_{i^{\prime}})\big)
.
Note
U\leq 0
is concave as
u\leq 0
is concave and the
S_{i}
are affine.
Since
G_{i}(S_{i}\geq\kappa)\leq Z_{u}(I)/W_{i}(I)
from (
B.54
), and as
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\frac{1}{K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}}
in the statement of this Lemma and the condition
W_{i}(I)\geq\exp(-ad)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\exp\big(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\big)\leq t\leq\frac{1}{4}\exp\big(-K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\max\{1,\kappa\}^{2}\big)\implies\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}\leq t\Bigg)\leq 16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}t^{1/K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\,,

(B.55)

for
K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}=K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda),K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}=K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\geq 2
and
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}=K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\geq 1
.

Upper bounding
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\big[\exp(\gamma Y_{1}(I))\big]
.

We apply Lemma
B.10
with

\displaystyle V=\exp\big(Y_{1}(I)\big)\,,\,C_{0}=K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\,,\,C_{1}=16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\,,\,C_{2}=K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\,,\,D_{0}=4\exp\big(K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\max\{1,\kappa\}^{2}\big)\,.

As
Z_{u}(I)\leq W_{i}(I)
by (
B.54
), we have
Y_{1}(I)=\Big|\log_{ad}\Big(\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}\Big)\Big|
and
\exp Y_{1}(I)\leq\min\Big\{\frac{W_{i}(I)}{Z_{u}(I)},\exp(ad)\Big\}
.
Thus by (
B.55
), it follows that the conditions for Lemma
B.10
are satisfied. Thus there is such a
\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{1}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)>0
for which

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\exp\big(\gamma_{1}\,Y_{1}(I)\big)\Big]\leq K(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,.

(B.56)

Upper bounding
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\big[\exp(\gamma Y_{2}(I))\big]
.

As
u\leq 0
, we have the pointwise bound

Z_{u}(I)\,,\,W_{i}(I)\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\big(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta=\Big(\frac{2\pi}{\beta}\Big)^{d/2}\,.

By definition of truncated logarithm, this implies

\big|\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)-\log_{ad}W_{i}(I)\big|\leq K(a,\beta_{0})d\,.

Thus for all
\gamma>0
, we have from the definition of
Y_{2}(I)
in (
B.49
) that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\big[\exp(\gamma Y_{2})\big]

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\Big(\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}<\exp(-ad)\Big)\exp\big(\gamma K(a,\beta_{0})d\big)+1\,.

(B.57)

Our strategy now will be to show
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\big(Z_{u}(I)/W_{i}(I)<\exp(-ad)\big)
is exponentially small in
d
, and then choose
\gamma
small enough to upper bound (
B.57
).
First, note as
d>K(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
, we have

\displaystyle\exp\big(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\big)\,,\,\exp(-ad)\leq\frac{1}{4}\exp\big(-K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\max\{1,\kappa\}^{2}\big)\,.

(B.58)

Suppose
\exp(-ad)\geq\exp\big(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\big)
. By (
B.58
), the condition for (
B.55
) with
t=\exp(-ad)
thus holds, and (
B.55
) yields

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\Big(\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}<\exp(-ad)\Big)\leq 16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\exp\big(-ad/K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\big)\,.

Else, say
\exp(-ad)<\exp\big(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\big)
. By (
B.58
), the conditions for (
B.55
) with
t=\exp\big(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\big)
thus holds, and (
B.55
) yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\Big(\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}<\exp(-ad)\Big)\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\Big(\frac{Z_{u}(I)}{W_{i}(I)}<\exp\big(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\big)\Big)\leq 16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\Big)\,.

Combining with (
B.57
) implies that for all
\gamma>0
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\big[\exp(\gamma Y_{2})\big]\leq 16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\exp\Big(-d\min\Big\{\frac{a}{K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}},\frac{1}{K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\Big\}+\gamma K(a,\beta_{0})d\Big)\,.

Taking

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}=\gamma_{2}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)=\frac{1}{2K(a,\beta_{0})}\min\Big\{\frac{a}{K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}},\frac{1}{K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\Big\}>0

to be small enough, we obtain from the above that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\big[\exp(\gamma_{2}Y_{2})\big]\leq 16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}+1\,.

(B.59)

Taking
\gamma=\min\{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\}>0
, as
Y_{1}(I),Y_{2}(I)\geq 0
, we obtain from (
B.56
), (
B.59
) that

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\big[\exp(\lambda Y_{1}(I))\big]\,,\,\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\big[\exp(\lambda Y_{2}(I))\big]\leq K(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,,

which establishes (
B.53
) and thus finishes the proof of Lemma
B.12
.
∎

Our next goal is to show that when the
\varepsilon^{\prime}
defining the mollifiers
u(\cdot)
are chosen suitably, the mollification has only a minor impact on the free energy.

Lemma B.14
.

For any
a>0
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, there is
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
depending on
\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
such that for any concave
u\leq 0
satisfying

u(x)=0\text{ for all }x\geq\kappa\text{ and }\exp u\big(\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}\big)\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\,,

the following holds. For all
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
, any
x\in[-1,1]
, and any interval
I=[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]
with
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}<\frac{1}{K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}}
where
K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}
depends on
a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
, we have for all
d\geq d(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and all
n\leq 2d
,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\beta}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)\big]\Big|\leq\varepsilon\,,

(B.60)

where we recall the definition of
Z_{\beta}(I)
from
(
B.2
)
and
Z_{u}(I)
from
(
B.42
)
.

Proof.

We replace each
\mathbf{1}\{U_{i}\}
with the corresponding mollified quantity
\exp\big(u(S_{i})\big)
and show this leads to only a small difference per
i
. For
i\leq n
, define

\displaystyle C_{i}:=\bigcap_{i^{\prime}<i}U_{i^{\prime}}\quad,\quad V_{i}(I):=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{C_{i}\cap\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\big\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i\leq i^{\prime}\leq n}u(S_{i^{\prime}})-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,.

(B.61)

The quantity of interest (
B.60
) in the statement of the Lemma is thus

\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}V_{1}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}V_{n+1}(I)\big]\Big|\leq\frac{1}{d}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\Big|\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}V_{i+1}(I)\big]-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}V_{i}(I)\big]\Big|\,.

We will bound each of the terms above. To do so, consider any
i,1\leq i\leq n
. We use
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
to denote expectation and
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}
to denote probability in the randomness defining the
i
-th sample. Notice

\displaystyle\Big|\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}V_{i+1}(I)\big]-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}V_{i}(I)\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}V_{i+1}(I)-\log_{ad}V_{i}(I)\big|\Big]\,.

We will prove for any
i,1\leq i\leq n
that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}V_{i+1}(I)-\log_{ad}V_{i}(I)\big|\Big]\leq K\varepsilon\,,

(B.62)

where
K>0
is a universal constant independent of everything else. Upon scaling
\varepsilon
by a universal constant, (
B.62
) proves the desired result.

To this end, define the Gibbs measure
G_{i}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
as follows: for all subsets
B\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we let

G_{i}(B):=\frac{1}{Z_{i}(I)}\int\mathbf{1}\big\{C_{i}\cap B\cap\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\big\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i<i^{\prime}\leq n}u(S_{i^{\prime}})-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,,

where

Z_{i}(I):=\int_{C_{i}}\mathbf{1}\big\{C_{i}\cap\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\big\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i<i^{\prime}\leq n}u(S_{i^{\prime}})-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta

is the normalizing constant. Recalling the definition of
C_{i}
from (
B.61
), note that
Z_{i}(I)
does not depend on the randomness defining the
i
-th sample.

Let
\langle\cdot\rangle
denote expectation w.r.t.
G_{i}
. Recalling the definition of
C_{i}
from (
B.61
) once more, we notice for analogous reasons as above,
\langle\cdot\rangle
does not depend on the randomness defining the
i
-th sample. Next, notice

\displaystyle V_{i}(I)

\displaystyle=Z_{i}(I)\,\big\langle\exp u(S_{i})\big\rangle\,,

(B.63)

\displaystyle V_{i+1}(I)

\displaystyle=Z_{i}(I)\,\big\langle\mathbf{1}\{U_{i}\}\big\rangle=Z_{i}(I)\,G_{i}(U_{i})=Z_{i}(I)\,G_{i}\big(\{S_{i}\geq\kappa\}\big)\,.

(B.64)

As
u\leq 0
, we have
V_{i}(I),V_{i+1}(I)\leq Z_{i}(I)
.

Now, we break into cases based on
Z_{i}(I)
. This step is permitted because
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}
is only w.r.t. the randomness defining the
i
-th sample, while as noted above,
Z_{i}(I)
does not depend on this randomness.
First, suppose
Z_{i}(I)\leq\exp(-ad)
. Here we have

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}V_{i+1}(I)-\log_{ad}V_{i}(I)\big|\Big]=0\,,

establishing (
B.62
).

Now, suppose
Z_{i}(I)\geq\exp(-ad)
from here on out, which is permitted as justified above. Note as
u(x)=0
for
x\geq\kappa
, observe that

\displaystyle 0\leq Y\leq X\leq 1\qquad\text{ where }\qquad X:=\big\langle\exp u(S_{i})\big\rangle\,,\,Y:=G_{i}\big(\{S_{i}\geq\kappa\}\big)\,.

(B.65)

The idea is that
X
and
Y
should be close if
u(\cdot)
is a mollifier that accurately represents the indicator; formalizing this intuition is how we make quantitative bounds.
Before breaking into further cases on
Z_{i}(I)
, we first present two useful Lemmas.

Lemma B.15
.

Let

\displaystyle c:=\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{4K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}K_{1}}\Big)^{K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}>0\,,

(B.66)

where
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}>0
is from Theorem
B.9
and depends on
a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
, and where
K_{1}>0
is large enough in terms of
a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
. Then if
Z_{i}(I)\geq\exp(-ad)
, for
d
large enough in terms of
\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
, we have

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}Y\big|\,\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq c\big\}\Big]\leq\varepsilon\,,

where
Y
is defined as per
(
B.65
)
.

Proof of Lemma
B.15
.

Since
Z_{i}(I)\geq\exp(-ad)
and as
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\frac{1}{K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}}
in the statement of Lemma
B.14
, we may apply Theorem
B.9
with
U(\theta)=\sum_{i<i^{\prime}\leq n}u(S_{i^{\prime}})
and
C=C_{i}
. Thus
G_{C}
defined in Theorem
B.9
is precisely
G_{i}
. Note
U\leq 0
and is concave as
u\leq 0
is concave and the
S_{i}
are affine. We obtain

\displaystyle\exp(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}})\leq t\leq\frac{1}{4}\exp\Big(-K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\max\{1,\kappa\}^{2}\Big)\implies\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(Y\leq t)\leq 16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}t^{1/K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\,.

(B.67)

for
K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}=K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)
,
K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}=K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)
, and
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}=K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)
.

Next, note
Y\leq 1
by (
B.65
), so

\big|\log_{ad}Y\big|=\log V\text{ where }V:=\min\big\{\exp(ad),1/Y\big\}\geq 1\,.

Thus we may apply Lemma
B.10
with
V
defined as above, and
C_{0}=K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}
,
C_{1}=16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}
,
C_{2}=K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}
, and
D_{0}=4\exp\big(K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\max\{1,\kappa\}^{2}\big)
.
We thus obtain that for
\gamma=\gamma(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)>0
,

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[V^{\gamma(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)}\Big]\leq K(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,.

As
V\geq 1
, we have
\log V<\tilde{K}(\gamma)V^{\gamma}
for all
\gamma>0
, where
\tilde{K}(\gamma)
is an explicit univariate function. Applying this for
\gamma=\gamma(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)/2
and combining with the above display gives, by taking
K_{1}
larger if needed,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big(\log V\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{K}(\gamma(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)/2)^{2}\cdot\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[V^{\gamma(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{K}(\gamma(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)/2)^{2}\cdot K(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,.

Thus, we may take
K_{1}
large enough in terms of
a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
so that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big(\log V\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq K_{1}\,,

(B.68)

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{4K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}K_{1}}\Big)^{K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\exp\big(-K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\max\{1,\kappa\}^{2}\big)\,.

(B.69)

Since
\varepsilon\leq 1
, for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
, our choice of
c>0
implies that the conditions of (
B.67
) hold with
t=c
.
Now (
B.67
) gives
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}(Y\leq c)\leq 4K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}c^{1/K^{\prime 2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}
. Cauchy-Schwarz and our choice of
c
now implies

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}Y\big|\,\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq c\big\}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}Y\big|^{2}\Big]^{1/2}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\mathbf{1}\big\{Y\leq c\big\}\Big]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big(\log V\big)^{2}\Big]^{1/2}\,\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\big(Y\leq c\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq K_{1}^{1/2}\cdot\big(4K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}c^{1/K^{\prime 2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\,,

by our choice of
c>0
. This proves Lemma
B.15
.
∎

Lemma B.16
.

Define
X,Y
as per
(
B.65
)
. Then if
Z_{i}(I)\geq\exp(-ad)
, for
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\min\{1,r_{\textup{\ref{lem:upperboundintegral}}}^{2}\}
where
r_{\textup{\ref{lem:upperboundintegral}}}
depends on
a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|X-Y\big|\Big]\leq\exp(-d)+K\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\,,

(B.70)

where
K>0
is a universal constant.

Proof of Lemma
B.16
.

Since
\exp u(\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime})\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}
, we have the following pointwise:

0\leq X-Y\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}+\Big\langle\mathbf{1}\big\{\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq S_{i}\leq\kappa\big\}\Big\rangle\,,

where we recall that
\langle\cdot\rangle
denotes expectation w.r.t.
G_{i}
, which does not depend on the randomness defining the
i
-th sample. Since the event
\big\{\|\bar{\theta}\|>\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}d}\big\}
is independent of the randomness defining the
i
-th sample, we obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|X-Y\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}+\Big\langle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\mathbf{1}\big\{\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq S_{i}\leq\kappa\big\}\Big]\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{\prime}+\Big\langle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\Big(\{\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq S_{i}\leq\kappa\}\,\mathbf{1}\{\big\|\bar{\theta}\|>\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}d}\big\}\Big)\Big\rangle+\Big\langle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\Big(\{\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq S_{i}\leq\kappa\}\,\mathbf{1}\big\{\|\bar{\theta}\|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}d}\big\}\Big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}+\Big\langle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\Big(\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq S_{i}\leq\kappa\Big)\,\mathbf{1}\big\{\|\bar{\theta}\|>\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}d}\big\}\Big\rangle+\Big\langle\mathbf{1}\big\{\|\bar{\theta}\|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}d}\big\}\Big\rangle\,.

(B.71)

Recall
Z_{i}(I)\geq\exp(-ad)
. Thus for
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq r_{\textup{\ref{lem:upperboundintegral}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1})^{2}
, noting the conditions apply for the Gibbs measure
\langle\cdot\rangle
as
u\leq 0
, we obtain from Lemma
B.11
that

\Big\langle\mathbf{1}\big\{\|\bar{\theta}\|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}d}\big\}\Big\rangle\leq\exp(-d)\,.

Now consider any
\kappa_{1}<\kappa_{2}
. Letting
\bar{\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}},\bar{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}}
denote probability and expectation in
g_{i}
, observe that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\big(\kappa_{1}<S_{i}<\kappa_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(s)\,\bar{\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}}\Big(\frac{\langle g_{i},\bar{\theta}\rangle}{\sqrt{d}}\in\Big[\kappa_{1}-\frac{s\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{d}},\kappa_{2}-\frac{s\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big]\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\,,

where
\phi(\cdot)
denotes the density of
s_{i}
. For a given
s\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}:=\kappa_{1}-\frac{s\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{d}}
,
\kappa_{2}^{\prime}:=\kappa_{2}-\frac{s\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{d}}
and
Z=\frac{\langle g_{i},\bar{\theta}\rangle}{\sqrt{d}}
. Note as
Z\sim N\Big(0,\frac{\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}}{d}\Big)
is Gaussian,

\displaystyle\bar{\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}}\Big(\frac{\langle g_{i},\bar{\theta}\rangle}{\sqrt{d}}\in\Big[\kappa_{1}-\frac{s\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{d}},\kappa_{2}-\frac{s\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{d}}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[Z^{2}]}}\int_{\kappa^{\prime}_{1}}^{\kappa^{\prime}_{2}}\exp\Big(-\frac{t^{2}}{2\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[Z^{2}]}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[Z^{2}]}}(\kappa^{\prime}_{2}-\kappa^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{K\sqrt{d}}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}(\kappa_{2}-\kappa_{1})\,,

for
K>0
a universal constant. Thus

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\big(\kappa_{1}<S_{i}<\kappa_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(s)\,\frac{K\sqrt{d}}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}(\kappa_{2}-\kappa_{1})\,\mathrm{d}s=\frac{K\sqrt{d}}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}(\kappa_{2}-\kappa_{1})\,,

yielding that for any
\theta
,

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\big(\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq S_{i}\leq\kappa\big)\,\mathbf{1}\big\{\|\bar{\theta}\|>\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}d}\big\}\leq\frac{K\varepsilon^{\prime}\sqrt{d}}{\|\bar{\theta}\|}\,\mathbf{1}\{\|\bar{\theta}\|>\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}d}\}\leq K\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\,.

Combining these steps with (
B.71
) yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|X-Y\big|\Big]\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}+\exp(-d)+K\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\,,

and hence (
B.70
).
∎

We now return to the proof of Lemma
B.14
. We break into cases on the value of
Z_{i}(I)
, recalling that it suffices to prove (
B.62
) when
Z_{i}(I)\geq\exp(-ad)
.

If
\exp(-ad)\leq Z_{i}(I)\leq 1
.

Applying Lemma
F.3
and Lemma
F.4
on truncated logarithms in the first and second inequality below respectively, as
X\geq Y
and combining (
B.63
), (
B.64
) and (
B.65
), we obtain for any
c>0
that

\displaystyle\big|\log_{ad}V_{i+1}-\log_{ad}V_{i}\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)Y\big)-\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)X\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\log_{ad}Y-\log_{ad}X\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\log_{ad}X-\log_{ad}Y\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\log_{ad}Y\big|\,\mathbf{1}\{Y\leq c\}+\frac{|X-Y|}{c}\,.

Taking
c=c(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)>0
as per (
B.66
), Lemmas
B.15
and
B.16
give

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}V_{i+1}-\log_{ad}V_{i}\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}Y\big|\,\mathbf{1}\{Y\leq c\}\Big]+\frac{1}{c}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|X-Y\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+\frac{1}{c}\big(\exp(-d)+K\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\big)\,.

Since
c=c(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
in (
B.66
) does not depend on
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, we have for
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and
d\geq d(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}V_{i+1}-\log_{ad}V_{i}\big|\Big]\leq K\varepsilon\,,

establishing (
B.62
) in this case.

If
Z_{i}(I)>1
.

Let
c=c(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)>0
be from (
B.66
). Note for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
that
\exp(-ad)\leq c
. Thus we may define the following events:

\displaystyle E_{1}:=\Big\{Y\leq\exp(-ad)\Big\}\,,\,E_{2}:=\Big\{\exp(-ad)<Y\leq c\Big\}\,,\,E_{3}:=\Big\{Y\geq c\Big\}\,.

Recall
1\geq X=\langle\exp u(S_{i})\rangle\geq Y
from (
B.65
) and that here
Z_{i}(I)>1
. Thus as
Y\geq c>0
on
E_{3}
,

\displaystyle Z_{i}(I)X\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}\,,\,Z_{i}(I)Y\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}\geq c\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}\geq\exp(-ad)\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}\,,

and therefore

\displaystyle\Big|\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)X\big)-\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)Y\big)\Big|\,\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}

\displaystyle=\Big|\log\big(Z_{i}(I)X\big)-\log\big(Z_{i}(I)Y\big)\Big|\,\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}

\displaystyle=\Big|\log\frac{X}{Y}\Big|\,\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{X}{Y}-1\Big)\,\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c}|X-Y|\,\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}\,.

By (
B.63
), (
B.64
), we thus may write

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}V_{i+1}-\log_{ad}V_{i}\big|\Big]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)X\big)-\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)Y\big)\big|\Big]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{E_{1}\}\big|\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)X\big)-\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)Y\big)\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{E_{2}\}\big|\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)X\big)-\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)Y\big)\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}\cdot\frac{1}{c}|X-Y|\Big]

\displaystyle:=\textsc{(I)}+\textsc{(II)}+\textsc{(III)}\,.

(B.72)

Now we upper bound each of
\textsc{(I)},\textsc{(II)},\textsc{(III)}
:

•

Upper bounding
(I)
:
Since
Z_{i}(I)\geq\exp(-ad)
and
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\frac{1}{K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}}
, we may apply Theorem
B.9
identically as in the proof of Lemma
B.15
, which establishes that the implication (
B.67
) holds.
First, suppose
\exp(-ad)\geq\exp\big(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\big)
. Taking
t=\exp(-ad)
in (
B.67
) gives

\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\big(Y<\exp(-ad)\big)\leq 16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{ad}{K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\Big)\,.

Else, say
\exp(-ad)<\exp\big(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\big)
. Taking
t=\exp\big(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\big)
in (
B.67
) gives

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\big(Y<\exp(-ad)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\Big(Y<\exp\big(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\big)\Big)\leq 16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}}\Big)\,.

Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}(E_{1})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}\big(Y<\exp(-ad)\big)\leq 16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\max\{1/a,K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\}}\Big)\,.

Now as
u\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle Z_{i}(I)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta\leq\Big(\frac{2\pi}{\beta_{0}}\Big)^{d/2}\,.

Thus as
X,Y\leq 1
by (
B.65
) and by definition of the truncated logarithm, we have

\Big|\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)X\big)-\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)Y\big)\Big|\leq K(a,\beta_{0})d\,.

Thus for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{E_{1}\}\Big|\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)X\big)-\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)Y\big)\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq K(a,\beta_{0})d\cdot\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{i}(E_{1})

\displaystyle\quad\leq K(a,\beta_{0})d\cdot 16K_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{K^{2}_{\textup{\ref{lem:keyconcentrationgaussianconversion}}}\max\{1/a,K_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}\}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\varepsilon\,.

(B.73)

•

Upper bounding
(II)
:

On the event
E_{2}
, we have
c\geq Y>\exp(-ad)
. Thus by (
B.65
), on the event
E_{2}
, we have
1\geq X\geq Y\geq\exp(-ad)
as well. As
Z_{i}(I)\geq 1
in this case, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\{E_{2}\}\Big|\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)X\big)-\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)Y\big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}\{E_{2}\}\Big|\log\big(Z_{i}(I)X\big)-\log\big(Z_{i}(I)Y\big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}\{E_{2}\}|\log X-\log Y|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}\{E_{2}\}|\log Y|

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}\{E_{2}\}\big|\log_{ad}Y\big|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}\{Y\leq c\}\big|\log_{ad}Y\big|\,.

Therefore as
Z_{i}(I)\geq 1\geq\exp(-ad)
, Lemma
B.15
implies for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{E_{2}\}\,\big|\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)X\big)-\log_{ad}\big(Z_{i}(I)Y\big)\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{Y\leq c\}\,\big|\log_{ad}Y\big|\Big]\leq\varepsilon\,.

(B.74)

•

Upper bounding
(III)
:

By Lemma
B.16
, as
Z_{i}(I)\geq 1
in this case, for
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1})
we have

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}\cdot\frac{1}{c}|X-Y|\Big]\leq\exp(-d)+K\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\,.

Hence, for
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{E_{3}\}\cdot\frac{1}{c}|X-Y|\Big]\leq\varepsilon\,.

(B.75)

Combining (
B.73
), (
B.74
), (
B.75
) with (
B.72
), we obtain that

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{i}\Big[\big|\log_{ad}V_{i+1}-\log_{ad}V_{i}\big|\Big]\leq 3\varepsilon\,,

hence establishing (
B.62
) when
Z_{i}(I)\geq 1
. This completes the proof of Lemma
B.14
.
∎

Finally, we put all these steps together to prove Theorem
B.1
.

Proof of Theorem
B.1
.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{4}
in the following.
Consider
\bar{\Phi}(x,q,\rho)
as defined in (
5.4
) with
\exp u(x)=\mathbf{1}\{x\geq\kappa\}
, and note this function is a continuous function of its three arguments.
By continuity supplied by the second part of Proposition
A.3
and compactness, we may let
a=a(\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)>0
be large enough so that the following holds. For all
(\alpha,\beta,x)\in[0,\alpha_{0}]\times[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]\times[-1,1]
, letting
q_{0}=q_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x),\rho_{0}=\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)
in the display below for brevity, we have

\bar{\Phi}(x,q_{0},\rho_{0})-\beta\rho+\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)\,,\,\bar{\Phi}(x,q_{0},\rho_{0})-\beta(\rho+x^{2})+\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)\geq-a+1\,.

Here we note
\alpha_{0}
depends only on
\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
as
C_{\textsc{sol}}
depends on
\beta_{0},\beta_{1}
.
Note
a
does not depend on
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime}
, since
\bar{\Phi}(x,q,\rho),q_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x),\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)
do not depend on
I
as per Remark
1
.
As
\textsc{RSG}_{I}
equals
\bar{\Phi}(x,q_{0},\rho_{0})-\beta\rho+\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)
or
\bar{\Phi}(x,q_{0},\rho_{0})-\beta(\rho+x^{2})+\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)
pointwise, we obtain for all
(\alpha,\beta,x)\in[0,\alpha_{0}]\times[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]\times[-1,1]
that

\displaystyle\textsc{RSG}_{I}(\alpha,\beta,x)\geq-a+1\,.

(B.76)

By Lemma
B.14
, there is
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}=\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)>0
such that for all
d\geq d(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and all
n\leq 2d
, the following holds if
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\frac{1}{K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}}
, where
K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}=K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)
. For any concave
u\leq 0
such that

u(x)=0\text{ for all }x\geq\kappa\text{ and }\exp u\big(\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}\big)\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}\,,

then

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\beta}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{u}(I)\big]\Big|\leq\varepsilon\,.

(B.77)

Likewise by Theorem
A.1
, there exists
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}=\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}(\varepsilon,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa^{+})
such that for any concave
u\leq 0
such that

1.

u^{\prime}\geq 0
with
u^{\prime}\neq 0
on a set of positive Lebesgue measure,

2.

u(x)=0
for
x\geq\kappa
,

3.

\exp u(\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2})\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}
,

4.

u
is four times differentiable and
|u^{(l)}|\leq D
for
l=1,2,3,4
,

there is a
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}_{0}}=\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}_{0}}(\varepsilon,u,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)>0
such that if
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}_{0}}
, the following holds.
For
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log Z_{u}(I)\big]-\textsc{RSG}_{I}(n/d,\beta,x)\Big|\leq\varepsilon\,.

(B.78)

Let

\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon^{\prime}(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda):=\min\{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}\}\,.

Given
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, it is straightforward to construct a mollifier
\hat{u}\leq 0
that is concave, satisfying
\hat{u}^{\prime}\geq 0
and
\hat{u}^{\prime}\neq 0
on a set of positive Lebesgue measure, satisfying
|\hat{u}^{(l)}|\leq D(\varepsilon^{\prime},\kappa)
for
l=1,2,3,4
, and satisfying

\hat{u}(x)=0\text{ for all }x\geq\kappa\,,\,\exp\hat{u}(\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime})\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\,.

Hence as
\hat{u}^{\prime}\geq 0
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}
, it follows that
\exp\hat{u}(\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1})\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}
and
\exp\hat{u}(\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2})\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}
.

Since
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon^{\prime}(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
, we may let

\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}=\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}\big(\varepsilon,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda\big):=\min\big\{1/K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}},\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}_{0}}\big\}\,,

where now
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}_{0}}=\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}_{0}}(\hat{u},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)
. This is well-defined as
\hat{u}
is constructed using
\kappa,\varepsilon^{\prime}
, and as
\varepsilon^{\prime}
in turn depends on
\varepsilon,a=a(\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda),\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa
, and
\lambda
.

Consider any
0\leq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}<\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}
and let
I=[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]
.
Note as
\exp\hat{u}(\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1})\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{1}
and
\exp\hat{u}(\kappa-\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2})\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{2}
, for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
, we have both (
B.77
) and (
B.78
) for
Z_{\hat{u}}(I)
.

With these steps in hand, the finish is as follows. We need one last argument about controlling the error of the truncated logarithm:

Lemma B.17
.

For
d
large enough in terms of
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]\Big|\leq\varepsilon\,.

(B.79)

Proof of Lemma
B.17
.

Let

\Omega:=\big\{Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\leq\exp(-ad)\big\}\,.

Thus by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{\Omega\}\Big(a+\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\Big)\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega)\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\Big(a+\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq K\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega)\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[a^{2}+\frac{1}{d^{2}}\log^{2}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\Big]\,.

First we upper bound
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[a^{2}+\frac{1}{d^{2}}\log^{2}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\Big]
. Notice as
\hat{u}\leq 0
,

Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\leq\int\exp\Big(H_{\hat{u}}(\theta)-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\leq\int\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta\leq\Big(\frac{2\pi}{\beta_{0}}\Big)^{d/2}\,,

thus

\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[a^{2}+\frac{1}{d^{2}}\log^{2}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\Big]\leq K(a,\beta_{0})\,.

Now we upper bound
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega)
. As
\log_{A}x\geq\log x
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and as
\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{4}
, (
B.78
) and (
B.76
) yields that for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
,

\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]\geq\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]\geq\textsc{RSG}_{I}(n/d,\beta,x)-\varepsilon\geq-a+1-\varepsilon\geq-a+3/4\,.

Therefore,

\Omega\subseteq\Big\{\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log_{ad}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]\Big|\geq\frac{3}{4}\Big\}\,.

Now as
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq 1/K^{\prime}_{\textup{\ref{thm:gaussiansetup}}}
, we may apply Lemma
B.12
with
u=\hat{u}
. Thus for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log_{ad}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]\Big|\geq\frac{3}{4}\Big)\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{K(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}\Big)\,.

Combining the above bounds, it follows for such
d
that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]\Big|\leq K(a,\beta_{0})\cdot 2\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{K(a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}\Big)\leq\varepsilon\,.

This proves Lemma
B.17
.
∎

Now summing together (
B.77
), (
B.78
), and (
B.79
), we obtain for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\beta}(I)\big]-\textsc{RSG}_{I}(n/d,\beta,x)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\beta}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log Z_{\hat{u}}(I)\big]-\textsc{RSG}_{I}(n/d,\beta,x)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 3\varepsilon\,.

By Lemma
B.12
with
\exp u(x)=\mathbf{1}\{x\geq\kappa\}
and
t=\varepsilon
, we obtain for such
d
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log_{ad}Z_{\beta}(I)-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\log_{ad}Z_{\beta}(I)\big]\Big|\geq\varepsilon\Big)\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{K(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}\Big)\,,

where we obtain dependence on
\varepsilon
in
K
as we took
t=\varepsilon
in Lemma
B.12
. Combining the above two displays, we obtain for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log_{ad}Z_{\beta}(I)-\textsc{RSG}_{I}(n/d,\beta,x)\Big|\geq 4\varepsilon\Big)\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{d}{K(\varepsilon,a,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}\Big)\,.

(B.80)

However, by (
B.76
), we have
\textsc{RSG}_{I}(n/d,\beta,x)\geq-a+1\geq-a+4\varepsilon
. Thus as events,

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log_{ad}Z_{\beta}(I)-\textsc{RSG}_{I}(n/d,\beta,x)\Big|\leq 4\varepsilon\Big\}\equiv\Big\{\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)-\textsc{RSG}_{I}(n/d,\beta,x)\Big|\leq 4\varepsilon\Big\}\,,

since under this event
\log_{ad}\equiv\log
as functions. Combining the above display with (
B.80
) and applying this result for
\varepsilon/4
and recalling
a=a(\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
proves Theorem
B.1
.
∎

B.2
Proof of Theorem
B.2
for posterior

Our strategy will be to apply Theorem
B.3
on concentration of Lipschitz functions of strongly log-concave measures. Specifically, we consider
\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)
as a function of the disorder
(s_{i},\bar{g}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
, and bound its resulting Lipschitz constant; recall as in Appendix
A
that the first four derivatives of
u
are upper bounded by some quantity
D
, which only depends on
\lambda
. Here in this proof we write dependence on
D
, and in fact we only need the bound on the first derivative of
u
.

Let us begin by considering the space
\mathcal{S}=\mathbb{R}^{nd}
where the first
n
coordinates correspond to the
s_{j}
and the next
(n-1)d
coordinates correspond to
g_{i,j}
.
We endow
\mathcal{S}
with the product measure
\gamma
where the measure on each coordinate is given by law of each corresponding part of the disorder (that is,
s_{j}
or
g_{i,j}
).
Define

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} B^{\star}&:=\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\bar{g}_{i}\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+\frac{1}{16d}\sum_{j=2}^{d}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{ij}\Big)^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\Big(n\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+\frac{1}{8dK_{\textsc{signal}}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}\Big)^{2}+Y^{2}+Z^{2}+\sqrt{2\pi/\beta_{0}}d\,,\end{aligned}

(B.81)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}:=\{B^{\star}\leq 3K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posteriorgaussian_Bstar_control}}}(\beta_{0},\lambda)\,d\}\,,

(B.82)

where
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posteriorgaussian_Bstar_control}}}(\beta_{0},\lambda)
is from Lemma
B.21
. Note
B^{\star}
is a convex function of the disorder and therefore
\mathcal{C}
is a convex subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Also recall
K_{\textsc{signal}}
depends solely on
\lambda
.

Next, let
\gamma^{\prime}
be a probability measure defined on
\mathcal{C}
with density proportional to that of
\gamma
. By Assumption
1
,
\gamma^{\prime}
is
\min\{1,\beta_{\textsc{signal}}\}
strongly log-concave.

Now, let us differentiate
\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)
w.r.t. the disorder, letting
\langle\cdot\rangle
below and in what follows here in Appendix
B.2
denote the Gibbs average w.r.t
\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u(S_{i})-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\big)
:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial s_{i}}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\Bigg(\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u(S_{i})-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\cdot u^{\prime}(S_{i})\cdot\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\,\mathrm{d}\theta\Bigg)/Z_{\beta}(I)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\big\langle u^{\prime}(S_{i})\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\big\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial g_{ij}}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\Bigg(\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u(S_{i})-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\cdot u^{\prime}(S_{i})\cdot\theta_{j}/\sqrt{d}\,\mathrm{d}\theta\Bigg)/Z_{\beta}(I)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\big\langle u^{\prime}(S_{i})\theta_{j}/\sqrt{d}\big\rangle\,.

Therefore, letting
\nabla
denote the gradient of
\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)
w.r.t. the disorder, and defining
R_{1,1}
as per (
A.16
), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\langle u^{\prime}(S_{i})\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\big\rangle^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\big\langle u^{\prime}(S_{i})\theta_{j}/\sqrt{d}\big\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{d^{2}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\langle u^{\prime}(S_{i})^{2}\theta_{1}^{2}/d\big\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\big\langle u^{\prime}(S_{i})^{2}\theta_{j}^{2}/d\big\rangle^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{D^{2}n}{d^{2}}\Big(4+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\big\langle\theta_{j}^{2}/d\big\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2D^{2}}{d}\big(4+R_{1,1}\big)\,.

(B.83)

Here we used that
n\leq\alpha_{0}d\leq 2d
, and that
\big\langle\theta_{1}^{2}/d\big\rangle\leq 4
due to the indicator
\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}
in the Gibbs average
\langle\cdot\rangle
.

Upper bounding
R_{1,1}
.

We now upper bound
R_{1,1}
in terms of
B^{\star}
defined in (
B.81
). To this end we introduce the following Lemmas.

Lemma B.18

(Lemma 3.1.5,
[
Tal10
]
)
.

Consider a measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
defined as follows: for some
C\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have for all
B\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mu(B)=\frac{1}{Z}\int\mathbf{1}\{B\cap C\}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Big[\exp\Big(U(\theta)-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}+\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}a_{j}\theta_{j}+a_{0}\Big)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,,

(B.84)

where
U\leq 0
,
Z
here denotes the appropriate normalizing constant, and
a_{0}
potentially depends on the annealed disorder
W
. Then

\displaystyle\int\exp\Big(\frac{\beta}{2}\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\theta)\leq\frac{1}{Z}\Big(\frac{2\pi}{\beta}\Big)^{d/2}\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2\beta}\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}a_{j}^{2}\Big)\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\big[\exp a_{0}\big]\,.

(B.85)

Technically the above Lemma is stated in
[
Tal10
]
for
C=\mathbb{R}^{d}
and with
a_{0}=0
, but the proof is identical. Note we will need this Lemma with
a_{0}
depending on the annealed disorder
W
from the interpolation argument in Appendix
D.3
.

We next find a way to upper bound the moments w.r.t. a measure in the form of Lemma
B.18
, provided that the normalizing constant is not too small.

Lemma B.19
.

Consider a measure
\mu
in the form
(
B.84
)
given in Lemma
B.18
, let
a_{j}
be defined as in Lemma
B.18
, and let
Z
be the corresponding normalizing constant. Suppose
Z\geq\exp\big(-K^{\prime}B)
for some
K^{\prime}
depending on
D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
and some
B\geq d
. Then we have:

1.

We have

\Big\langle\exp\Big(\frac{\beta}{2}\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}\Big)\Big\rangle\leq\Big\langle\exp\Big(\frac{\beta}{2}\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\Big\rangle\leq\exp\Big(K_{\textup{\ref{lem:logconcaveboundmomentslemma}}}B+\frac{1}{2\beta_{0}}\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}a_{j}^{2}+\log\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\big[\exp a_{0}\big]\Big)\,,

where
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:logconcaveboundmomentslemma}}}\geq 1
depends on
K^{\prime},D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
.

2.

For
k\leq 4d
, we have

\big\langle\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2k}\big\rangle\leq\big\langle\|\theta\|^{2k}\big\rangle\leq\Big(KB+\frac{1}{2\beta_{0}}\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}a_{j}^{2}+\log\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\big[\exp a_{0}\big]\Big)^{k}\,,

where
K
depends on
K^{\prime},D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
.

Proof of Lemma
B.19
.

For the first part of this Lemma, we directly combine Lemma
B.18
with the given hypothesis
Z^{\prime}\geq\exp\big(-K^{\prime}B)
. For the second part of this Lemma, apply Lemma
A.7
with
X=\frac{\beta}{2}\|\theta\|^{2}
and expectation w.r.t.
\langle\cdot\rangle
. By the first part of this Lemma, we have

\log\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp X\big]\leq K_{\textup{\ref{lem:logconcaveboundmomentslemma}}}B+\frac{1}{2\beta_{0}}\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}a_{j}^{2}+\log\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\big[\exp a_{0}\big]\,,

where expectation here is w.r.t.
\langle\cdot\rangle
. The proof is complete upon noting
k\leq 4d\leq 4B
and multiplying by
(2/\beta)^{k}
on both sides.
∎

Now, note that
u(x)\geq-D(1+|x|)
where
u(x)
is from (
3.17
), (
3.18
) in the GMM and logistic cases respectively. Thus Lemma
D.9
implies that for
d\geq d(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
, we have

\displaystyle Z_{\beta}(I)\geq\exp\big(-K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)B^{\star}\big)\,,

(B.86)

where
B^{\star}
is given in (
B.81
). (The proof of Lemma
D.9
is deferred to Appendix
D.3
for simplicity of notation, since the Lemma is stated for the interpolating Gibbs measures from Appendix
A
, of which the measure considered here is a special case with
t=v=1
.)

We now show in both the GMM and logistic cases that

\displaystyle R_{1,1}\leq\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\,B^{\star}}{d}\,.

(B.87)

•

In the logistic case where
u(x)
is from (
3.18
), we have
\varphi\leq 1
and therefore
u\leq 0
. Hence by (
B.86
), we may apply Lemma
B.19
with the
a_{j}=0
, which gives (
B.87
).

•

In the GMM case where
u(x)
is from (
3.17
), as
u(x)=\sqrt{\lambda}x
, we can write

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}u(S_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{d}}\Big(s_{i}\theta_{1}+\sum_{j=2}^{d}g_{ij}\theta_{j}\Big)=\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{d}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}\Big)\theta_{1}+\sum_{j=2}^{d}\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{d}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{ij}\Big)\theta_{j}\,.

(B.88)

Thus applying Lemma
B.19
with (
B.86
), we obtain that

\displaystyle R_{1,1}\leq\frac{1}{d}\Bigg(K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)B^{\star}+\frac{\lambda}{2\beta_{0}d}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}\Big)^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2\beta_{0}d}\sum_{j=2}^{d}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{ij}\Big)^{2}\Bigg)\leq\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\,B^{\star}}{d}\,,

where the last step follows from the definition of
B^{\star}
in (
B.81
). Again this proves (
B.87
).

Completing the argument.

Now we apply Theorem
B.3
with
f=\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)
and the strongly log-concave measure
\gamma^{\prime}
. By (
B.2
) and (
B.87
),
f
is Lipschitz with constant
K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)/\sqrt{d}
on
\mathcal{C}
.
Since
\gamma^{\prime}
is supported on
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{C}
is convex, Theorem
B.3
yields for
d\geq d(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]\Big|\geq\varepsilon/2\Big)\leq\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon,D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\big)\,,

(B.89)

where
\varepsilon>0
is as in the statement of Theorem
B.2
.

We next state the following Lemma that essentially lets us reduce the desired tail bound in Theorem
B.2
to (
B.89
). Its proof will be presented later.

Lemma B.20
.

For
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\gamma}\big(\mathcal{C}^{c}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-d)\,,

(B.90)

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon/4\,.

(B.91)

We will now prove Theorem
B.2
given Lemma
B.20
.
Let

\displaystyle E_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]\Big|\geq\varepsilon/4\Big\}\,.

(B.92)

By Lemma
B.20
, for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\gamma}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]\Big|\geq\varepsilon/2\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\gamma}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]+\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]\Big|\geq\varepsilon/2\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\gamma}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]\Big|\geq\varepsilon/4\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{E^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{E^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}\Big]+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{E^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}^{c}\}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\gamma(\mathcal{C})\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{E^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\}\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}\Big]+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}^{c}\}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon,D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\big)\,.

(B.93)

Here in the last inequality, we applied (
B.89
).

Finally, recall by Theorem
A.2
that for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]-\textsc{RSG}_{I}(n/d,\beta,x)\Big|\leq\varepsilon/2\,.

Therefore combining with (
B.93
) gives

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\gamma}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)-\textsc{RSG}_{I}(n/d,\beta,x)\big]\Big|\geq\varepsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\gamma}\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{d}\log Z_{\beta}(I)-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]\Big|\geq\varepsilon/2\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon,D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\big)\,.

This proves Theorem
B.2
given Lemma
B.20
.

Proof of Lemma
B.20
.

Finally, we prove Lemma
B.20
. We first introduce the following Lemma which gives us a tail bound on
B^{\star}
. This Lemma follows as a corollary of the proof of Lemma
D.8
, which is proven later in Appendix
D.3
.

Lemma B.21
.

For all
k\leq d
we have for
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posteriorgaussian_Bstar_control}}}
depending on
\beta_{0},\lambda
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[B^{\star k}\big]\leq\big(K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posteriorgaussian_Bstar_control}}}d\big)^{k}\,.

Given Lemma
B.21
, by the definition of
\mathcal{C}
, we directly obtain (
B.90
).
To prove (
B.91
), we introduce an additional Lemma:

Lemma B.22
.

For
d\geq d(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big|\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)B^{\star}\,.

Proof of Lemma
B.22
.

The lower bound on
\log Z_{\beta}(I)
follows from
B.86
. For the upper bound, note

\displaystyle Z_{\beta}(I)\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\Big(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}+\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}a_{j}\theta_{j}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\leq(2\pi/\beta_{0})^{d/2}\,,

for some
(a_{j})_{j=1}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}
. (Note the integral is invariant to coordinate translation.) Specifically, in the logistic case we use that
u\leq 0
, and in the GMM case we use (
B.88
). This proves Lemma
B.22
.
∎

Now to prove (
B.91
), we write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}\big]+\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}^{c}\}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\gamma(\mathcal{C})\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]+\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}^{c}\}\big]\,,

where we note
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}\big]=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]
as the support of
\gamma^{\prime}
equals
\mathcal{C}
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]-\Big(\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}\}\big]+\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}^{c}\}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1-\gamma(\mathcal{C})}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}^{c}\}\big]\,.

(B.94)

By Lemma
B.22
, and since
\gamma^{\prime}
is supported on
\mathcal{C}
on which
B^{\star}\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)d
, we have
