\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big]\Big|\leq\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\big[\big|\log Z_{\beta}(I)\big|\big]\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\,.

(B.95)

Next by Lemma
B.21
, note

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\gamma}\big(B^{\star}\geq t\big)\leq\big(K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posteriorgaussian_Bstar_control}}}(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)/t\big)^{d}\text{ for all }t\geq 0\,.

(B.96)

Thus by Lemma
B.22
and integrating the tail bound (
B.96
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[\log Z_{\beta}(I)\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}^{c}\}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{\gamma}\big[B^{\star}\mathbf{1}\{\mathcal{C}^{c}\}\big]

\displaystyle\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\int_{3K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posteriorgaussian_Bstar_control}}}(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)d}^{\infty}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}_{\gamma}\big(B^{\star}\geq t\big)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\exp(-d)\,.

(B.97)

Combining (
B.90
), (
B.94
), (
B.95
), (
B.97
) now proves (
B.91
) for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)
, finishing the proof of Lemma
B.20
.

Appendix C
Conversion from Gaussian to spherical measure: proof of Theorems
3.1
,
3.5

Here we complete the proof of our main results Theorems
3.1
,
3.5
.
Theorem
3.1
on interpolators and Theorem
3.5
on the posterior follow as a corollary of the following Theorems
C.1
,
C.2
respectively.

Theorem
C.1
for interpolators:

This result states that for a narrow interval
I
near
x\in[-1,1]
(whose width is independent of
d
), the normalized logarithm of the spherical measure of the interpolators constrained to this interval, approximately equals
\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)
for
d
large enough:

Theorem C.1

(For interpolators)
.

Let
\mu_{d}(\cdot)
denote the uniform measure on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})
.
Consider any
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
and
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
, and let
n=\lfloor\alpha d\rfloor
.
Define
\delta=\delta(\alpha,\lambda,\kappa_{+})
as follows, where the inequality uses
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
:

\displaystyle\delta=\delta(\alpha,\lambda,\kappa_{+}):=\frac{1/2+\alpha K_{\textup{\ref{lem:controlexpectationofAW}}}C_{\textsc{sol}}^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}}{9}\leq\frac{1}{10}\,.

(C.1)

Recalling the definition of
C
in
(
B.1
)
and letting
\Phi(x,q)
be as in
(
3.1
)
with
\exp u(x)=\mathbf{1}\{x\geq\kappa\}
, we have for all
x\in[-1+\delta,1-\delta]
and
\varepsilon>0
:

1.

Upper bound:
Let
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}=\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
be from Theorem
B.1
and consider any
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\min\{\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}\}
. Define the interval

\displaystyle I:=\begin{cases}\big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\big]&:x\geq 0\,,\,\kappa<0\,,\\
\big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}\big]&:x<0\,,\,\kappa<0\,,\\
\big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}\big]&:x\geq 0\,,\,\kappa\geq 0\,,\\
\big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\big]&:x<0\,,\,\kappa\geq 0\,.\end{cases}

(C.2)

If
|x|>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, for
d
large enough in terms of
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
and with probability at least
1-\exp\bigl(-d/K\bigr)
where
K
depends on
\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Bigr)\leq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)+\varepsilon\,.

(C.3)

(Note this bound is non-vacuous; we have
I\neq\emptyset
.)

2.

Lower bound: Suppose
\varepsilon<\frac{1}{K^{\prime}}
where
K^{\prime}
is large enough in terms of
\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
. Then there exists
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}>0
depending on
\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
such that for all
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
, the following holds.
Define the interval

\displaystyle I:=\begin{cases}\big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1+\varepsilon},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\big]&:x\geq 0\,,\,\kappa<0\,,\\
\big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1+\varepsilon}\big]&:x<0\,,\,\kappa<0\,,\\
\big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1-\varepsilon}\big]&:x\geq 0\,,\,\kappa\geq 0\,,\\
\big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1-\varepsilon},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\big]&:x<0\,,\,\kappa\geq 0\,.\end{cases}

(C.4)

If
|x|>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, for
d
large enough in terms of
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
and with probability at least
1-\exp\bigl(-d/K\bigr)
where
K
depends on
\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Bigr)\geq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)-\varepsilon\,.

(C.5)

Theorem
C.2
for posterior:

This result is similar to Theorem
C.1
, but now is for the normalized logarithm of the integral of the posterior constrained to the interval
I
, rather than the spherical measure of the interpolators:

Theorem C.2

(For posterior)
.

Consider any
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
and let
n=\lfloor\alpha d\rfloor
.
Consider
u(x)
from
(
3.17
)
,
(
3.18
)
in the GMM and logistic cases respectively and let
\Phi(x,q)
be as in
(
3.1
)
with this
u(x)
. Define
\delta=\delta(\alpha,\lambda)
as follows, where the inequality uses
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
:

\displaystyle\delta=\delta(\alpha,\lambda):=\frac{1}{18-\alpha(2D^{2}+D)}\leq\frac{1}{10}\,.

(C.6)

For any interval
I\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, let

\displaystyle Z(I):=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\mathbf{1}\big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\big\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u(S_{i})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,,

(C.7)

where
S_{i}=S_{i}(\theta)
is as in
(
A.1
)
. Then for all
x\in[-1+\delta,1-\delta]
and
\varepsilon>0
, we have:

1.

Upper bound:
Let
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}=\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)
be from Theorem
B.2
, consider any
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\min\{\varepsilon/K^{\prime},\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}\}
where
K^{\prime}
depends on
\lambda
. Define the interval
I
by

\displaystyle I:=\begin{cases}\big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}\big]&:x\geq 0\,,\\
\big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\big]&:x<0\,.\end{cases}

(C.8)

If
|x|>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, for
d
large enough in terms of
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
and with probability at least
1-\exp\bigl(-d/K\bigr)
where
K
depends on
\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log Z(I)\leq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)+\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)+\varepsilon\,.

(C.9)

(As in Theorem
C.1
, note this bound is non-vacuous; we have
I\neq\emptyset
.)

2.

Lower bound: Suppose
\varepsilon<\frac{1}{K^{\prime}}
where
K^{\prime}
is large enough in terms of
\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
. Then there exists
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}>0
depending on
\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
such that for all
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
, the following holds.
Define the interval

\displaystyle I:=\begin{cases}\big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1-\varepsilon}\big]&:x\geq 0\,,\\
\big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1-\varepsilon},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\big]&:x<0\,.\end{cases}

(C.10)

If
|x|>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, for
d
large enough in terms of
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
and with probability at least
1-\exp\bigl(-d/K\bigr)
where
K
depends on
\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log Z(I)\geq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)+\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)-\varepsilon\,.

(C.11)

We will prove Theorem
C.1
in the
\kappa<0
case in Appendix
C.2
, prove Theorem
C.1
in the
\kappa\geq 0
case in Appendix
C.3
, and prove Theorem
C.2
in Appendix
C.4
. All three proofs follow the same unified strategy that leverages our work in Appendix
B
, specifically Theorem
B.1
for the interpolators and Theorem
B.2
for the posterior. We present a full proof for
\kappa<0
in Appendix
C.2
and highlight the necessary modifications to complete the proof when
\kappa>0
or for the posterior in Appendices
C.3
,
C.4
.

Remark 2
.

We emphasize that the interval
I
from Theorems
C.1
,
C.2
does
not
equal
[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]
. This is because our analysis relies on several comparison inequalities where we scale
[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]
by a factor close to 1 independent of
d
; see e.g.
(
C.24
)
,
(
C.25
)
.

I
is then a larger interval containing the scaled intervals for the lower bound, or a smaller interval contained within the scaled intervals for the upper bound.
For the upper bound, the parameter
w
governing the range of these scalings equals
1\pm\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
(the sign depending on whether
\kappa\geq 0,\kappa<0
), and we can check that
I\neq\emptyset
. For the lower bound,
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
delicately depends on the parameter
w=1\pm\varepsilon
governing these scalings, but now
I\supseteq[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]
and is therefore nonempty.

Before we present the proofs of Theorems
C.1
,
C.2
, we combine them with an application of the Laplace Method over
x\in[-1,1]
to prove our main results: Theorem
3.1
on interpolators and Theorem
3.5
on the posterior respectively. Note the Laplace Method gives the ‘
\sup_{x}\inf_{q}
’ structure therein.
In preparation, we will present the following properties of the RS equations, which follow from the convex-concave structure established in Appendix
E
.
The proof of this Lemma is in Appendix
E.3
.

Lemma C.3
.

Let
\delta=\delta(\alpha,\lambda,\kappa_{+})
from
(
C.1
)
for the interpolators, or
\delta=\delta(\alpha,\lambda)
from
(
C.6
)
for the posterior. We have the following:

1.

For all
(\alpha,\beta,x)\in[0,\alpha_{0}]\times[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]\times[-1,1]
, we have
\frac{\partial\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)}{\partial\beta}<0
.

2.

For all
(\alpha,x)\in[0,\alpha_{0}]\times[-1+\delta,1-\delta]
, there is a unique
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
such that
\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)=1-x^{2}
, and moreover
\beta(\alpha,x)\in(1/4,9)
.

3.

For all
(\alpha,x)\in[0,\alpha_{0}]\times[-1+\delta,1-\delta]
, recalling the definition of
\bar{\Phi}
in
(
5.4
)
,

\displaystyle f(\beta):=\beta+\bar{\Phi}\big(x,q_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x),\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)\big)-\beta\big(\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)+x^{2}\big)

attains a unique minimum on
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
at
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)
. Thus for any interval
I
, if
0\not\in I
, then
\beta+\textsc{RSG}_{I}(\alpha,\beta,x)
attains a unique minimum on
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
at
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)
.

4.

For all
(\alpha,x)\in[0,\alpha_{0}]\times[-1+\delta,1-\delta]
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)=f\big(\beta(\alpha,x)\big)\,.

5.

For all
(\alpha,x)\in[0,\alpha_{0}]\times[-1+\delta,1-\delta]
,
\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)
is continuous in
(\alpha,x)
(note
\alpha
is implicitly an argument in
\Phi(x,q)
).

We now prove Theorems
3.1
,
3.5
using Theorems
C.1
,
C.2
and the above Lemma
C.3
.

C.1
Proof of Theorems
3.1
,
3.5

We provide the proof of Theorem
3.1
using Theorem
C.1
when
\kappa<0
; the proof of Theorem
3.1
in the
\kappa\geq 0
case and the proof of Theorem
3.5
using Theorem
C.2
are analogous.
Recall that
y_{i}X_{i}\equiv(s_{i},g_{i})^{T}
in law as random variables and that without loss of generality we can set
\theta_{\star}=\sqrt{d}e_{1}
.

First, we show (
C.3
), (
C.5
) with the interval
I
replaced by any interval
I\subseteq[-1+\delta,1-\delta]
. Recall as per (
A.7
) that
\beta_{0},\beta_{1}
are universal constants, therefore in this proof we will not explicitly write dependence on them.

Proof of (
C.3
):

Note for
d\geq d(\varepsilon)
,
\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in[-1/\sqrt{d},1/\sqrt{d}]\}\bigr)\leq\varepsilon
.
Now if
0\in I
, we subdivide
I
into the two closed intervals
I_{1}=I\cap[-1,-1/\sqrt{d}]
,
I_{2}=I\cap[1/\sqrt{d},1]
, and the remaining part
I-I_{1}-I_{2}
(if
0\not\in I
this step is not necessary, and we can just apply the same argument as in the following upper bound for
I_{1}
).

We now upper bound
\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I_{1}\}\cap C\bigr)
, the upper bound for
I_{2}
being analogous.
We divide the closed interval
I_{1}
into a set of consecutive closed intervals
\bar{I}_{k}
each of width
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}=\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon,\alpha_{0},\kappa,\lambda)
(recall
\beta_{0},\beta_{1}
are universal constants),
1\leq k\leq K(\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}})
, that overlap at exactly their endpoints.
Here
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}
comes from Theorem
B.1
.

For each interval
\bar{I}_{k}=[a,b]
, note
0<a<b\leq 1
,
b-a\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}
.
Letting
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}=\frac{b-a}{2-a}<b-a
and
x=b-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, we have
\bar{I}_{k}=[a,b]=\big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\big]
,
x>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
,
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq b-a\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}
.
Since
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\geq b-a=\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}=\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon,\alpha_{0},\kappa,\lambda)
, Theorem
C.1
yields that for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)
, with probability at least
1-\exp\bigl(-d/K(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)\bigr)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}_{k}\}\cap C\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)+\varepsilon\leq\sup_{x\in I}\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)+\varepsilon\,.

Here the second inequality uses that
\bar{I}_{k}\subseteq I_{1}
.
As we have
K(\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}})
such intervals
\bar{I}_{k}
, combining the above bound over all
1\leq k\leq K(\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}})
and applying a Union Bound (note we sum the bounds on
\mu_{d}\bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I_{1}\}\cap C\bigr)
, which behaves at the exponential scale) yields that for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)
, with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)\bigr)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I_{1}\}\cap C\Bigr)\leq\sup_{x\in I}\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)+2\varepsilon\,.

An analogous bound applies for
I_{2}
via the same proof, and for
I-I_{1}-I_{2}
we recall the bound
\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in[-1/\sqrt{d},1/\sqrt{d}]\}\bigr)\leq\varepsilon
. Combining these three upper bounds and applying a Union Bound yields for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)
, with probability at least
1-\exp\bigl(-d/K(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)\bigr)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Bigr)\leq\sup_{x\in I}\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)+4\varepsilon\,.

Upon taking
\varepsilon\leftarrow\varepsilon/4
, this proves (
C.3
) for this
I
.

Proof of (
C.5
):

In this proof, we write
I=[a,b]
for
-1\leq a<b\leq 1
.
Note it suffices to prove the result when
\varepsilon<\frac{1}{K^{\prime}(\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)}
.
First, continuity from Lemma
C.3
implies
\sup_{x\in I}\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)
is attained at some
x^{\star}\in I
.
Noting
\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)
only depends on
\alpha,\kappa,\lambda
, by continuity supplied by Lemma
C.3
and compactness, we let
\bar{\delta}=\bar{\delta}(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)
be small enough so that

\displaystyle\big|\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x_{1},q)-\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x_{2},q)\big|\leq\varepsilon\,,\qquad\forall\,x_{1},x_{2}\in[-1+\delta,1-\delta]\,,\,|x_{1}-x_{2}|\leq\bar{\delta}\,.

(C.12)

Since
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, taking
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough in terms of
\alpha,\kappa,\lambda,|I|
, we may suppose that
|I|\geq\bar{\delta}
.

We first prove the result when
I\subseteq[0,\infty)
; the proof when
I\subseteq(-\infty,0]
is analogous, and later we will provide the argument when neither case holds.

1.

Suppose
x^{\star}>0
is distance at least
\bar{\delta}/4
from
a,b
.
Let
\bar{\varepsilon}=\bar{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon,\bar{\delta},\alpha,\lambda)\leq\min\{\bar{\delta}/8,\varepsilon\}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}<\min\big\{\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}(\bar{\varepsilon},\alpha,\kappa,\lambda),\bar{\delta}/8\big\}
be small enough, where
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
comes from Theorem
C.1
. Let

\displaystyle\bar{I}:=\Big[\frac{x^{\star}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1+\bar{\varepsilon}},x^{\star}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\,.

(C.13)

As
|a|\leq 1
,
\bar{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}<\bar{\delta}/4
,
|x^{\star}|\leq 1
and
x^{\star}
is distance at least
\bar{\delta}/4
from
a,b
, we have
\bar{I}\subseteq I
.
Also, clearly
x^{\star}\geq\bar{\delta}/4>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
.
Applying Theorem
C.1
, recalling that
\bar{\delta}
and
\bar{\varepsilon}
depend only on
\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda
, we obtain for
d\geq d(\bar{\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)=d(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)
that with probability at least
1-\exp\bigl(-d/K(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)\bigr)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x^{\star},q)-\varepsilon\,,

(C.14)

where we use that
\bar{\varepsilon}\leq\varepsilon
in the last inequality.

2.

Suppose
x^{\star}
is within distance
\bar{\delta}/4
from
a,b
. Now, we simply apply the above argument with
x^{\star}
replaced by
\bar{x}^{\star}=a+\bar{\delta}/4
if
|a-x^{\star}|\leq\bar{\delta}/4
or
\bar{x}^{\star}=b-\bar{\delta}/4
if
|b-x^{\star}|\leq\bar{\delta}/4
.
Since
|I|\geq\bar{\delta}
, the resulting
\bar{x}^{\star}
is always distance at least
\bar{\delta}/4
from
a,b
, and hence
\bar{x}^{\star}>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
as well.
Define
\bar{\varepsilon}
,
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
identically to case 1) above and
\bar{I}
now in terms of
\bar{x}^{\star}
as per (
C.13
). Applying Theorem
C.1
analogously, by
|x^{\star}-\bar{x}^{\star}|\leq\bar{\delta}
and (
C.12
), we obtain for the same
d
and with the same probability as (
C.14
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(\bar{x}^{\star},q)-\bar{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x^{\star},q)-2\varepsilon\,.

(C.15)

Finally suppose
I
is not contained within
[0,\infty)
or
(-\infty,0]
.
We suppose
x^{\star}\geq 0
in what follows, the argument when
x^{\star}<0
is analogous.

1.

Suppose
\big|I\cap[0,\infty)\big|\geq\bar{\delta}/2
.
If
x^{\star}\geq\bar{\delta}/4
we simply apply the argument in either cases 1) or 2) above, defining
\bar{\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\bar{I}
the same way as there.
In particular, we still have
\bar{I}\subseteq I
as
\big|I\cap[0,\infty)\big|\geq\bar{\delta}/2
; if we need to define
\bar{x}^{\star}
as in case 2) above (note this only arises when
x^{\star}
is within
\bar{\delta}/4
of the right endpoint of
I
), since
\big|I\cap[0,\infty)\big|\geq\bar{\delta}/2
, the resulting
\bar{x}^{\star}
is always distance at least
\bar{\delta}/4>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
from the endpoints of
I
and 0.

Next suppose
0\leq x^{\star}\leq\bar{\delta}/4
.
We apply the argument in case 2) above with
\bar{x}^{\star}=\bar{\delta}/4
. Note that defining
\bar{I}
analogously, we have
\bar{I}\subseteq I
as
\big|I\cap[0,\infty)\big|\geq\bar{\delta}/2
. Combining with the fact that
|\bar{x}^{\star}-x^{\star}|\leq\bar{\delta}/4
and (
C.12
), we obtain (
C.15
).

2.

Suppose
\big|I\cap[0,\infty)\big|<\bar{\delta}/2
.
Therefore as
|I|\geq\bar{\delta}
,
\big|I\cap(-\infty,0]\big|\geq\bar{\delta}/2
.
We now apply the argument in either cases 1) or 2) above now with
\bar{x}^{\star}=-\bar{\delta}/4
, defining
\bar{\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
analogously and now defining
\bar{I}:=\big[\bar{x}^{\star}-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{\bar{x}^{\star}+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{1+\bar{\varepsilon}}\big]
.

Since the interval
I
is not fully contained in
[0,\infty)
or
(-\infty,0]
and
\big|I\cap(-\infty,0]\big|\geq\bar{\delta}/2
, we have
\bar{x}^{\star}\in I
, and furthermore that
\bar{I}\subseteq I\cap(-\infty,0]
. Also,
|\bar{x}^{\star}|\geq\bar{\delta}/4>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
. Finally, as
x^{\star}\geq 0
and
\big|I\cap[0,\infty)\big|<\bar{\delta}/2
, we have
|\bar{x}^{\star}-x^{\star}|\leq 3\bar{\delta}/4
. Combining with (
C.12
) gives (
C.15
).

This yields the desired lower bound in all cases. Now, (
C.5
) follows by taking
\varepsilon\leftarrow\varepsilon/2
.

To complete the proof, we must upper bound
\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\big(\mathbf{1}\{|\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}|\geq 1-\delta\}\cap C\big)
. As
\delta\leq\frac{1}{10}
, it remains to show that with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)\big)
for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)
, we have the following for a universal constant
c>0
. For the interpolators,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Big(\mathbf{1}\big\{|\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}|\geq 9/10\big\}\cap C\Big)<\sup_{x\in[-1+\delta,1-\delta]}\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)-c\,,

(C.16)

and for the posterior, defining
Z(I)
as per (
C.7
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\Big(Z\big([-1,-9/10]\big)+Z\big([9/10,1]\big)\Big)-\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)<\sup_{x\in[-1+\delta,1-\delta]}\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)-c\,.

(C.17)

We now upper bound the
LHS
in (
C.16
), (
C.17
).
By following the same proof as that of Lemma
C.4
, we have with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u(S_{i})\leq K(\lambda)\sqrt{nd}\,.

(C.18)

Standard bounds on the surface area of a spherical cap gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{|\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}|\geq 9/10\Big)\leq-c^{\prime}\,,

where
c^{\prime}>0
is a universal constant.
Letting
LHS
denote the
LHS
in (
C.16
), (
C.17
), combined with the bounds
u=\log\varphi\leq 0
for the logistic case for the posterior or (
C.18
) for the GMM case for the posterior, we obtain with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon,\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)\big)
and for
d\geq K
,

\displaystyle\mbox{\small\rm LHS}\leq-c^{\prime}+K(\lambda)\sqrt{\alpha}\,.

(C.19)

On the other hand, by the proof of 1) in Appendix
E.4
, we have
\sup_{x\in[-1,1]}\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)=\Phi(x^{\star},q^{\star})
for a unique
(x^{\star},q^{\star})\in[0,K(\lambda)\alpha]^{2}
. Since
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}(\lambda,\kappa_{+})
and
\delta\leq 1/10
, the bound (
E.39
) implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-1+\delta,1-\delta]}\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)=\Phi(x^{\star},q^{\star})\geq-\alpha K(\lambda,\kappa_{+})\,.

(C.20)

Since
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}(\lambda,\kappa_{+})
and
c^{\prime}>0
is a universal constant, combining (
C.19
), (
C.20
) now implies (
C.16
) for the interpolators or (
C.17
) for the posterior. This completes the proof of Theorems
3.1
,
3.5
.

C.2
Proof of Theorem
C.1
for interpolators:
\kappa<0

Throughout this proof, we let
\bar{I}:=[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]
.
Note
0\not\in\bar{I}
as
|x|>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
.
Consequently recalling (
A.9
), we note
\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}
does not depend on
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
as
r_{\bar{I}}(x)
, the only part depending on
\bar{I}
, equals
x^{2}
.
In the following Appendices
C.2
,
C.3
,
C.4
, we let
\mathrm{Area}(A)
refer to the surface area any Lebesgue measurable subset of
v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})
for any
v>0
.

Preliminaries:

Due to the lack of continuity caused by the hard indicator functions, we instead use the monotonicity from the following comparison inequalities.
For any
v>0
, consider

\displaystyle vU_{i}=\Bigl\{v\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Bigl(s_{i}\theta_{1}+\langle g_{i},\bar{\theta}\rangle\Bigr)\geq\kappa\Bigr\}=\Bigl\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Bigl(s_{i}\theta_{1}+\langle g_{i},\bar{\theta}\rangle\Bigr)\geq v\kappa\Bigr\}\,,

(C.21)

where the last equality follows from the transformation
\theta\leftarrow v\theta
.
Therefore as
\kappa<0
, we have

\displaystyle\text{For $v\geq 1$:}\quad vU_{i}\supseteq\Bigl\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Bigl(s_{i}\theta_{1}+\langle g_{i},\bar{\theta}\rangle\Bigr)\geq\kappa\Bigr\}=U_{i},\text{ hence }vC\supseteq C\,,

(C.22)

\displaystyle\text{For $v\leq 1$:}\quad vU_{i}\subseteq\Bigl\{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Bigl(s_{i}\theta_{1}+\langle g_{i},\bar{\theta}\rangle\Bigr)\geq\kappa\Bigr\}=U_{i},\text{ hence }vC\subseteq C\,.

(C.23)

We next set up several comparison inequalities letting us to relate the indicator
\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}
to
\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}
(the difference is between
I,\bar{I}
).
For any interval
[a,b]
, remark

\displaystyle\text{For $1\leq v\leq w$}:\quad v[a,b]\supseteq[wa,b]\text{ if $b\geq a\geq 0$}\quad,\quad v[a,b]\supseteq[a,wb]\text{ if $a\leq b\leq 0$}\,,

(C.24)

\displaystyle\text{For $w\leq v\leq 1$}:\quad v[a,b]\subseteq[wa,b]\text{ if $b\geq a\geq 0$}\quad,\quad v[a,b]\subseteq[a,wb]\text{ if $a\leq b\leq 0$}\,.

(C.25)

Proof of (
C.3
):

Let
w=1-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
here in the proof of (
C.3
) and let
I
be as per (
C.2
).
Consider any
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
. By (
C.23
) and (
C.25
), we have the following. If
x\geq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\mbox{\small\rm LHS}:=

\displaystyle\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\big\}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\geq\text{Vol}\Bigl(\mathbf{1}\big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]\big\}\cap C\cap\mathbf{1}\big\{w^{2}d\leq\|\theta\|^{2}\leq d\big\}\Bigr)\exp(-\beta d)

\displaystyle=\exp(-\beta d)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]\big\}\cap C\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta d)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in v\Big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\Big\}\cap vC\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=\exp(-\beta d)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\Big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\Big\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)v^{d-1}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=\exp(-\beta d)\,\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\,(1-w^{d})\,

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta d)\,\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{d}}\,.

Here we used the change of variable
\theta\leftarrow v\theta
, and that
w^{d}=(1-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})^{d}\leq\frac{1}{2}
for
d\geq d(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
.
Similarly if
x\leq-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}<0
, by an analogous derivation as above, again using (
C.23
) and (
C.25
),

LHS

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta d)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]\big\}\cap C\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta d)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in v\Big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w}\Big]\Big\}\cap vC\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta d)\,\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{d}}\,.

For both cases, taking logarithms gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\big(\mbox{\small\rm LHS}\big)

\displaystyle\geq-\beta+\frac{1}{d}\log\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)+\frac{1}{d}\log\Big(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{d}}\Big)\,.

(C.26)

By Theorem
B.1
applied with the interval
\bar{I}
, since
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
, we have for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\big(\mbox{\small\rm LHS}\big)\in\Big[\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta,x)-\varepsilon,\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta,x)+\varepsilon\Big]\,.

(C.27)

By Proposition
A.3
, as
0\not\in\bar{I}
, the Lipschitz constant of
\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)
over
(\alpha,\beta,x)\in[0,\alpha_{0}]\times[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]\in[-1,1]
is upper bounded in terms of
\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
.
Therefore, we obtain that for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
, we have
\big|\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta,x)-\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)\big|\leq\varepsilon
.
Noting

\displaystyle\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)=\mu_{d}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Big)\mathrm{Area}\big(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\big)\,,

combining with (
C.26
), (
C.27
) yields for the same
d
and the same probability as (
C.27
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Big)

(C.28)

\displaystyle\leq\beta+\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)+2\varepsilon+\frac{\log(2\sqrt{d})}{d}-\frac{1}{d}\log\mathrm{Area}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d}))\,.

By Lemma
C.3
, as
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
and as
0\not\in\bar{I}
,
\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)=\beta+\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)-\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)
for
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)
.
Moreover, recall
\lim_{d\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{d}\log\mathrm{Area}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d}))=\frac{1}{2}\log(2e\pi)
.

Taking
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)
in (
C.28
) thus gives for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Big)\leq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)+3\varepsilon\,,

where we note
\lim_{d\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{d}\log\mathrm{Area}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d}))-\frac{1}{2}\log(2e\pi)=0
. The result follows taking
\varepsilon\leftarrow\varepsilon/3
.

Proof of (
C.5
):

We proceed with a similar strategy to the proof of (
C.3
) above, though this direction is more involved.
First, by the second part of Proposition
A.3
,
\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)
is infinitely differentiable in
\beta
and
x
for
(\beta,x)\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]\times[-1,1]
. We thus may define

\displaystyle\underline{H}=\underline{H}(\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\inf_{(\beta,x)\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]\times[-1,1]}-\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)\,,

(C.29)

\displaystyle\overline{H}=\overline{H}(\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\sup_{(\beta,x)\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]\times[-1,1]}-\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)\,.

(C.30)

By Lemma
C.3
, Proposition
A.3
, and compactness,
\underline{H}>0
.
\underline{H}>0
yields strong convexity, which will be crucial in our subsequent arguments.
For the rest of the proof of (
C.5
), we consider
\varepsilon<\min\big\{1,\frac{1}{12}\underline{H}\big\}
.

Choice of parameters and properties of variational problem.

Let
w=1+\varepsilon
and define

\displaystyle\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta,x):=\bar{\Phi}\big(x,q_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x),\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)\big)-\beta\big(\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)+x^{2})\,,

(C.31)

where consider
\bar{\Phi}(x,q,\rho)
with
u(x)
therein such that
\exp u(x)=\mathbf{1}\{x\geq\kappa\}
. (Here as
0\not\in\bar{I}
, we have
\bar{f}=\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}-\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)
, however since
\bar{I}
is technically defined in terms of
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, we will define
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
in terms of
\bar{f}
and then consider
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
.) We now define the following positive parameters:

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}>0

\displaystyle\text{ as the unique solution to }\rho_{0}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta},x\big)=w^{2}-x^{2}\,,

(C.32)

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}_{1},\bar{\delta}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\bar{\delta}/2,

(C.33)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\min\Big\{\frac{1}{3}\Big(\bar{f}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}_{1},x\big)-\big(\bar{\delta}_{1}+\bar{f}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x\big)\big)\Big),\varepsilon\Big\}\,,

(C.34)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\min\Big\{\frac{1}{6}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}_{2}^{2},\varepsilon\Big\}\,,

(C.35)

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle:=\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}_{1}>\beta_{0}=\frac{1}{8}\,.

(C.36)

Therefore,
\bar{\delta},\bar{\delta}_{1},\bar{\delta}_{2},\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}
above depend on
x,\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda
. Later on we will show
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}
can be taken independently of
x
. First we will justify the statements implicit in the above, including positivity, and derive some useful estimates. Recall by Lemma
C.3
,
\beta(\alpha,x)\geq\frac{1}{4}
. Now note:

•

By definition of
\underline{H}
, for small enough
\varepsilon\leq\min\big\{1,\frac{1}{12}\underline{H}\big\}
, we have

\displaystyle\rho_{0}\big(\alpha,1/8,x\big)

\displaystyle\geq\rho_{0}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x\big)+\underline{H}\big(\beta(\alpha,x)-1/8\big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-x^{2}+3\varepsilon

\displaystyle>w^{2}-x^{2}

\displaystyle>1-x^{2}=\rho_{0}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x\big)\,.

As
\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)
is strictly decreasing on
[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
by Lemma
C.3
, it follows that such a
\bar{\delta}
exists, is unique, and that
\bar{\delta}>0
. This justifies (
C.32
).

•

We have

\displaystyle 1+\varepsilon\geq w

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\rho_{0}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta},x\big)+x^{2}}\geq\sqrt{\rho_{0}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x\big)+x^{2}+\underline{H}\bar{\delta}}=\sqrt{1+\underline{H}\bar{\delta}}\,,

thus as
\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{12}\underline{H}
, we have
\bar{\delta}<\frac{1}{8}
. Hence,
\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}_{1}\geq\frac{1}{4}-\bar{\delta}_{1}>\frac{1}{8}
, justifying (
C.36
).

•

By Lemma
C.3
, we have for all
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
that

\beta+\bar{f}\big(\alpha,\beta,x\big)>\beta(\alpha,x)+\bar{f}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x\big)\,.

Taking
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}_{1}>\frac{1}{8}=\beta_{0}
in the above justifies
\varepsilon_{1}>0
and hence (
C.34
). Moreover by the definition of
\varepsilon_{1}
, the above display gives

\displaystyle-\bar{\delta}_{1}+\bar{f}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}_{1},x\big)>\bar{f}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x\big)+3\varepsilon_{1}\,.

(C.37)

•

Let
F(x,q,\rho):=\bar{\Phi}(x,q,\rho)-\beta(\rho+x^{2})
and let
\rho_{0}=\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x),q_{0}=q_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)
.
Note
\frac{\partial F}{\partial\rho}=\frac{\partial F_{I}}{\partial\rho}
,
\frac{\partial F}{\partial q}=\frac{\partial F_{I}}{\partial q}
for any interval
I
, thus
\frac{\partial F}{\partial\rho}(x,q_{0},\rho_{0})=\frac{\partial F}{\partial q}(x,q_{0},\rho_{0})=0
.
Now for all
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta,x)

\displaystyle=-\big(\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)+x^{2}\big)+\frac{\partial F}{\partial\rho}(x,q_{0},\rho_{0})\cdot\frac{\partial\rho_{0}}{\partial\beta}+\frac{\partial F}{\partial q}(x,q_{0},\rho_{0})\cdot\frac{\partial q_{0}}{\partial\beta}

\displaystyle=-\big(\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)+x^{2}\big)\,.

(C.38)

Thus by definition of
\underline{H}
, we have for all
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\beta^{2}}\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta,x)=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)\geq\underline{H}>0\,.

(C.39)

Now in what follows, let
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}_{1}
as per (
C.36
), thus
\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2}=\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}
as
\bar{\delta}_{1}+\bar{\delta}_{2}=\bar{\delta}
.
By our choice of
w
,
\bar{\delta}
from (
C.32
), we obtain that for this choice of
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}_{1}
,

\displaystyle\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta,x)

\displaystyle\geq\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2},x)+\bar{\delta}_{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2},x)+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2},x)-\bar{\delta}_{2}\big(\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2},x)+x^{2}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2},x)-\bar{\delta}_{2}w^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2},x)-\bar{\delta}_{2}w^{2}+3\varepsilon_{2}\,.

(C.40)

As our last preliminary, we claim we can take
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}
, and

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}:=\min\big\{\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon_{1},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda),\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big\}>0

(C.41)

independently of
x
, where
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}
is from Theorem
B.1
. To this end first observe

\displaystyle w^{2}-x^{2}

\displaystyle=\rho_{0}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta},x\big)\leq\rho_{0}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x\big)+\overline{H}\bar{\delta}=1-x^{2}+\overline{H}\bar{\delta}\,.

Since
w=1+\varepsilon
, it follows that
\bar{\delta}>\frac{2}{\overline{H}}\varepsilon
, and hence
\bar{\delta},\bar{\delta}_{1},\bar{\delta}_{2}
are lower bounded independently of
x
.
Next note

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\big(\beta+\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta,x)\big)=1-\big(\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)+x^{2}\big)\,,\,\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\beta^{2}}\big(\beta+\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta,x)\big)=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)\geq\underline{H}>0

by (
C.38
), (
C.39
). Thus as
\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x)=1-x^{2}
by Lemma
C.3
, we have

\displaystyle\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}_{1}+\bar{f}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}_{1},x\big)

\displaystyle\geq\beta(\alpha,x)+\bar{f}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x\big)-\bar{\delta}_{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\big(\beta+\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta,x)\big)+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\beta(\alpha,x)+\bar{f}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x\big)+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}_{1}^{2}\,.

Therefore

\displaystyle\bar{f}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}_{1},x\big)\geq\bar{\delta}_{1}+\bar{f}\big(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x\big)+\frac{\underline{H}}{2}\bar{\delta}_{1}^{2}\,.

Since
\bar{\delta}>\frac{2}{\overline{H}}\varepsilon
, it follows from the above display and (
C.34
) that
\varepsilon_{1}
can be taken independently of
x
. Recalling
\bar{\delta}_{2}
is lower bounded independently of
x
and
\underline{H}
does not depend on
x
, it follows that
\varepsilon_{2}
can be taken independently of
x
.
Hence
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
can also be taken independently of
x
.

We now turn to establishing (
C.5
).
We consider any
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
,
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
, and define
I
as in (
C.4
).
Note
0\not\in\bar{I}
as
|x|>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
.
In the following proofs, we take
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)-\bar{\delta}_{1}
as per (
C.36
).

Initial Bound.

Suppose
x\geq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}>0
. Recall
I=[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]
where
w=1+\varepsilon
.
By (
C.22
) and (
C.24
),

LHS

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{d\leq\|\theta\|^{2}\leq w^{2}d\}\big\}\exp\big(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta d)\text{Vol}\Big(\mathbf{1}\big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]\big\}\cap C\cap\big\{d\leq\|\theta\|\leq w^{2}d\big\}\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp(-\beta d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\Big[w\cdot\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\Big\}\cap C\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in v\Big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\Big\}\cap vC\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=\exp(-\beta d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\Big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\Big\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)v^{d-1}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta d)\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}(w^{d}-1)\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,.

(C.42)

Analogously, when
x\leq-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}<0
, we have by (
C.22
) and (
C.24
) that

LHS

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\Big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},w\cdot\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w}\Big]\Big\}\cap C\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in v\Big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w}\Big]\Big\}\cap vC\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta d)\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}(w^{d}-1)\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,.

This yields the same inequality as (
C.42
) in the
x<0
case.
(Note here that
0\geq\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w}>x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
as
x\leq-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}<0
.)
Next, note

\displaystyle\mbox{\small\rm LHS}=\textsc{(I)}-\textsc{(II)}-\textsc{(III)}\,,

where

(I)

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\big\}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,,

(II)

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{\|\theta\|\leq\sqrt{d}\}\big\}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,,

(III)

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{\|\theta\|>w\sqrt{d}\}\big\}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,.

We now lower bound
(I)
and upper bound
\textsc{(II)},\textsc{(III)}
via Theorem
B.1
.

Lower bounding
(I)
:

As
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
, by definition of
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
in (
C.41
), and as
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
by (
C.36
), Theorem
B.1
applied with
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{1}\wedge\varepsilon_{2}
and the interval
\bar{I}
implies that for
d\geq d(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

(I)

\displaystyle=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\big\}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\geq\max\Big\{\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta,x)-\varepsilon_{1}/2\big)d\Big),\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta,x)-\varepsilon_{2}/2\big)\Big)d\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq\max\Big\{\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)-\varepsilon_{1}\big)d\Big),\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)-\varepsilon_{2}\big)d\Big)\Big\}\,.

(C.43)

Here, we used the second part of Proposition
A.3
in the last inequality, following identical reasoning as the proof for the upper bound to convert from
\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta,x)
to
\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)
.

Upper bounding
(II)
:

As
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon_{1},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
, by Theorem
B.1
, for
d\geq d(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon_{1},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

(II)

\displaystyle=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{\|\theta\|\leq\sqrt{d}\}\big\}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{\|\theta\|\leq\sqrt{d}\}\big\}\exp\big(-(\beta+\bar{\delta}_{1})\|\theta\|^{2}\big)\exp\big(\bar{\delta}_{1}\|\theta\|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\exp(\bar{\delta}_{1}d)\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\big\}\exp\big(-(\beta+\bar{\delta}_{1})\|\theta\|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\exp(\bar{\delta}_{1}d)\cdot\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta+\bar{\delta}_{1},x)+\varepsilon_{1}/2\big)d\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(\bar{\delta}_{1}d)\cdot\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta+\bar{\delta}_{1},x)+\varepsilon_{1}\big)d\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta+\bar{\delta}_{1},x)+\bar{\delta}_{1}+\varepsilon_{1}\big)d\Big)\,.

(C.44)

Here we again use Proposition
A.3
to convert from
\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta,x)
to
\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)
.
Now by (
C.37
) and as
\bar{f}=\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}-\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)
as
0\not\in\bar{I}
, combining (
C.43
), (
C.44
) yields that for
d\geq d(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
, with probability at least
1-\exp\big(d/K(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

(I)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)-\varepsilon_{1}\big)d\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\exp(\varepsilon_{1}d)\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta+\bar{\delta}_{1},x)+\bar{\delta}_{1}+\varepsilon_{1}\big)d\Big)\geq 4\textsc{(II)}\,.

(C.45)

Upper bounding
(III)
:

As
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
, by Theorem
B.1
, for
d\geq d(\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

(III)

\displaystyle=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{\|\theta\|>w\sqrt{d}\}\big\}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{\|\theta\|>w\sqrt{d}\}\big\}\exp(-(\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2})\|\theta\|^{2})\exp(-\bar{\delta}_{2}\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\bar{\delta}_{2}w^{2}d)\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\big\}\exp(-(\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2})\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\bar{\delta}_{2}w^{2}d)\cdot\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2},x)+\varepsilon_{2}/2\big)d\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\bar{\delta}_{2}w^{2}d)\cdot\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2},x)+\varepsilon_{2}\big)d\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2},x)-\bar{\delta}_{2}w^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}\big)d\Big)\,.

(C.46)

Here, we again used Proposition
A.3
.
By (
C.40
) and as
\bar{f}=\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}-\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)
, we obtain that for
d\geq d(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

(I)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)-\varepsilon_{2}\big)d\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\exp(\varepsilon_{2}d)\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta-\bar{\delta}_{2},x)-\bar{\delta}_{2}w^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}\big)d\Big)\geq 4\textsc{(III)}\,.

(C.47)

Finish.

Combining (
C.2
), (
C.47
), we obtain for
d\geq d(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
, with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
, we have

\displaystyle\mbox{\small\rm LHS}=\textsc{(I)}-\textsc{(II)}-\textsc{(III)}\geq\textsc{(I)}-\frac{1}{4}\textsc{(I)}-\frac{1}{4}\textsc{(I)}=\frac{1}{2}\textsc{(I)}\,.

Combining the above display with (
C.42
) and (
C.43
) gives for
d\geq d(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
, with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sqrt{d}}{w^{d}-1}\cdot\exp(\beta d)\cdot\frac{1}{2}\textsc{(I)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sqrt{d}}{2}\exp\Big(\big(-\log w+\beta+\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)-\varepsilon\big)d\Big)\,,

(C.48)

where we recall that
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\leq\varepsilon
. Recall
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
, so by Lemma
C.3
and as
0\not\in\bar{I}
,

\beta+\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)\geq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)+\frac{1}{2}\log(2e\pi)\,.

Since
\log w\leq w-1\leq\varepsilon
, we obtain for the same
d
and the same probability as (
C.48
),

\displaystyle\mu_{d}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Big)\mathrm{Area}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d}))\geq\frac{\sqrt{d}}{2}\exp\Big(\Big(\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)+\frac{1}{2}\log(2e\pi)-2\varepsilon\Big)d\Big)\,.

(C.49)

Again recall
\lim_{d\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{d}\log\mathrm{Area}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d}))=\frac{1}{2}\log(2e\pi)
, and that
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}
depend only on
\varepsilon
,
\alpha
,
\beta_{0},\beta_{1}
,
\kappa
,
\lambda
and do not depend on
x
as justified earlier. Rearranging (
C.49
) yields for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Big)\geq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)-4\varepsilon\,.

Taking
\varepsilon\leftarrow\varepsilon/4
completes the proof of (
C.5
).

C.3
Proof of Theorem
C.1
for interpolators:
\kappa\geq 0

Again, we let
\bar{I}:=[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]
, and we still have
0\not\in\bar{I}
as
|x|>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
.
In this proof many details are analogous to Appendix
C.2
above and we only highlight the differences.
The notation in the following is also identical to Appendix
C.2
.
Since
\kappa\geq 0
, (
C.21
) now yields the comparison inequalities

\displaystyle vC\subseteq C\text{ for }v\geq 1\quad,\quad vC\supseteq C\text{ for }v\leq 1\,.

(C.50)

Now for any interval
[a,b]
, we have

\displaystyle\text{For $1\leq v\leq w$}:\quad v[a,b]\subseteq[a,bw]\text{ if $b\geq a\geq 0$}\quad,\quad v[a,b]\subseteq[aw,b]\text{ if $a\leq b\leq 0$}\,,

(C.51)

\displaystyle\text{For $w\leq v\leq 1$}:\quad v[a,b]\supseteq[a,bw]\text{ if $b\geq a\geq 0$}\quad,\quad v[a,b]\supseteq[aw,b]\text{ if $a\leq b\leq 0$}\,.

(C.52)

Proof of (
C.3
):

Let
w=1+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
for the proof of this upper bound.
For any
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
, we obtain the following from (
C.50
), (
C.51
).
When
x>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
we have for
d\geq d(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
,

LHS

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\big\}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]\Big\}\cap C\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in v\Big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w}\Big]\Big\}\cap vC\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\,\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{d}}\,.

Similarly when
x<-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, for
d\geq d(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
we have

LHS

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]\Big\}\cap C\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in v\Big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\Big\}\cap vC\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\,\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\frac{1}{2\sqrt{d}}\,.

In either case, applying Theorem
B.1
with the interval
\bar{I}
, we have for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\bigl(\mbox{\small\rm LHS}\big)\in\Big[\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta,x)-\varepsilon,\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta,x)+\varepsilon\Big]\,.

(C.53)

We apply the second part of Proposition
A.3
identically as the proof in the
\kappa<0
case to convert the first argument of
\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(n/d,\beta,x)
to
\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)
for
d\geq d(\varepsilon,\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
.
We obtain for the same
d
and the same probability as (
C.53
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Big)\leq\beta w^{2}+\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)+K\varepsilon-\frac{1}{d}\log\mathrm{Area}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d}))+\frac{1}{d}\log(2\sqrt{d})\,.

We now take
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)
in the above, use Lemma
C.3
and the fact that
0\not\in\bar{I}
, and finish identically to the proof of the upper bound in the
\kappa<0
case.
Here we account for the presence of
\beta w^{2}
rather than
\beta
by noting
\beta(w^{2}-1)\leq 3\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\beta_{1}\leq 30\varepsilon
.
This proves (
C.3
) for the
\kappa\geq 0
case.

Proof of (
C.5
):

We let
w=1-\varepsilon
.
We define
\underline{H}=\underline{H}(\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)
,
\overline{H}=\overline{H}(\alpha,\kappa,\lambda)
identically as (
C.29
), (
C.30
) and define
\bar{f}
identically as (
C.31
).
We now let

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}=\bar{\delta}(\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda):=\min\Big\{\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2\overline{H}}\varepsilon\Big\}\,.

By definition of
\overline{H}
and as
\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)<0
by Lemma
C.3
, it follows that uniformly in
x\in[-1,1]
,

\displaystyle\big|\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x)-\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+2\bar{\delta},x)\big|\leq\varepsilon\,,

and hence by Lemma
C.3
,

\displaystyle\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+2\bar{\delta},x)\geq 1-x^{2}-\varepsilon\,.

(C.54)

Also note
2\bar{\delta}+\beta(\alpha,x)<\beta_{1}=10
, where we use that
\beta(\alpha,x)\leq 9
.
By (
C.38
), (
C.39
), and Lemma
C.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+\bar{\delta},x)

\displaystyle\geq\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x)+\bar{\delta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x)+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x)-\bar{\delta}\big(\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x)+x^{2}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x)-\bar{\delta}+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}^{2}\,.

(C.55)

Similarly we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+\bar{\delta},x)

\displaystyle\geq\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+2\bar{\delta},x)-(-\bar{\delta})\big(\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+2\bar{\delta},x)+x^{2}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+2\bar{\delta},x)+\bar{\delta}(1-\varepsilon)+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+2\bar{\delta},x)+\bar{\delta}w^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}^{2}\,,

(C.56)

where we used (
C.54
) and
w=1-\varepsilon
combined with
\varepsilon<1
.
Finally, we let

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}

\displaystyle:=\min\Big\{\frac{1}{6}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}^{2},\varepsilon\Big\}\,,

(C.57)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}

\displaystyle:=\min\big\{\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon_{1},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda),\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big\}>0\,.

(C.58)

We now turn to establishing (
C.5
).
We consider any
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
,
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
, and define
I
as in (
C.4
).
Thus
0\not\in\bar{I}
as
|x|>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
.
We now let
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)+\bar{\delta}
for the rest of this proof.
First suppose
x\geq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}>0
.
Here we have by (
C.50
), (
C.52
) that

LHS

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{w^{2}d\leq\|\theta\|^{2}\leq d\}\big\}\exp\big(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\text{Vol}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\Big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},w\cdot\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w}\Big]\Big\}\cap C\cap\{w^{2}d\leq\|\theta\|\leq d\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in v\Big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w}\Big]\Big\}\cap vC\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,.

(C.59)

Analogously, when
x\leq-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}<0
, we have

LHS

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\Big[w\cdot\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\Big\}\cap C\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in v\Big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\Big\}\cap vC\cap v\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\mathrm{Area}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})\Big)\,.

(Note here that
0\geq x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}>\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w}
.)
Now note

\displaystyle\mbox{\small\rm LHS}=\textsc{(I)}-\textsc{(II)}-\textsc{(III)}\,,

where

(I)

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\big\}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,,

(II)

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{\|\theta\|\geq\sqrt{d}\}\big\}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,,

(III)

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{\|\theta\|\leq w\sqrt{d}\}\big\}\exp(-\beta\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,.

We again lower bound
(I)
and upper bound
\textsc{(II)},\textsc{(III)}
via Theorem
B.1
. First, by Theorem
B.1
with interval
\bar{I}
and the second part of Proposition
A.3
, as
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
and
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
, we have for
d\geq d(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
,

\displaystyle\textsc{(I)}\geq\max\Big\{\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)-\varepsilon_{1}\big)d\Big),\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)-\varepsilon_{2}\big)d\Big)\Big\}\,.

By Theorem
B.1
and Proposition
A.3
, for the same
d
and with the same probability,

(II)

\displaystyle=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{\|\theta\|\geq\sqrt{d}\}\big\}\exp(-(\beta-\bar{\delta})\|\theta\|^{2})\exp(-\bar{\delta}\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\bar{\delta}d)\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\big\}\exp(-(\beta-\bar{\delta})\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta-\bar{\delta},x)-\bar{\delta}+\varepsilon_{1}\big)d\Big)\,.

Analogously, for the same
d
and with the same probability,

(III)

\displaystyle=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap C\cap\{\|\theta\|\leq w\sqrt{d}\}\big\}\exp(-(\beta+\bar{\delta})\|\theta\|^{2})\exp(\bar{\delta}\|\theta\|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta+\bar{\delta},x)+\bar{\delta}w^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}\big)d\Big)\,.

Recall
\beta-\bar{\delta}=\beta(\alpha,x)
and
\bar{f}=\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}-\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi e)
as
0\not\in\bar{I}
. Thus applying (
C.55
), (
C.56
) to upper bound
(II)
,
(III)
respectively gives for
d\geq d(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
and probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big)
, we have
\textsc{(II)},\textsc{(III)}\leq\frac{1}{4}\textsc{(I)}
. Therefore as
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\leq\varepsilon
, for the same
d
and with the same probability,

\displaystyle\mbox{\small\rm LHS}\geq\frac{1}{2}\textsc{(I)}\geq\frac{1}{2}\exp\Big(\big(\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)-\varepsilon\big)d\Big)\,.

Via the same steps as the
\kappa<0
case, combining the above display with (
C.59
) yields that for the same
d
and with the same probability,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log\mu_{d}\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\cap C\Big)+\frac{1}{d}\log\mathrm{Area}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d}))\geq\beta w^{2}+\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)-\varepsilon+\frac{1}{d}\log\Big(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{2}\Big)\,.

Note that as
\beta\leq\beta_{1}=10
, we have
\beta w^{2}\geq\beta-3\beta\varepsilon\geq\beta-30\varepsilon
.
Finally, as
0\not\in\bar{I}
, we recall
\beta+\textsc{RSG}_{\bar{I}}(\alpha,\beta,x)\geq\inf_{q\in[0,1)}\Phi(x,q)
by Lemma
C.3
. Combining these steps with the above display and taking
\varepsilon\leftarrow\varepsilon/K
for universal constant
K
, (
C.5
) follows.

C.4
Proof of Theorem
C.2
for posterior

Much of this proof is identical to the above and we will only highlight the differences.
We again define
\bar{I}:=[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]
and follow the same notation as Appendices
C.2
,
C.3
.
Similarly as before, we parametrize
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by the scalar
v=\|\theta\|/\sqrt{d}
and
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})
.

By Lipschitzness of
u(x)
, instead of the comparison inequalities relating
vC
and
C
, we directly study how
u\big((s_{i}\theta_{1}+\langle g_{i},\bar{\theta}\rangle)/\sqrt{d}\big)
changes in
v
via the following Lemma.

Lemma C.4
.

With probability at least
1-\exp(-d/K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}})
, we have for all
v\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(v\theta)\big)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(\theta)\big)\Big|\leq K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}|v-1|d\,,

where
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}\geq 1
depends on
\lambda
. Here probability is over the
s_{i},g_{i}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.

Note
S_{i}(v\theta)=vS_{i}(\theta)
. As
u(\cdot)
is
D
-Lipschitz, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(vS_{i}(\theta)\big)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(\theta)\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq D|v-1|\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big|S_{i}(\theta)\big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq D|v-1|\Big(\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|s_{i}\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}|+\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big|\langle g_{i},\bar{\theta}\rangle/\sqrt{d}\big|\Big)

\displaystyle\quad:=D|v-1|\big(\textsc{(I)}+\textsc{(II)}\big)\,.

(C.60)

We now establish high-probability upper bounds on
\textsc{(I)},\textsc{(II)}
.
Starting with
(I)
, observe for any
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})
that
\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\leq 1
. Hence
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|s_{i}\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}|s_{i}|
. Recall the
s_{i}^{2}
are sub-Exponential with parameter
K_{\textsc{signal}}=K(\lambda)
, and hence the
|s_{i}|
are
K(\lambda)
sub-Gaussian. As
n\leq 2d
, Hoeffding’s Inequality implies with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\lambda)\big)
, we have

\displaystyle\textsc{(I)}\leq K(\lambda)d\,.

(C.61)

We now upper bound
(II)
.
Since
\|\bar{\theta}\|\leq\sqrt{d}
,
(II)
is maximized for
\|\bar{\theta}\|=\sqrt{d}
. Shifting
d
by 1, it suffices to consider

\displaystyle F(G):=\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big|\langle g_{i},\theta\rangle/\sqrt{d}\big|

where now
g_{i}\sim N(0,I_{d})
and where
G
refers to the
n\times d
Gaussian matrix with rows
g_{i}
.

Consider two Gaussian matrices
G_{0}=\{g_{i}^{0}\}_{i=1}^{n},G_{1}=\{g_{i}^{1}\}_{i=1}^{n}
whose rows
g_{i}^{0},g_{i}^{1}
are i.i.d. from
N(0,I_{d})
. If
F(G_{1})\geq F(G_{0})
, by continuity of
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big|\langle g_{i}^{1},\theta\rangle/\sqrt{d}\big|
in
\theta
, we have for some
\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})
,

\displaystyle F(G_{1})-F(G_{0})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big|\langle g_{i}^{1},\theta^{\prime}\rangle/\sqrt{d}\big|-F(G_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big|\langle g_{i}^{1},\theta^{\prime}\rangle/\sqrt{d}\big|-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big|\langle g_{i}^{0},\theta^{\prime}\rangle/\sqrt{d}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big|\langle g_{i}^{1}-g_{i}^{0},\theta^{\prime}\rangle/\sqrt{d}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|g_{i}^{1}-g_{i}^{0}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(g_{ij}^{1}-g_{ij}^{0})^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\,.

An analogous bound applies if
F(G_{0})\geq F(G_{1})
. Therefore
F(G)
is
\sqrt{n}
-Lipschitz as a function of
G
measured w.r.t. Euclidean norm. Clearly the density of
G
is 1 strongly log-concave. It follows by concentration of Lipschiz functions of strongly log-concave measures, Theorem
B.3
, that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Big(F(G)\geq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[F(G)\big]+t)\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{t^{2}}{Kn}\Big)\,,

(C.62)

where probability is over
G
.
Next we upper bound
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[F(G)\big]
. Writing
u=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})
where each
u_{i}\in\{\pm 1\}
, observe that

\displaystyle F(G)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d}),u\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{i}\langle g_{i},\theta\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d}),u\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{d}u_{i}\theta_{j}g_{ij}\,.

Define the centered Gaussian processes
X_{\theta,u}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{d}u_{i}\theta_{j}g_{ij}
,
Y_{\theta,u}:=\|u\|_{2}\langle g,\theta\rangle+\|\theta\|_{2}\langle h,u\rangle
, where
u=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})^{T}
, and where
g\sim N(0,I_{d})
,
h\sim N(0,I_{n})
are independent.
For any
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})
,
u,u^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
, observe

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[(X_{\theta,u}-X_{\theta^{\prime},u^{\prime}})^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=\|\theta\|^{2}\|u\|^{2}+\|\theta^{\prime}\|^{2}\|u^{\prime}\|^{2}-2\langle u,u^{\prime}\rangle\langle\theta,\theta^{\prime}\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[(Y_{\theta,u}-Y_{\theta^{\prime},u^{\prime}})^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=2\|\theta\|^{2}\|u\|^{2}+2\|\theta^{\prime}\|^{2}\|u^{\prime}\|^{2}-2\big(\|u\|\|u^{\prime}\|\langle\theta,\theta^{\prime}\rangle+\|\theta\|\|\theta^{\prime}\|\langle u,u^{\prime}\rangle\big)\,.

As
\|\theta\|=\|\theta^{\prime}\|=\sqrt{d},\|u\|=\|u^{\prime}\|=\sqrt{n}
and
\big(n-\langle u,u^{\prime}\rangle\big)\big(d-\langle\theta,\theta^{\prime}\rangle\big)\geq 0
, it follows that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[(X_{\theta,u}-X_{\theta^{\prime},u^{\prime}})^{2}\big]\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[(Y_{\theta,u}-Y_{\theta^{\prime},u^{\prime}})^{2}\big]\,.

By the Sudakov-Fernique inequality (see e.g. Theorem 7.2.8,
[
Ver18
]
), noting
\|u\|=\sqrt{n},\|\theta\|=\sqrt{d}
always holds for
u\in\{\pm 1\}^{n},\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[F(G)\big]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d}),u\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}}X_{\theta,u}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d}),u\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}}Y_{\theta,u}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\langle g,\theta\rangle\big]+\sqrt{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sup_{u\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}}\langle h,u\rangle\big]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{nd}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|g\|_{2}\big]+\sqrt{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|h\|_{1}\big]

\displaystyle=O(n^{1/2}d)\,.

Using
n\leq 2d
, it follows that
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[F(G)\big]\leq O(d)
. Combining with (
C.62
) gives that with probability at least
1-\exp(-d/K)
, we have

\displaystyle\textsc{(II)}\leq Kd\,.

(C.63)

Now combining (
C.4
), (
C.61
), (
C.63
) with a Union Bound, the Lemma follows.
∎

We now have the technical ingredients in place to prove Theorem
C.2
.

Proof of (
C.9
):

Let
K^{\prime}(\lambda):=K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}(\lambda)
, where
K^{\prime}
is as in the statement of Theorem. We thus consider
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\min\{\varepsilon/K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}(\lambda),\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}\}
and let
I
be as per (
C.8
). Let
w=1+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
.

For any
\beta\in[\beta_{0},\beta_{1}]
, by (
C.51
), we obtain the following, very similarly to the proof of (
C.3
).
When
x>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, we have for
d\geq d(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
,

LHS

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(\theta)\big)-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\geq\int\mathbf{1}\Big\{\big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]\big\}\cap\big\{d\leq\|\theta\|^{2}\leq w^{2}d\big\}\Big\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(\theta)\big)-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\mathbf{1}\big\{v\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]\big\}v^{d-1}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(v\theta)\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\Big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w}\Big]\Big\}v^{d-1}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(v\theta)\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}v\,,

where the last inequality uses (
C.51
).
When
x<-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, we have for
d\geq d(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
, now using (
C.51
) in the
a\leq b\leq 0
case,

\displaystyle\mbox{\small\rm LHS}\geq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\Big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\Big\}v^{d-1}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(v\theta)\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}v\,.

By Lemma
C.4
, with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\lambda)\big)
, we have for all
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})
and all
v,1\leq v\leq w
that

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(v\theta)\big)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(\theta)\big)\Big|\leq K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}|v-1|d\leq\varepsilon d\,,

where we use that
1\leq v\leq w\leq 1+\varepsilon/K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}
. Combining the above displays and recalling the definition of
I
in (
C.8
) yields that for
d\geq d(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
, with probability at least
1-\exp\big(d/K(\lambda)\big)
,

LHS

\displaystyle\geq\exp\big(-\beta w^{2}d-\varepsilon d\big)\sqrt{d}\int_{1}^{w}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\mathbf{1}\big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\big\}v^{d-1}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(\theta)\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\exp\big(-\beta w^{2}d-\varepsilon d\big)}{2\sqrt{d}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\mathbf{1}\big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\big\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(\theta)\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,.

That is,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log Z(I)\leq\frac{1}{d}\log(\mbox{\small\rm LHS})+\frac{1}{d}\log(2\sqrt{d})+\beta w^{2}+\varepsilon\,.

(C.64)

With (
C.64
), the rest of the proof of (
C.9
) now follows identically as the proof of (
C.3
), the one difference being that we apply Theorem
B.2
rather than Theorem
C.2
to control
\frac{1}{d}\log(\mbox{\small\rm LHS})
.

Proof of (
C.11
):

Let
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}=K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}(\lambda)\geq 1
, and
w=1-\frac{\varepsilon}{K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}}
.
Recall that Lemma
C.3
, Proposition
A.3
still hold here, where the RS equations are given in terms of
u(x)
rather than the hard indicator
\mathbf{1}\{\cdot\geq\kappa\}
.
Thus we may perform the following steps.

In particular, define
\underline{H},\overline{H}
as in (
C.29
), (
C.30
). We now define

\displaystyle\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta,x):=\bar{\Phi}\big(x,q_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x),\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)\big)-\beta\big(\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta,x)+x^{2})\,,

(C.65)

where consider
\bar{\Phi}(x,q,\rho)
given with
u(x)
(rather than the hard indicator function
\mathbf{1}\{\cdot\geq\kappa\}
for the interpolators).
We now let

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}=\bar{\delta}(\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda):=\min\Big\{\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2\overline{H}K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}}\varepsilon\Big\}\,.

Analogously as the proof of (
C.54
), we have uniformly in
x\in[-1,1]
:

\displaystyle\rho_{0}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+2\bar{\delta},x)\geq 1-x^{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}}\,.

(C.66)

Again analogously the proof of (
C.55
), (
C.56
), now using (
C.66
) and
\varepsilon<1,K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}\geq 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+\bar{\delta},x)

\displaystyle\geq\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x),x)-\bar{\delta}+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}^{2}\,,

(C.67)

\displaystyle\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+\bar{\delta},x)

\displaystyle\geq\bar{f}(\alpha,\beta(\alpha,x)+2\bar{\delta},x)+\bar{\delta}w^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}^{2}\,.

(C.68)

Finally, we again let

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}=\varepsilon_{2}

\displaystyle:=\min\Big\{\frac{1}{6}\underline{H}\bar{\delta}^{2},\varepsilon\Big\}\,,

(C.69)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}

\displaystyle:=\min\big\{\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon_{1},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda),\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}(\varepsilon_{2},\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda)\big\}>0\,,

(C.70)

where now
\varepsilon_{\textsc{RSG}}
comes from Theorem
B.2
.

We now turn to establishing (
C.11
).
Again, consider any
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
,
0<\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\leq\varepsilon_{\textsc{RS}}
, and define
I
as in (
C.10
).
Thus
0\not\in\bar{I}
as
|x|>\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
.
We now let
\beta=\beta(\alpha,x)+\bar{\delta}
for the rest of this proof.
First suppose
x\geq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}>0
.
Here we have by (
C.52
) that

LHS

\displaystyle:=\int\mathbf{1}\big\{\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\bar{I}\}\cap\{w^{2}d\leq\|\theta\|^{2}\leq d\}\big\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(\theta)\big)-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\mathbf{1}\big\{v\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}]\big\}v^{d-1}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(v\theta)\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\Big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w}\Big]\Big\}v^{d-1}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(v\theta)\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}v\,.

Analogously, when
x\leq-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}<0
, we have

LHS

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\beta w^{2}d)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\mathbf{1}\Big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in\Big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\Big\}v^{d-1}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(v\theta)\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}v\,.

By Lemma
C.4
, with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-d/K(\lambda)\big)
, we have for all
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})
and all
v,w\leq v\leq 1
that

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(v\theta)\big)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(\theta)\big)\Big|\leq K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}|v-1|d\leq\varepsilon d\,,

where we use that
1-\varepsilon/K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}=w\leq v\leq 1
.
Note as
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:posterior_spherical_continuity}}}\geq 1
,
w\geq 1-\varepsilon
. Therefore by the definition of
I
in (
C.10
),

\displaystyle\Big[x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime},\frac{x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w}\Big]\subseteq I\text{ when }x\geq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\,,\,\Big[\frac{x-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}{w},x+\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\Big]\subseteq I\text{ when }x\leq-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\,.

Combining the above displays now yields that with probability at least
1-\exp\big(d/K(\lambda)\big)
,

LHS

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-\beta w^{2}d+\varepsilon d\big)\sqrt{d}\int_{w}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\mathbf{1}\big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\big\}v^{d-1}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(\theta)\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}v
