\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\big(-\beta w^{2}d+\varepsilon d\big)}{\sqrt{d}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(\sqrt{d})}\mathbf{1}\big\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\big\}\exp\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u\big(S_{i}(\theta)\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,.

That is,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\log Z(I)\geq\frac{1}{d}\log(\mbox{\small\rm LHS})+\frac{1}{d}\log\sqrt{d}+\beta w^{2}-\varepsilon\,.

(C.71)

With (
C.71
), the rest of the proof of (
C.11
) now follows identically as the proof of (
C.5
) in the
\kappa\geq 0
case. We again write

\displaystyle\mbox{\small\rm LHS}=\textsc{(I)}-\textsc{(II)}-\textsc{(III)}\,,

where now
\mathbf{1}\{C\}
in the definition of
\textsc{(I)},\textsc{(II)},\textsc{(III)}
is replaced by
\exp\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}u(S_{i}(\theta))\big)
. (Observe that in the proof of (
C.5
),
C
takes the same form, for
u(x)=\mathbf{1}\{x\geq\kappa\}
.)
We now apply Theorem
B.2
to establish high-probability formulas for
\textsc{(I)},\textsc{(II)},\textsc{(III)}
up to accuracy
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}
, where
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}
are as defined in (
C.69
). Combined with (
C.67
), (
C.68
), this proves that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\textsc{(I)}\geq\textsc{(II)},\textsc{(III)}\,.

Combining with (
C.71
) and finishing as in the proof of (
C.5
) now establishes (
C.11
).

Appendix D
Technical results for proof of Theorems
A.1
,
A.2

Here we prove the intermediate technical results Lemma
A.5
, Lemma
A.8
, and Proposition
A.6
on the interpolation argument and concentration of measure, used in the proof of Theorems
A.1
and
A.2
. Throughout Appendix
D
, for the interpolators we let
D
be as stated in Theorem
A.1
, and for the posterior we recall that
|u^{l}|\leq D=D(\lambda)
for
l=1,2,3,4
.
Also here in Appendix
D
, as per the proof for the interpolators, we will always write dependence on
\kappa
in
K
when it is present in the following proofs; note there is no such dependence for the posterior.

We first prove Lemma
A.5
, which lets us ‘transfer’ expectations of the form
\nu_{t,v}(f)
for
0\leq v\leq 1
to expectations
\nu_{t,1}(f)
when
v=1
, using some of the ideas from cavity in
n
.
We then finish the cavity in
n
argument and execute the cavity in
d
argument to prove Lemma
A.8
, which gives us control over the derivatives
\big|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{1}\theta_{d}^{2}\big)\big|
,
\big|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{1})^{2}\big)\big|
arising in the interpolation argument.
Both these arguments will crucially use Proposition
A.6
on concentration of the interpolating Gibbs measure
\nu_{t,v}(\cdot)
, which we prove later in Appendix
D.3
.
The proof of Proposition
A.6
will crucially use classical results on concentration of Lipschitz functions of strongly log-concave measures; note the quadratic component
-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}
we added when relaxing to the Gaussian measure is strongly concave and
u(\cdot)
in Theorem
A.1
is concave.

D.1
Proof of Lemma
A.5

Our first step will be to establish the following on the derivative of
\nu_{t,v}
w.r.t.
v
.

Lemma D.1
.

Consider any
t\in[0,1]
and a function
B_{v}
that is either 1,
u^{\prime}(S_{n,t,v}^{1})u^{\prime}(S_{n,t,v}^{2})
, or
u^{\prime\prime}(S_{n,t,v}^{1})+u^{\prime}(S_{n,t,v}^{1})^{2}
. Then for any
f:(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})^{L}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\nu_{t,v}(fB_{v})\Big|
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle K\Bigg(\sum_{\ell\leq L+1}\nu_{t,v}\big(\big|f\big|\big|R_{\ell,\ell}-\rho_{n,d}\big|\big)+\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}\leq L+2}\nu_{t,v}\big(\big|f\big|\big|R_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}-q_{n,d}\big|\big)+\Big(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\frac{1}{d}\Big)\nu_{t,v}(f^{2})^{1/2}\Bigg)\,,

where
K
depends on
D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda,L
, and also on
\kappa
for the interpolators.

For proving Lemma
A.5
we will only need Lemma
D.1
with
B_{v}=1
.
However the full generality where
B_{v}
equals 1,
u^{\prime}(S_{n,t,v}^{1})u^{\prime}(S_{n,t,v}^{2})=u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{2})
, or
u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})+u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})^{2}
will be needed in proving Lemma
A.8
later, specifically in the proof of Lemma
D.4
.

Proof of Lemma
D.1
.

Throughout this proof, we use the shorthand
S_{v}^{\ell}=S_{n,t,v}^{\ell}
. Recall

\displaystyle\nu_{t,v}(fB_{v})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\frac{\big\langle fB_{v}\exp\big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}u(S^{\ell}_{v})\big)\big\rangle_{t,\sim}}{\big\langle\exp\big(u(S^{L+1}_{v})\big)\big\rangle_{t,\sim}^{L}}=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{g(v)}{h(v)}\Big]\,,

where we let

\displaystyle g(v):=\Big\langle fB_{v}\exp\big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}u(S^{\ell}_{v})\big)\Big\rangle_{t,\sim}\,,\,h(v):=\Big\langle\exp\big(u(S^{L+1}_{v})\big)\Big\rangle_{t,\sim}^{L}\,.

Note the denominator of the Gibbs average defining
\langle\cdot\rangle_{t,\sim}
does not depend on
v
. We thus compute

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial v}h(v)=L\Big\langle\exp\big(u(S^{L+1}_{v})\big)\Big\rangle_{t,\sim}^{L-1}\,\Big\langle\exp\big(u(S^{L+1}_{v})\big)u^{\prime}(S^{L+1}_{v})\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial v}S^{L+1}_{v}\Big\rangle_{t,\sim}\,,

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial v}g(v)=\Big\langle fB_{v}\exp\Big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}u(S^{\ell}_{v})\Big)\Big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{v})\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial v}S^{\ell}_{v}+r(v)\Big)\Big\rangle_{t,\sim}\,,

where

\displaystyle r(v):=\begin{cases}0&:B_{v}=1\,,\\
u^{\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})u^{\prime}(S^{2}_{v})\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial v}S^{1}_{v}+u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})u^{\prime\prime}(S^{2}_{v})\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial v}S^{2}_{v}&:B_{v}=u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{2})\,,\\
u^{\prime\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial v}S^{1}_{v}+2u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})u^{\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial v}S^{1}_{v}&:B_{v}=u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})+u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})^{2}\,.\end{cases}

Note

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial v}S^{\ell}_{v}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{\theta_{1}^{\ell}}{\sqrt{d}}-x\Big)s_{n}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{vd}}\Big(\sum_{2\leq j\leq d-1}g_{n,j}\theta_{j}^{\ell}+\sqrt{t}g_{n,d}\theta_{d}^{\ell}\Big)-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{1-v}}\big(\sqrt{q_{n,d}}Z+\sqrt{\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d}}W^{\ell}\big)\,.

Collecting common terms into the ‘signal part’
(I)
corresponding to
s_{n}
and the rest of the expression
(II)
for
\frac{\partial}{\partial v}S^{\ell}_{v}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\nu_{t,v}(fB_{v})

\displaystyle=\textsc{(I)}+\textsc{(II)}\,,

where
\textsc{(I)},\textsc{(II)}
are defined by

(I)

\displaystyle:=\nu_{t,v}\Bigg(fB_{v}\Bigg(\sum_{\ell\leq L}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{v})\cdot\Big(\frac{\theta_{1}^{\ell}}{\sqrt{d}}-x\Big)s_{n}+r_{1}(v)\Bigg)\Bigg)-L\nu_{t,v}\Bigg(fB_{v}\cdot u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{L+1})\cdot\Big(\frac{\theta_{1}^{L+1}}{\sqrt{d}}-x\Big)s_{n}\Bigg)\,,

where

\displaystyle r_{1}(v)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}0&:B_{v}=1\,,\\
u^{\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})u^{\prime}(S^{2}_{v})\cdot\Big(\frac{\theta_{1}^{1}}{\sqrt{d}}-x\Big)s_{n}+u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})u^{\prime\prime}(S^{2}_{v})\cdot\Big(\frac{\theta_{1}^{2}}{\sqrt{d}}-x\Big)s_{n}&:B_{v}=u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})u^{\prime}(S^{2}_{v})\,,\\
\big(u^{\prime\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})+2u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})u^{\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})\big)\cdot\Big(\frac{\theta_{1}^{1}}{\sqrt{d}}-x\Big)s_{n}&:B_{v}=u^{\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})+u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})^{2}\,,\end{cases}

and

(II)

\displaystyle:=\nu_{t,v}\Bigg(fB_{v}\Big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{v})\bar{S}_{v}^{\ell}+r_{2}(v)\Big)\Bigg)-L\nu_{t,v}\Big(fB_{v}u^{\prime}(S^{L+1}_{v})\bar{S}_{v}^{L+1}\Big)\,,

where

\displaystyle\bar{S}_{v}^{\ell}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{vd}}\Big(\sum_{2\leq j\leq d-1}g_{n,j}\theta_{j}^{\ell}+\sqrt{t}g_{n,d}\theta_{d}^{\ell}\Big)-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{1-v}}\big(\sqrt{q_{n,d}}Z+\sqrt{\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d}}W^{\ell}\big)\,,

\displaystyle r_{2}(v)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}0&:B_{v}=1\,,\\
u^{\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})u^{\prime}(S^{2}_{v})\bar{S}^{1}_{v}+u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})u^{\prime\prime}(S^{2}_{v})\bar{S}^{2}_{v}&:B_{v}=u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})u^{\prime}(S^{2}_{v})\,,\\
\big(u^{\prime\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})+2u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})u^{\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})\big)\bar{S}^{1}_{v}&:B_{v}=u^{\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})+u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})^{2}\,.\end{cases}

First we upper bound
\big|\textsc{(I)}\big|
, via an analogous derivation as in the proof of Proposition
A.4
(specifically, the derivation of the upper bound on
\big|\textsc{(I)}\big|
therein).
Since
\exp(\cdot)\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\textsc{(I)}\big|\leq K(L)\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq L+1}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\frac{\Big\langle\big|fs_{n}\big(\frac{\theta_{1}^{\ell}}{\sqrt{d}}-x\big)h_{\ell}\big|\cdot\exp\big(\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq L+1}u(S_{v}^{\ell})\big)\Big\rangle_{t,\sim}}{\big(\big\langle\exp u(S_{n,t,v}^{L+2})\big\rangle_{t,\sim}\big)^{L+1}}\,,

where
h_{\ell}
is a sum of products of
u^{(l^{\prime})}(s_{v}^{1}),\ldots,u^{(l^{\prime})}(s_{v}^{L+1})
for
l^{\prime}=1,2,3
, given by the above definition for
(I)
. Thus we have
|h_{\ell}|\leq K(D,L)
by our bounds on the first 4 derivatives of
u(\cdot)
. Due to the indicator
\mathbf{1}\{\theta^{\ell}_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}
in the definition of the Gibbs measure
\langle\cdot\rangle_{t,\sim}
from (
A.24
), which is applied for all
1\leq\ell\leq L+1
since each
fs_{n}\big(\frac{\theta_{1}^{\ell}}{\sqrt{d}}-x\big)h_{\ell}
is a function of all the
L+1
replicas, we can upper bound the numerator in the above expression as follows:

\displaystyle\Big\langle\Big|fs_{n}\Big(\frac{\theta_{1}^{\ell}}{\sqrt{d}}-x\Big)h_{\ell}\Big|\cdot\exp\Big(\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq L+1}u(S_{v}^{\ell})\Big)\Big\rangle_{t,\sim}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}K(D,L)\cdot\Big\langle\big|f\big|\big|s_{n}\big|\exp\Big(\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq L+1}u(S_{v}^{\ell})\Big)\Big\rangle_{t,\sim}\,.

Thus by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\big|\textsc{(I)}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}K(D,L)\cdot\nu_{t,v}\big(|f||s_{n}|\big)\leq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}K(D,L)\cdot\nu_{t,v}(s_{n}^{2})^{1/2}\nu_{t,v}(f^{2})^{1/2}\,.

Since
s_{n}
does not depend on the replicas
\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L}
or the annealed disorder
W^{1},\ldots,W^{L}
, we have
\nu_{t,v}(s_{n}^{2})^{1/2}=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[s_{n}^{2}]^{1/2}\leq K(\lambda)
. Combining with the above yields

\displaystyle\big|\textsc{(I)}\big|\leq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}K(D,\lambda,L)\cdot\nu_{t,v}(f^{2})^{1/2}\,.

(D.1)

Next we upper bound
\big|\textsc{(II)}\big|
. We simplify
(II)
via Guassian Integration by Parts w.r.t. the
g_{n,j},g_{n,d},z,W^{\ell}
.
We give the computation in the case where
B_{v}=u^{\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})+u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})^{2}
, the computation when
B_{v}=1
or
B_{v}=u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{2})
is analogous. This computation is analogous to the proof of Lemma 3.2.8,
[
Tal10
]
; this is because the derivatives of
S_{v}^{\ell}
w.r.t. the
g_{n,j},g_{n,d},z,W^{\ell}
are the same as when the
S_{v}^{\ell}
are defined without the signal part. A similar computation is detailed on the proof of Lemma 2.3.2, p. 172 of
[
Tal10
]
. Spelling out the computation in full detail when
B_{v}=u^{\prime\prime}(S^{1}_{v})+u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{v})^{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\nu_{t,v}\Bigg(fB_{v}\Big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{v})\bar{S}_{v}^{\ell}+r_{2}(v)\Big)\Bigg)

(D.2)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\nu_{t,v}\Big(f(R_{1,1}-\rho_{n,d})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})\big(u^{\prime\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})+2u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq L}\nu_{t,v}\Big(f(R_{1,1}-\rho_{n,d})\big(u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{\ell})+u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{\ell})^{2}\big)B_{v}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{2\leq\ell\leq L}\nu_{t,v}\Big(f(R_{1,l}-q_{n,d})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{\ell})\big(u^{\prime\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})+2u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{1\leq\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}\leq L}\nu_{t,v}\Big(f(R_{l,l^{\prime}}-q_{n,d})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{\ell})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{\ell^{\prime}})B_{v}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{L}{2}\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq L}\nu_{t,v}\Big(f(R_{l,L+1}-q_{n,d})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{\ell})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{L+1})B_{v}\Big)+\mbox{\rm err}_{1}\,,

and

\displaystyle\nu_{t,v}\Big(fB_{v}r_{2}(v)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\nu_{t,v}\Bigg(f(R_{1,1}-\rho_{n,d})\Big(\big(u^{\prime\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})+2u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})\big)^{2}

(D.3)

\displaystyle+\big(u^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})+2u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})^{2}+2u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})\big)B_{v}

\displaystyle+\big(u^{\prime\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})+2u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})\big)u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})B_{v}\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{2\leq\ell\leq L}\nu_{t,v}\Big(f(R_{1,l}-q_{n,d})\big(u^{\prime\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})+2u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})\big)u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})B_{v}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{L}{2}\nu_{t,v}\Big(f(R_{1,L+1}-q_{n,d})\big(u^{\prime\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})+2u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})\big)u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{L+1})\Big)+\mbox{\rm err}_{2}\,,

and

\displaystyle\nu_{t,v}\Big(fB_{v}u^{\prime}(S^{L+1}_{v})\bar{S}_{v}^{L+1}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\nu_{t,v}\Big(f(R_{L+1,L+1}-\rho_{n,d})\big(u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{L+1}+u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{L})^{2})\big)B_{v}\Big)

(D.4)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\nu_{t,v}\Big(f(R_{1,L+1}-q_{n,d})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{L+1})\big(u^{\prime\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})+2u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime\prime}(S_{v}^{1})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq L}\nu_{t,v}\Big(f(R_{l,L+1}-q_{n,d})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{\ell})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{L+1})B_{v}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{L+1}{2}\nu_{t,v}\Big(f(R_{L+1,L+2}-q_{n,d})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{L+1})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{L+2})B_{v}\Big)+\mbox{\rm err}_{3}\,,

where
\mbox{\rm err}_{1},\mbox{\rm err}_{2},\mbox{\rm err}_{3}
are such that

\displaystyle|\mbox{\rm err}_{1}|,|\mbox{\rm err}_{2}|,|\mbox{\rm err}_{3}|\leq\frac{1-t}{2d}\cdot K\nu_{t,v}\big(|f|\max_{1\leq\ell\leq L+2}(\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}\big)\,.

In particular,
\mbox{\rm err}_{1},\mbox{\rm err}_{2},\mbox{\rm err}_{3}
arise because the last coordinate
\theta_{d}^{\ell}
is interpolated via the parameter
t\in[0,1]
.
By the second part of Proposition
A.6
and Cauchy-Schwarz we have

\displaystyle|\mbox{\rm err}_{1}|,|\mbox{\rm err}_{2}|,|\mbox{\rm err}_{3}|\leq\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda,L)}{d}\cdot\nu_{t,v}(f^{2})^{1/2}\,.

(D.5)

Recall that

\displaystyle\textsc{(II)}=\nu_{t,v}\Bigg(fB_{v}\Big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{v})\bar{S}_{v}^{\ell}+r_{2}(v)\Big)\Bigg)-L\nu_{t,v}\Big(fB_{v}u^{\prime}(S^{L+1}_{v})\bar{S}_{v}^{L+1}\Big)\,.

Thus combining (
D.2
), (
D.3
), (
D.4
) and using the bound (
D.5
) on
|\mbox{\rm err}_{1}|,|\mbox{\rm err}_{2}|,|\mbox{\rm err}_{3}|
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\textsc{(II)}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda,L)\Big(\sum_{\ell\leq L+1}\nu_{t,v}\big(\big|f\big|\big|R_{\ell,\ell}-\rho_{n,d}\big|\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}\leq L+2}\nu_{t,v}\big(\big|f\big|\big|R_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}-q_{n,d}\big|\big)+\frac{1}{d}\cdot\nu_{t,v}(f^{2})^{1/2}\Big)\,.

Again, we obtain an analogous bound as the above when
B_{v}=1
or
B_{v}=u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{1})u^{\prime}(S_{v}^{2})
, with the same arguments. Combining this bound with the bound (
D.1
) on
|\textsc{(I)}|
proves Lemma
D.1
.
∎

We will also use the following variant of Grönwall’s Inequality:

Lemma D.2

(Lemma A.13.1 in
[
Tal10
]
)
.

Suppose a differentiable function
g:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
g\geq 0
is such that
\Big|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}g(t)\Big|\leq c_{1}g(t)+c_{2}
for some
c_{1},c_{2}\geq 0
. Then

\displaystyle g(t)\leq\exp\big(c_{1}(1-t)\big)\Big(g(1)+\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}\Big)\,.

Now we return to the proof of Lemma
A.5
.
By the second part of Proposition
A.6
,

\displaystyle\nu_{t,v}\Big(\Big\{\big|R_{\ell,\ell}-\rho_{n,d}\big|\geq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq K\exp(-N)\,,

\displaystyle\nu_{t,v}\Big(\Big\{\big|R_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}-q_{n,d}\big|\geq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\Big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq K\exp(-N)\text{ for }\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}\,.

Here to bound
\rho_{n,d},q_{n,d}
we apply Proposition
A.6
with
t=v=1
. Additionally, the second pat of Proposition
A.6
combined with Lemma
A.7
implies that

\displaystyle\nu_{t,v}\big((R_{\ell,\ell}-\rho_{n,d})^{4}\big)^{1/4}\,,\,\nu_{t,v}\big((R_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}-q_{n,d})^{4}\big)^{1/4}\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,.

Now Hölder’s Inequality implies that

\displaystyle\nu_{t,v}\big(|f|\big|R_{\ell,\ell}-\rho_{n,d}\big|\big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\nu_{t,v}\big(|f|\mathbf{1}\{|R_{\ell,\ell}-\rho_{n,d}|\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\nu_{t,v}\big(f^{2}\big)^{1/2}\nu_{t,v}\big(\big|R_{\ell,\ell}-\rho_{n,d}\big|^{4}\big)^{1/4}\nu_{t,v}\big(\mathbf{1}\{|R_{\ell,\ell}-\rho_{n,d}|\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\}\big)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\quad\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\nu_{t,v}\big(|f|\big)+K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\exp(-N)\nu_{t,v}\big(f^{2}\big)^{1/2}\,.

The same argument as above establishes the same upper bound on
\nu_{t,v}\big(|f|\big|R_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}-q_{n,d}\big|\big)
.

Now applying Lemma
D.1
with
B_{v}=1
, and combining with the above, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\nu_{t,v}(f)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda,L)\Bigg(\sum_{\ell\leq L+1}\nu_{t,v}\big(|f|\big|R_{\ell,\ell}-\rho_{n,d}\big|\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}\leq L+2}\nu_{t,v}\big(|f|\big|R_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}-\rho_{n,d}\big|\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Big(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\frac{1}{d}\Big)\nu_{t,v}(f^{2})^{1/2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda,L)\Bigg(\nu_{t,v}\big(|f|\big)+\Big(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\frac{1}{d}+\exp(-d)\Big)\nu_{t,v}\big(f^{2}\big)^{1/2}\Bigg)\,.

For
f\geq 0
we thus have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\nu_{t,v}(f)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda,L)\Bigg(\nu_{t,v}\big(f\big)+\Big(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\frac{1}{d}\Big)\sup_{t,v\in[0,1]^{2}}\nu_{t,v}\big(f^{2}\big)^{1/2}\Bigg)\,.

Recalling
\nu_{t}\equiv\nu_{t,1}
, we conclude Lemma
A.5
via Lemma
D.2
.

D.2
Proof of Lemma
A.8

First we explicitly compute the derivative
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\nu_{t}(f)
using Gaussian Integration by Parts:

Lemma D.3
.

For a function
f:(\mathbb{R}^{d-1})^{L}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\nu_{t}(f)=\textsc{(I)}_{f}+\textsc{(II)}_{f}+\textsc{(III)}_{f}+\textsc{(IV)}_{f}+\textsc{(V)}_{f}\,,

where we define the quantities
\textsc{(I)}_{f},\textsc{(II)}_{f},\textsc{(III)}_{f},\textsc{(IV)}_{f},\textsc{(V)}_{f}
as follows:

\displaystyle\textsc{(I)}_{f}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\alpha}{2}\Big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t})^{2})f\big)-L\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{L})^{2}(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{L+1}_{n,t})+u^{\prime}(S^{L+1}_{n,t})^{2})f\big)\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\textsc{(II)}_{f}

\displaystyle:=\alpha\Big(\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1}<\ell_{2}\leq L}\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{1}}_{n,t})u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{2}}_{n,t})f\big)-L\sum_{\ell\leq L}\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell}\theta_{d}^{L+1}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t})u^{\prime}(S^{L+1}_{n,t})f\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{L(L+1)}{2}\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{L+1}\theta_{d}^{L+2}u^{\prime}(S^{L+1}_{n,t})u^{\prime}(S^{L+2}_{n,t})f\big)\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\textsc{(III)}_{f}

\displaystyle:=-r\Big(\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1}<\ell_{2}\leq n}\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}f\big)-L\sum_{\ell\leq L}\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell}\theta_{d}^{L+1}f\big)+\frac{L(L+1)}{2}\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{L+1}\theta_{d}^{L+2}f\big)\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\textsc{(IV)}_{f}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{r}{2}\Big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big)-L\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{L+1})^{2}f\big)\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\textsc{(V)}_{f}

\displaystyle:=\frac{r-\bar{r}}{2}\Big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big)-L\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{L+1})^{2}f\big)\Big)\,.

Proof of Lemma
D.3
.

Note

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\nu_{t}(f)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\leq L}\nu_{t}\Big(f\frac{\partial}{\partial t}H^{\ell}_{t,n}\Big)-L\nu_{t}\Big(f\frac{\partial}{\partial s}H^{L+1}_{t,n}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}H^{\ell}_{t,n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\leq n}u^{\prime}(S_{i,t}^{\ell})\cdot\frac{1}{2\sqrt{td}}g_{i,d}\theta^{\ell}_{d}-\sqrt{\frac{r}{1-t}}\theta^{\ell}_{d}Y+\frac{r-\bar{r}}{2}\big(\theta^{\ell}_{d}\big)^{2}\,.

The result now follows by symmetry w.r.t.
i,1\leq i\leq n
, and then applying Gaussian Integration by Parts w.r.t.
g_{n,d},z^{\prime\prime}
. (The argument is analogous to the proof of Proposition 3.2.1,
[
Tal10
]
.)
∎

Next collecting like terms from Lemma
D.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\nu_{t}(f)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell\leq L}\Big(\alpha\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t})^{2})f\big)-\bar{r}\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big)\Big)

(D.6)

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq\ell_{1}<\ell_{2}\leq L}\Big(\alpha\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{1}}_{n,t})u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{2}}_{n,t})f\big)-r\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}f\big)\Big)

\displaystyle-L\sum_{\ell\leq L}\Big(\alpha\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell}\theta_{d}^{L+1}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t})u^{\prime}(S^{L+1}_{n,t})f\big)-r\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell}\theta_{d}^{L+1}f\big)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{L(L+1)}{2}\Big(\alpha\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{L+1}\theta_{d}^{L+2}u^{\prime}(S^{L+1}_{n,t})u^{\prime}(S^{L+2}_{n,t})f\big)-r\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{L+1}\theta_{d}^{L+2}f\big)\Big)\,.

We now upper bound the right hand side in the above.
This is where we use the particular choice of
r,\bar{r}
from (
A.22
), (
A.23
).
Specifically, we will employ cavity in
n
over the parameter
v
to prove:

Lemma D.4
.

Let
f=\big(\theta_{d}^{1}\big)^{2}
or
\theta_{d}^{1}\theta_{d}^{2}
. For any
t\in[0,1]
and any
\ell\leq L+2
,
1\leq\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}\leq L+2
,

\displaystyle\Big|\alpha\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t})^{2})f\big)-\bar{r}\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\vee\Big|\alpha\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{1}}_{n,t})u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{2}}_{n,t})f\big)-r\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}f\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq K\Bigg(\nu_{t}\big((R_{1,2}-q_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\nu_{t}\big((R_{1,1}-\rho_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\Big(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\frac{1}{d}\Big)\Bigg)\,,

where
K
depends on
D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda,L
, and also on
\kappa
for the interpolators.

Proof of Lemma
D.4
.

Recall
S_{n,t}^{\ell}=S_{n,t,1}^{\ell}
. First, we show

\displaystyle\alpha\nu_{t,0}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,0})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,0})^{2})f\big)

\displaystyle=\bar{r}\nu_{t,0}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big)\,

(D.7)

\displaystyle\alpha\nu_{t,0}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{1}}_{n,t,0})u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{2}}_{n,t,0})f\big)

\displaystyle=r\nu_{t,0}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}f\big)\,.

(D.8)

We prove (
D.7
), the proof of (
D.8
) being analogous. Note
S_{n,t,0}^{\ell}=xs_{n}+\sqrt{q_{n,d}}Z+\sqrt{\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d}}W^{\ell}
does not depend on
\theta
,
Y
,
g_{i,j}
,
s_{i}
for
i\leq n-1
and that
H_{t,n-1,d}
does not depend on
W
,
z
, or
s_{n}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\Big\langle(\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,0})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,0})^{2})f\,\exp\Big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}u(S^{\ell}_{n,t,0})\Big)\Big\rangle_{t,\sim}

\displaystyle\quad=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Big[\big(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,0})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,0})^{2}\big)\,\exp\Big(\sum_{\ell\leq L}u(S^{\ell^{\prime}}_{n,t,0})\Big)\Big]\,\big\langle(\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big\rangle_{t,\sim}\,.

By the same rationale,
\nu_{t,0}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f)=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big\langle(\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big\rangle_{t,\sim}
. Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\nu_{t,0}\Big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}\big(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,0})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,0})^{2}\big)f\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}[(u^{\prime\prime}(S_{n,t,0}^{\ell})+u^{\prime}(S_{n,t,0}^{\ell})^{2})\exp u(S_{n,t,0}^{\ell})]}{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}[\exp u(S_{n,t,0}^{L+1})]}\cdot\big\langle(\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big\rangle_{t,\sim}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{z,s_{n}}\Bigg[\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}[(u^{\prime\prime}(S_{n,t,0}^{\ell})+u^{\prime}(S_{n,t,0}^{\ell})^{2})\exp u(S_{n,t,0}^{\ell})]}{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}[\exp u(S_{n,t,0}^{\ell})]}\Bigg]\cdot\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{Y,g_{i,j},s_{i}:i\leq n-1}\Big[\big\langle(\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big\rangle_{t,\sim}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\bar{r}}{\alpha}\nu_{t,0}\Big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\Big)\,.

This proves (
D.7
). Again, (
D.8
) follows with an analogous argument.

In light of (
D.7
), (
D.8
), recalling
\nu_{t}\equiv\nu_{t,1}
, we next decompose

\displaystyle\Big|\alpha\nu_{t}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t})^{2})f\big)-\bar{r}\nu_{t,1}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big)\Big|

(D.9)

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\alpha\nu_{t,1}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,1})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,1})^{2})f\big)-\alpha\nu_{t,0}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,0})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,0})^{2})f\big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\bar{r}\nu_{t,0}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big)-\bar{r}\nu_{t,1}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big)\Big|\,,

and similarly we decompose

\displaystyle\Big|\alpha\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{1}}_{n,t})u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{2}}_{n,t})f\big)-r\nu_{t}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}f\big)\Big|

(D.10)

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\alpha\nu_{t,1}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{1}}_{n,t})u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{2}}_{n,t})f\big)-\alpha\nu_{t,0}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{1}}_{n,t})u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{2}}_{n,t})f\big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|r\nu_{t,0}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}f\big)-r\nu_{t,1}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}f\big)\Big|\,.

Note
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
and
\big|r\big|,\big|\bar{r}\big|\leq\alpha_{0}\cdot K(D)
as
|u^{\prime}|,|u^{\prime\prime}|\leq D
. Now consider any
v\in[0,1]
. We apply Lemma
D.1
with the function
(\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f
, recalling here that
f=\big(\theta_{d}^{1}\big)^{2}
or
\theta_{d}^{1}\theta_{d}^{2}
.
Applying Cauchy-Schwarz on each of the terms arising from Lemma
D.1
, and using Proposition
A.6
and Lemma
A.7
, we obtain that
\nu_{t,v}(f^{2})^{1/2}\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
. Therefore we get

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\nu_{t,v}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,1})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,1})^{2})f\big)\Big|\,,\,\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\nu_{t,v}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda,L)\Bigg(\nu_{t,v}\big((R_{1,2}-q_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\nu_{t,v}\big((R_{1,1}-\rho_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\Big(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\frac{1}{d}\Big)\Bigg)\,,

where we use symmetry between replicas.
Next applying Lemma
A.5
with
f=R_{1,1}-\rho_{n,d}
and
f=R_{1,2}-q_{n,d}
, and then applying the second part of Proposition
A.6
with Lemma
A.7
, we obtain

\displaystyle\nu_{t,v}\big((R_{1,2}-q_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\Bigg(\nu_{t}\big((R_{1,2}-q_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\Big(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\frac{1}{d}\Big)\Bigg)\,,

and analogously for
\nu_{t,v}\big((R_{1,1}-\rho_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}
. Combining the above two displays, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\nu_{t,v}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}(u^{\prime\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,1})+u^{\prime}(S^{\ell}_{n,t,1})^{2})f\big)\Big|\,,\,\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\nu_{t,v}\big((\theta_{d}^{\ell})^{2}f\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda,L)\Bigg(\nu_{t}\big((R_{1,2}-q_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\nu_{t}\big((R_{1,1}-\rho_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\Big(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\frac{1}{d}\Big)\Bigg)\,.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\nu_{t,v}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{1}}_{n,t})u^{\prime}(S^{\ell_{2}}_{n,t})f\big)\Big|\,,\,\Big|\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\nu_{t,v}\big(\theta_{d}^{\ell_{1}}\theta_{d}^{\ell_{2}}f\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda,L)\Bigg(\nu_{t}\big((R_{1,2}-q_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\nu_{t}\big((R_{1,1}-\rho_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\Big(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\frac{1}{d}\Big)\Bigg)\,.

Combining the above with (
D.9
), (
D.10
), and noting
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}\leq 2
, the Lemma follows.
∎

Combining Lemma
D.4
and (
D.6
), it follows that for
f=\big(\theta_{d}^{1}\big)^{2}
or
\theta_{d}^{1}\theta_{d}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\nu_{t}(f)\Big|\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda,L)\Bigg(\nu_{t}\big((R_{1,2}-q_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\nu_{t}\big((R_{1,1}-\rho_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}+\Big(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}+\frac{1}{d}\Big)\Bigg)\,.

By Proposition
A.6
, we have

\displaystyle\nu_{t}\big((R_{1,1}-\rho_{n,d})^{2}\big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq K\nu_{t}\big((R_{1,1}-\nu_{t}(R_{1,1}))^{2}\big)^{1/2}+2\big|\nu_{t}(R_{1,1})-\nu_{1}(R_{1,1})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}{\sqrt{d}}+2\big|\nu_{t}(R_{1,1})-\nu_{1}(R_{1,1})\big|\,,

and similarly for
R_{1,2}
. Here we use that
\rho_{n,d}=\nu_{1}(R_{1,1})
,
q_{n,d}=\nu_{1}(R_{1,2})
. Thus combining the above two displays, to complete the proof of Lemma
A.8
, it suffices to upper bound

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\nu_{t}(R_{1,1})\Big|\,,\,\Big|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})\Big|\leq\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}{\sqrt{d}}\,.

To this end, considering
f=R_{1,2}
and following the notation of Lemma
D.3
, we note that

\displaystyle\textsc{(I)}_{f}=\textsc{(I)}_{f}-\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})=\textsc{(I)}_{f-\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})}\,,

because
\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})
is just a constant (note that for any constant function
c
,
\textsc{(I)}_{c}=0
). Similarly
\textsc{(II)}_{f}=\textsc{(II)}_{f-\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})}
,
\textsc{(III)}_{f}=\textsc{(III)}_{f-\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})}
,
\textsc{(IV)}_{f}=\textsc{(IV)}_{f-\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})}
,
\textsc{(V)}_{f}=\textsc{(V)}_{f-\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})}
. However note that
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
and
r,\bar{r}\leq K(D,\alpha_{0})
. Thus by Proposition
A.6
, since
|u^{\prime}|,|u^{\prime\prime}|\leq D
,
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}\leq 2
, and by Hölder’s Inequality used on each individual summand comprising the following expressions, we obtain

\displaystyle\textsc{(I)}_{f-\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})}\,,\,\textsc{(II)}_{f-\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})}\,,\,\textsc{(III)}_{f-\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})}\,,\,\textsc{(IV)}_{f-\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})}\,,\,\textsc{(V)}_{f-\nu_{t}(R_{1,2})}\leq\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}{\sqrt{d}}\,.

The same derivation applies for
f=R_{1,1}
, thus completing the proof of Lemma
A.8
.

D.3
Proof of Proposition
A.6

Here, we prove Proposition
A.6
.
For only Appendix
D.3
, we define the following Gibbs average
\langle\cdot\rangle
:

\displaystyle\langle f\rangle:=\frac{\big\langle f\exp\big(\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq L}u(S_{n,t,v}^{\ell})\big)\big\rangle_{t,\sim}}{\big\langle\exp u(S_{n,t,v}^{L+1})\big\rangle_{t,\sim}^{L}}\,.

Consequently by (
A.26
), we have
\nu_{t,v}(f)=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\langle f\rangle
.
Note we can rewrite
\langle f\rangle
as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Z^{\prime L}}\int\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Bigg[f\prod_{1\leq l\leq L}\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\Big(u\big(S^{l}_{n,t,v}(\theta^{l})\big)+H_{t,n-1,d}(\theta^{l})-\beta\|\theta^{l}\|^{2}\Big)\Bigg]\,\mathrm{d}\theta^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\theta^{L}\,,

(D.11)

where

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\Big(u(S_{n,t,v})+H_{t,n-1,d}(\theta)-\beta\|\theta\|^{2}\Big)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,.

(D.12)

Note the constraint
\prod_{1\leq l\leq L}\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}
is convex,
u
is concave, the
S_{n,t,v}
and
S_{i,t}
are affine in
\theta
, and the rest of
H_{t,n-1,d}
is log-concave or affine in
\theta
. It follows that the measure
\langle\cdot\rangle
is
\beta
-strongly log-concave in
\theta
, a fact that will be exploited repeatedly via concentration of Lipschitz functions of strongly log-concave measures (Theorem
B.3
) in what follows.

D.3.1
First part of Proposition
A.6

We prove the first part of Proposition
A.6
by directly combining the following Propositions
D.5
,
D.6
. First, we have the following concentration property of
f=R_{1,1}
or
R_{1,2}
about their average
\langle f\rangle
with the quenched disorder fixed:

Proposition D.5
.

Consider
u(\cdot)
as in Theorem
A.1
for the interpolators or Theorem
A.2
for the posterior.
Then for
d\geq d_{\textup{\ref{prop:concentrationoverlapfixedgibbs}}}(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
and all
k\leq d
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\langle\big(R_{1,1}-\langle R_{1,1}\rangle\big)^{2k}\Big\rangle\,,\,\Big\langle\big(R_{1,2}-\langle R_{1,2}\rangle\big)^{2k}\Big\rangle\leq\Big(\frac{kKB^{\star}}{d^{2}}\Big)^{k}\,,

where
K
depends on
D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
and also on
\kappa
for the interpolators, and where

\displaystyle B^{\star}

\displaystyle:=\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\bar{g}_{i}\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+\frac{1}{16d}\sum_{j=2}^{d}\Big\{\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{ij}\Big)^{2}+g_{nj}^{2}\Big\}

(D.13)

\displaystyle+\Big(n\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+\frac{1}{8dK_{\textsc{signal}}}\Big\{\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}s_{i}\Big)^{2}+s_{n}^{2}\Big\}+Y^{2}+Z^{2}+d\,.

Second, we have concentration of
\langle f\rangle
about
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\langle f\rangle
:

Proposition D.6
.

Consider
u(\cdot)
as in Theorem
A.1
for the interpolators or Theorem
A.2
for the posterior. Then for
k\leq\frac{d}{4}
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big(\big\langle R_{1,1}\big\rangle-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big\langle R_{1,1}\big\rangle\big)^{2k}\Big]~,~\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big(\big\langle R_{1,2}\big\rangle-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big\langle R_{1,2}\big\rangle\big)^{2k}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{Kk}{d}\Big)^{k}\,,

where
K
depends on
D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
, and also on
\kappa
for the posterior.

Combining Proposition
D.5
and Proposition
D.6
yields the first part of Proposition
A.6
. For the rest of the proof of the first part of Proposition
A.6
, we assume
u(\cdot)
satisfies the conditions of Theorem
A.1
.
Central in the proofs of both Proposition
D.5
and Proposition
D.6
is concentration of Lipschitz functions w.r.t strongly log-concave measures, as written in Theorem
B.3
.
We will also use the following fact which follows by a standard symmetrization argument, see (3.22) of
[
Tal10
]
.

Lemma D.7
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{R}
, any function
f
such that the following is defined, and any probability measure
\mu
, we have

\displaystyle\int\Big(f-\int f\mathrm{d}\mu\Big)^{2k}\,\mathrm{d}\mu\leq 2^{2k}\int(f-m)^{2k}\,\mathrm{d}\mu\,.

Proof of Proposition
D.5
:

We now prove Proposition
D.5
.
First, we show that
B^{\star}
can be controlled on the exponential scale:

Lemma D.8
.

For all
k\leq d
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[B^{\star k}\big]\leq\big(Kd\big)^{k}\,,

where
K
depends on
\lambda
.

Proof.

Our strategy will be to upper bound
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp B^{\star}\big]
and then convert this to a bound on the
k
-th moment of
B^{\star}
by Lemma
A.7
. Since
n\leq 2d
, and by applying Cauchy-Schwarz on the
(s_{i})
,

\displaystyle B^{\star}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{8n}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\bar{g}_{i}\|^{2}\Big)+2n+\frac{1}{16d}\sum_{j=2}^{d}\Big\{\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{ij}\Big)^{2}+g_{nj}^{2}\Big\}+\frac{1}{2K_{\textsc{signal}}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}^{2}\Big)+K_{\textsc{signal}}n

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{Y^{2}}{4}+\frac{Z^{2}}{4}+Kd

\displaystyle=\textsc{(I)}+\textsc{(II)}+\textsc{(III)}+\frac{Y^{2}}{4}+\frac{Z^{2}}{4}+(K_{\textsc{signal}}+2)n+Kd\,,

where we define

\displaystyle\textsc{(I)}:=\frac{1}{8n}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\bar{g}_{i}\|^{2}\Big)\,,\,\textsc{(II)}:=\frac{1}{16d}\sum_{j=2}^{d}\Big\{\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{ij}\Big)^{2}+g_{nj}^{2}\Big\}\,,\,\textsc{(III)}:=\frac{1}{2K_{\textsc{signal}}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}^{2}\Big)\,.

By independence of
g_{ij},s_{i},Y,Z
, and as
n\leq 2d
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp B^{\star}\big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp\big(\textsc{(I)}+\textsc{(II)}\big)\big]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp\textsc{(III)}\big]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp Y^{2}/4]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp Z^{2}/4]\exp\big(K(\lambda)d\big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp 2\textsc{(I)}]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp 2\textsc{(II)}]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp\textsc{(III)}\big]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp Y^{2}/4]\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp Z^{2}/4]\exp\big(K(\lambda)d\big)\,,

(D.14)

where the last step used Cauchy-Schwarz. Note
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\big(\exp\frac{Y^{2}}{4}\big)\big]
,
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\big(\exp\frac{Z^{2}}{4}\big)\big]\leq K
as
Y,Z\sim N(0,1)
.
It remains to upper bound
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp 2\textsc{(I)}\big]
,
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp 2\textsc{(II)}\big]
, and
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp\textsc{(III)}\big]
.

•

Upper bounding
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp 2\textsc{(I)}\big]
: By a direct calculation, we have the following, as stated as (A.11) in
[
Tal10
]
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\Big(\exp\frac{g_{ij}^{2}}{4n}\Big)\Big]\leq\Big(1-\frac{1}{2n}\Big)^{-1/2}\,.

Thus the independence of the
(g_{ij})
implies

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp 2\textsc{(I)}\big]\leq\Big(1-\frac{1}{2n}\Big)^{-nd/2}\leq K^{d}\leq\exp(Kd)\,.

•

Upper bounding
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp 2\textsc{(II)}\big]
: By independence of the
(g_{ij})
, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{ij}\sim N(0,n-1)
for all
2\leq j\leq d
, and thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8d}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{ij}\Big)^{2}+\frac{1}{8d}g_{nj}^{2}\sim\frac{1}{8d}N(0,n-1)^{2}+\frac{1}{8d}N(0,1)^{2}\sim\frac{n}{8d}N(0,1)^{2}\implies 2\textsc{(II)}\sim\frac{n}{8d}\chi^{2}_{d-1}\,,

where
\chi^{2}_{d-1}
denotes a
\chi^{2}
distribution with
d-1
degrees of freedom. Since
n\leq 2d
, standard bounds on the MGF of a
\chi^{2}
random variable now gives

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp 2\textsc{(II)}\big]=\Big(1-2\cdot\frac{n}{8d}\Big)^{-\frac{d-1}{2}}\leq 2^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\leq\exp(Kd)\,.

•

Upper bounding
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp\textsc{(III)}\big]
: Since the
s_{i}^{2}
are
K_{\textsc{signal}}
sub-Exponential, by e.g. Proposition 2.7.1 of
[
Ver18
]
), we have
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp s_{i}^{2}/2K_{\textsc{signal}}\big]\leq K
. As the
(s_{i})
are independent, we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp\textsc{(III)}\big]\leq K^{n}\leq\exp(Kd)\,.

Combining the bounds in the above three cases together with (
D.14
) and recalling
K_{\textsc{signal}}
depends only on
\lambda
implies

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\exp B^{\star}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(K(\lambda)d\big)\,,

(D.15)

and the conclusion now follows from Lemma
A.7
.
∎

We next lower bound the partition function
Z^{\prime}
in order to apply Lemma
B.19
to control the relevant overlaps.

Lemma D.9
.

For
d\geq d_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}}(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
, letting
Z^{\prime}
be as per
(
D.12
)
, we have

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}\geq\exp\big(-K_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}}B^{\star}\big)\,,

where
K_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}}
depends on
D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
, and also on
\kappa
for the posterior.

Proof.

We will prove this Lemma in two stages. First we will prove it when
v=t=1
, which lets us upper bound
|q_{n,d}|
. Then we use this upper bound on
|q_{n,d}|
to complete the proof for general
t,v\in[0,1]
. Note the conditions on
u
from Theorem
A.1
give
u(x)\geq-D(\kappa)(|x|+1)
for the interpolators; similarly, we have
u(x)\geq-D(\lambda)(|x|+1)
for the posterior. In an abuse of notation, we will write this bound as
u(x)\geq-D(\kappa,\lambda)(|x|+1)
, where we emphasize that there is no dependence on
\kappa
for the posterior.

We begin with steps of the proof that are common to both stages.
Rewrite

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\theta_{1}\in I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Big[\exp\Big(u(S_{n,t,v})+\sum_{1\leq i\leq n-1}u(S_{i,t})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\theta_{d}\sqrt{(1-t)r}Y-\frac{(1-t)(r-\bar{r})}{2}\theta_{d}^{2}-\beta\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}-\beta\theta_{1}^{2}\Big)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\overline{\theta}\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}\,.

Notice
\bar{r}\leq r\leq K(D)
as per their definitions in (
A.20
), (
A.21
), thus
\frac{(1-t)(r-\bar{r})}{2}\leq\frac{r-\bar{r}}{2}\leq K_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}}(D)
. Letting
\beta^{\prime}:=K_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}}(D)+\beta
, we consider the density
\gamma
on
\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
with density
(\beta^{\prime}/\pi)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\exp(-\beta^{\prime}\|\overline{\theta}\|^{2})
.
We observe that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Big[\exp\Big(u(S_{n,t,v})+\sum_{1\leq i\leq n-1}u(S_{i,t})+\theta_{d}\sqrt{(1-t)r}Y-\frac{(1-t)(r-\bar{r})}{2}\theta_{d}^{2}-\beta\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}-\beta\theta_{1}^{2}\Big)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\overline{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\exp(-\beta\theta_{1}^{2})\Big(\frac{\pi}{\beta^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Big[\exp\Big(u(S_{n,t,v})+\sum_{1\leq i\leq n-1}u(S_{i,t})+\theta_{d}\sqrt{(1-t)r}Y\Big)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\gamma\big(\bar{\theta})

\displaystyle\quad\geq\exp(-\beta\theta_{1}^{2})\Big(\frac{\pi}{\beta^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\exp\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Big[u(S_{n,t,v})+\sum_{1\leq i\leq n-1}u(S_{i,t})+\theta_{d}\sqrt{(1-t)r}Y\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\gamma\big(\bar{\theta})\Bigg)\,.

Next by rotational invariance of
\gamma
, we have the following bound, as stated in (3.24) in
[
Tal10
]
:

\displaystyle\int\big|\langle x,\overline{\theta}\rangle\big|\mathrm{d}\gamma\big(\bar{\theta})=\sqrt{\frac{1}{\pi\beta^{\prime}}}\|x\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta^{\prime}}}\|x\|\quad\text{for all}\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\,.

(D.16)

Also define
\bar{g_{i}}^{\prime}:=\Big(g_{i,2},\ldots,g_{i,d-1},\sqrt{t}g_{i,d}+\frac{\sqrt{d}}{n}\cdot\sqrt{(1-t)r}Y\Big)
. Using (
D.16
), it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Big[u(S_{n,t,v})+\sum_{1\leq i\leq n-1}u(S_{i,t})+\theta_{d}\sqrt{(1-t)r}Y\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\gamma\big(\bar{\theta})

\displaystyle\quad\geq-D(\kappa,\lambda)\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Bigg[\Big|\sqrt{v}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{2\leq j\leq d-1}g_{n,j}\theta_{j}+\sqrt{\frac{t}{d}}g_{n,d}\theta_{d}\Big)+\sqrt{1-v}\big(\sqrt{q_{n,d}}Z+\sqrt{\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d}}W\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{1\leq i\leq n-1}\Big|\frac{s_{i}\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{d}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{2\leq j\leq d-1}g_{i,j}\theta_{j}+\sqrt{\frac{t}{d}}g_{i,d}\theta_{d}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Big|v\cdot\frac{s_{n}\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{d}}+(1-v)s_{n}x\Big|+n+\theta_{d}\sqrt{(1-t)r}Y\Bigg]\,\mathrm{d}\gamma\big(\bar{\theta})\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\geq-D(\kappa,\lambda)\Bigg(n+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n-1}\int\big|\langle\bar{g}_{i}^{\prime},\bar{\theta}\big|\rangle\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{\theta})+\sqrt{\frac{v}{d}}\int\big|\langle\bar{g}_{n}^{\prime},\bar{\theta}\rangle\big|\mathrm{d}\gamma(\bar{\theta})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{|\theta_{1}|}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}|s_{i}|+|Z|\sqrt{(1-v)q_{n,d}}+(1-v)|s_{n}|+K\sqrt{(1-v)(\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d})}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\geq-D(\kappa,\lambda)\Bigg(n+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d\beta^{\prime}}}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\bar{g}_{i}^{\prime}\|+\frac{|\theta_{1}|}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}|s_{i}|+|Z|\sqrt{(1-v)q_{n,d}}+|s_{n}|+K\sqrt{\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d}}\Bigg)\,.

Here, in the second inequality we took expectation with respect to
W
and used the bound
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\big[|W|\big]\leq K
, and in the third inequality we used (
D.16
).

Next, remark that
\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\big|s_{i}\big|\leq\big(n\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}s_{i}^{2}\big)^{1/2}
. Also by Triangle Inequality and as
|r|\leq K(D)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\bar{g_{i}}^{\prime}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\Big(\|\bar{g}_{i}\|+\frac{\sqrt{d(1-t)r}}{n}|Y|\Big)\leq\Big(\frac{n}{d}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\bar{g}_{i}\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+K(D)|Y|\,.

Now specializing to when
t=v=1
, combining the above displays, recalling the definition
\beta^{\prime}=K_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}}(D)+\beta
, and using that
\frac{\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{d}}\in I\subseteq[-1,1]
,
n\leq\alpha_{0}d\leq 2d
and the definition of
B^{\star}
from (
D.13
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Big[\exp\Big(u(S_{n,1,1})+\sum_{1\leq i\leq n-1}u(S_{i,1})\beta\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}-\beta\theta_{1}^{2}\Big)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\overline{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\exp(-\beta\theta_{1}^{2})\Big(\frac{\pi}{\beta^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\exp\Bigg(-D(\kappa,\lambda)\Big(n+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d\beta^{\prime}}}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\bar{g}_{i}^{\prime}\|+\frac{|\theta_{1}|}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}|s_{i}|+|s_{n}|\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\exp\Bigg(-K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa)\Big(d+\Big(\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\bar{g}_{i}\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+\Big(d\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}s_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+|Y|\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\exp\Big(-K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa)B^{\star}\Big)\,.

Using that
|\sqrt{d}I|\geq 2\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, that
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\geq\exp(-d)
if
d\geq d_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}}(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
, and that
B^{\star}\geq d
, we thus obtain when
t=v=1
,

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}\geq\int_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\theta_{1}\in I}\exp\Big(-K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa)B^{\star}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}\geq\exp\Big(-K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa)B^{\star}\Big)\,.

The condition of Lemma
B.19
thus applies, and combining it with Lemma
D.8
yields that

\displaystyle 0\leq|q_{n,d}|\leq\rho_{n,d}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big\langle\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\Big[K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)B^{\star}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{d}\cdot K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)d=K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,.

(D.17)

We now return to the proof for general
t,v\in[0,1]^{2}
. Again by the aforementioned upper bounds on
\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}|s_{i}|
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\bar{g}_{i}^{\prime}\|
, recalling the definition
\beta^{\prime}=K_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}}(D)+\beta
, and using that
\frac{\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{d}}\in I\subseteq[-1,1]
,
n\leq\alpha_{0}d\leq 2d
and the definition of
B^{\star}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d-1}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Big[\exp\Big(u(S_{n,t,v})+\sum_{1\leq i\leq n-1}u(S_{i,t})\beta\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}-\beta\theta_{1}^{2}\Big)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\overline{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\exp(-\beta\theta_{1}^{2})\Big(\frac{\pi}{\beta^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\exp\Bigg(-D(\kappa,\lambda)\Big(n+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d\beta^{\prime}}}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\bar{g}_{i}^{\prime}\|+\frac{|\theta_{1}|}{\sqrt{d}}\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}|s_{i}|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+|Z|\sqrt{(1-v)q_{n,d}}+K\sqrt{\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d}}+|s_{n}|\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\exp\Bigg(-K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\Big(d+\Big(\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\bar{g}_{i}\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+\Big(d\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}s_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+|Y|+|Z|\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\exp\Big(-K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)B^{\star}\Big)\,.

Here, we used (
D.17
) to upper bound
|\rho_{n,d}|,|q_{n,d}|
and hence
K\sqrt{\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d}}
.

Using that
|\sqrt{d}I|\geq 2\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}
, that
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\geq\exp(-d)
if
d\geq d_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}}(\varepsilon^{\prime\prime})
, and that
B^{\star}\geq d
, we thus obtain for general
t,v\in[0,1]^{2}
that

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}\geq\int_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\theta_{1}\in I}\exp\Big(-K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)B^{\star}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}\geq\exp\Big(-K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)B^{\star}\Big)\,.

This proves Lemma
D.9
.
∎

We now have the ingredients needed to prove Proposition
D.5
.

Proof of Proposition
D.5
.

First, we claim that

\displaystyle\big\langle\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2k}\big\rangle\leq\big\langle\|\bar{\theta}\|^{4k}\big\rangle^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\big(K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}\big)^{k}\,,

(D.18)

\displaystyle\Big\langle\exp\Big(\frac{\beta}{2}\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}\Big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(K_{\textup{\ref{lem:logconcaveboundmomentslemma}}}(K_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}},D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}\big)\,.

(D.19)

Note that the functions we take averages with respect to in the Gibbs measure
\langle\cdot\rangle
above are a function of
L=1
replica. Next, note we have
Z^{\prime}\geq\exp(-K_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}}B^{\star})
by Lemma
D.9
. For the interpolators or for the posterior in the logistic case where
u\leq 0
, (
D.18
), (
D.19
) follow immediately by Lemma
B.19
, as the corresponding
U(\cdot)
from (
B.84
) is such that
U\leq 0
and all the
a_{j}=0
. For the posterior in the GMM case, since
u(x)=\sqrt{\lambda}x
, we note Lemma
B.18
applies where

\displaystyle a_{0}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-v}\big(\sqrt{q_{n,d}}Z+\sqrt{\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d}}W\big)+s_{n}\cdot(1-v)x\,,

\displaystyle a_{1}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{d}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}s_{i}+s_{n}v\Big)\,,

\displaystyle a_{j}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{d}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{ij}+\sqrt{v}g_{nj}\Big)\text{ for all }2\leq j\leq d-1\,,

\displaystyle a_{d}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\lambda t}{d}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{ij}+\sqrt{v}g_{nj}\Big)+\sqrt{(1-t)r}Y\,,

\displaystyle U(\theta)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(1-t)(r-\bar{r})}{2}\theta_{d}^{2}\leq 0\,.

By (
D.17
), using that
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}[\exp KW]\leq K
we have

\displaystyle\log\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\big[\exp a_{0}\big]\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\big(|Z|+|s_{n}|\big)+K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,.

It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\beta_{0}}\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}a_{j}^{2}+\log\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\big[\exp a_{0}\big]\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)B^{\star}\,.

Combining the above display with Lemma
B.19
and Lemma
D.9
now establishes (
D.18
), (
D.19
).

We first prove the desired result for
R_{1,2}
. Let
m=\big\langle\bar{\theta}\big\rangle
, thus
\langle R_{1,2}\rangle=\frac{\|m\|^{2}}{d}
, and

\big|R_{1,2}-\langle R_{1,2}\rangle\big|\leq\Big|\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},\bar{\theta}^{2}\rangle}{d}-\frac{\langle\bar{\theta^{1}},m\rangle}{d}\Big|+\Big|\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},m\rangle}{N}-\frac{\langle m,m\rangle}{N}\Big|\,.

For fixed
\theta^{1}
, the map
f(x)=\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},x\rangle}{d}
is Lipschitz with constant
\frac{\|\bar{\theta}^{1}\|}{d}
. Letting
\mu_{I}(\theta^{l})
denote the Gibbs measure w.r.t. only the
l
-th replica, which is
\beta
strongly log-concave, Theorem
B.3
gives

\int\Big(\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},\bar{\theta}^{2}\rangle}{d}-\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},m\rangle}{d}\Big)^{2k}\mathrm{d}\mu_{I}(\theta^{2})\leq\Big(\frac{k\,K(\beta)\,\|\bar{\theta}^{1}\|^{2}}{d^{2}}\Big)^{k}\,.

We now integrate this inequality for
\theta^{1}
with respect to
\mu_{I}(\theta^{1})
. By (
D.18
),

\displaystyle\Big\langle\Big(\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},\bar{\theta}^{2}\rangle}{d}-\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},m\rangle}{d}\Big)^{2k}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\Big(\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},\bar{\theta}^{2}\rangle}{d}-\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},m\rangle}{d}\Big)^{2k}\mathrm{d}\mu_{I}(\theta^{1})\mathrm{d}\mu_{I}(\theta^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{k\,K(\beta)}{d^{2}}\Big)^{k}\big\langle\|\bar{\theta}^{1}\|^{2k}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{k\,K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}}{d^{2}}\Big)^{k}\,.

(D.20)

Likewise, the map
f(x)=\frac{\langle x,m\rangle}{d}
is Lipschitz with constant
\frac{\|m\|}{d}
. Thus Theorem
B.3
gives

\Big\langle\Big(\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},m\rangle}{d}-\frac{\langle m,m\rangle}{d}\Big)^{2k}\Big\rangle=\int\Big(\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},m\rangle}{d}-\frac{\langle m,m\rangle}{d}\Big)^{2k}\mathrm{d}\mu_{I}(\theta^{1})\leq\Big(\frac{k\,K(\beta)\,\|m\|^{2}}{d^{2}}\Big)^{k}~.

Again by Lemma
B.19
and Jensen’s Inequality repeatedly, this gives

\|m\|^{2k}=\big\|\langle\bar{\theta}\rangle\big\|^{2k}\leq\big\langle\|\bar{\theta}\|\big\rangle^{2k}\leq\big\langle\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2k}\big\rangle\leq\big(K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}\big)^{k}\,.

Combining with the above display yields

\displaystyle\Big\langle\Big(\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{1},m\rangle}{d}-\frac{\langle m,m\rangle}{d}\Big)^{2k}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{k\,K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}}{d^{2}}\Big)^{k}\,.

(D.21)

As
(a+b)^{2k}\leq 2^{2k-1}(a^{2k}+b^{2k})
, combining (
D.20
) and (
D.21
), we obtain the desired upper bound for
\big\langle(R_{1,2}-\langle R_{1,2}\rangle)^{2k}\big\rangle
.

We now prove the result for
R_{1,1}
. The idea is to truncate
R_{1,1}
into a part
f
where we have the requisite bounds, and another part
\phi
which is 0 with high probability. Specifically, define the parameter

\displaystyle a:=\frac{K_{\textup{\ref{lem:logconcaveboundmomentslemma}}}(K_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}},D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\sqrt{6B^{\star}}}{\sqrt{\beta_{0}}}>0

controlling the truncation, and in terms of
a
define

f(\theta)=\min\Big\{\frac{\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}}{d},\frac{a^{2}}{d}\Big\}=\frac{\big(\min\{\|\bar{\theta}\|,a\}\big)^{2}}{d}\,.

As the cylinder
\big\{\theta:\|\bar{\theta}\|\leq a\big\}
is convex, it follows that
f
is Lipschitz with constant
\frac{2a}{d}
. Thus Theorem
B.3
gives

\displaystyle\Big\langle\big(f-\langle f\rangle\big)^{2k}\Big\rangle\leq\Big(\frac{k\,K(\beta)\,a^{2}}{d^{2}}\Big)^{k}\,.

(D.22)

Define

\displaystyle\phi(\theta):=\frac{\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}}{d}-f(\theta)\geq 0\,.

Thus
\phi(\theta)\leq\frac{\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}}{d}\,\mathbf{1}\{\|\bar{\theta}\|\geq a\}
.
By Lemma
D.7
and Cauchy–Schwarz,

\displaystyle\Big\langle\big(\phi-\langle\phi\rangle\big)^{2k}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2k}\big\langle\phi^{2k}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2k}\Big\langle\Big(\frac{\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}}{d}\Big)^{2k}\,\mathbf{1}\{\|\bar{\theta}\|\geq a\}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2k}\Big\langle\Big(\frac{\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}}{d}\Big)^{4k}\Big\rangle^{1/2}\Big\langle\mathbf{1}\{\|\bar{\theta}\|\geq a\}^{2k}\Big\rangle^{1/2}

\displaystyle=2^{2k}\Big\langle\Big(\frac{\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}}{d}\Big)^{4k}\Big\rangle^{1/2}\Big\langle\mathbf{1}\{\|\bar{\theta}\|\geq a\}\Big\rangle^{1/2}\,.

(D.23)

By (
D.18
), (
D.19
), Markov’s Inequality and the choice of
a
gives

\displaystyle\Big\langle\mathbf{1}\{\|\bar{\theta}\|\geq a\}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\exp\Big(\frac{\beta}{2}\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}\Big)\geq\exp\Big(\frac{\beta a^{2}}{2}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(K_{\textup{\ref{lem:logconcaveboundmomentslemma}}}(K_{\textup{\ref{lem:lem316}}},D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}-\frac{\beta a^{2}}{2}\Big)\leq\exp\big(-2B^{\star}\big)\,.

(D.24)

Plugging in (
D.18
), (
D.24
) into (
D.23
), we obtain

\Big\langle\big(\phi-\langle\phi\rangle\big)^{2k}\Big\rangle\leq\exp\big(-B^{\star}\big)\Big(\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}}{d}\Big)^{2k}\,.

Since
R_{1,1}=\|\bar{\theta}\|^{2}/d=f+\phi
, using that
(a+b)^{2k}\leq 2^{2k}(a^{2k}+b^{2k})
, combining with (
D.22
) and recalling our choice of
a
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\langle\big(R_{1,1}-\big\langle R_{1,1}\big\rangle\big)^{2k}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2k}\Bigg(\Big\langle\big(f-\langle f\rangle\big)^{2k}\Big\rangle+\Big\langle\big(\phi-\langle\phi\rangle\big)^{2k}\Big\rangle\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{k\,K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}}{d^{2}}\Big)^{k}+\exp\big(-B^{\star}\big)\Big(\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}}{d}\Big)^{2k}\,.

Note for
x\geq 0
that
x^{k}\leq k^{k}\exp(x)
, thus
\exp(-x)\leq\left(k/x\right)^{k}
for
x>0
.
Clearly
B^{\star}>0
.
Hence

\displaystyle\exp\big(-B^{\star}\big)\Big(\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}}{d}\Big)^{2k}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{k}{B^{\star}}\Big)^{k}\Big(\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}}{d}\Big)^{2k}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{k\,K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)\,B^{\star}}{d^{2}}\Big)^{k}\,.

Combining the above two displays gives the desired upper bound on
\big\langle(R_{1,1}-\big\langle R_{1,1}\big\rangle)^{2k}\big\rangle
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
D.6

We will follow the strategy of
[
Tal10
]
of considering
\langle R_{1,1}\rangle
and
\langle R_{1,2}\rangle
as functions of the disorder
(s_{j})_{j\leq n}\times(g_{i,j})_{2\leq i\leq n,1\leq j\leq d}\times(W^{l})_{1\leq l\leq L}\times Z\times Y
. The density of the disorder is strongly log-concave by Assumption
1
, and to apply Theorem
B.3
on concentration of Lipschitz functions of strongly log-concave measures, we need to establish control on the Lipschitz constant of these functions.

Lemma D.10
.

Let
f=R_{1,1}
or
R_{1,2}
and let
\nabla
denote the derivative of the function
\langle f\rangle
w.r.t. the disorder. Let

\displaystyle u(\theta):=\Big(u^{\prime}\big(S_{1,t}(\theta)\big),\ldots,u^{\prime}\big(S_{n-1,t}(\theta)\big),u^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}(\theta)\big)\Big)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,,

and analogously define
u^{l}(\theta^{l})
for each replica
l
. Then
\|\nabla\|^{2}
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle K\Bigg(\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{\theta^{1}_{d}}\big\rangle^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\Big\|\Big\langle\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)\dot{f}u^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}^{1}(\theta^{1})\big)\Big\rangle\Big\|^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i\leq n-1}\Big\|\Big\langle\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)\dot{f}u^{\prime}\big(S_{i,t}^{1}(\theta^{1})\big)\Big\rangle\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Big(1+\frac{1}{d}\big\langle\|\theta^{1}\|^{2}\big\rangle\Big)\Big\|\Big\langle\big(u^{1}(\theta^{1})-\big\langle u^{1}(\theta^{1})\big\rangle\big)\dot{f}\Big\rangle\Big\|^{2}\Bigg)\,,

where
K
depends on
D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
and also on
\kappa
for the interpolators, and where we use the notation

\displaystyle\dot{f}:=f-\langle f\rangle\,,\,\dot{\theta^{l}}:=\theta^{l}-\langle\theta^{l}\rangle\,,\,\dot{\theta_{j}^{l}}:=\theta_{j}^{l}-\langle\theta_{j}^{l}\rangle\,,

and

\displaystyle\dot{u}^{\prime}(S^{l}):=u^{\prime}(S^{l})-\langle u^{\prime}(S^{l})\rangle\quad\text{ for }\quad S^{l}=S^{l}_{i,t}(\theta^{l})\text{ or }S^{l}_{n,t,v}(\theta^{l})\,.

Proof.

First, consider
x
an element of the quenched disorder, i.e. not one of the
W^{l}
. For just this proof, we let
H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta^{l}):=u\big(S^{l}_{n,t,v}(\theta^{l})\big)+H_{t,n-1,d}(\theta^{l})-\beta\|\theta^{l}\|^{2}
. By (
D.11
), we remark that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\langle f\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z^{\prime L}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Bigg[f\sum_{1\leq l\leq L}\Big\{\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\big(H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta^{l})\big)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial x}H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta^{l})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\prod_{1\leq l^{\prime}\neq l\leq L}\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l^{\prime}}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\big(H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta^{l^{\prime}})\big)\Big\}\Bigg]\,\mathrm{d}\theta^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\theta^{L}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{L}{Z^{\prime L+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Bigg[f\prod_{1\leq l\leq L}\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\big(H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta^{l})\big)\Bigg]\,\mathrm{d}\theta^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\theta^{L}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{\theta^{L+1}_{1}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\big(H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta^{L+1})\big)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial x}H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta^{L+1})\Big]\,\mathrm{d}\theta^{L+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq l\leq L}\Big\langle f\frac{\partial}{\partial x}H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta^{l})\Big\rangle-\langle f\rangle\Big\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial x}H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta)\Big\rangle\,.

Note that the
\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L}
such that there exists some
l^{\prime}
with
\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\theta^{l^{\prime}}\not\in I
make no contribution to
\langle f\rangle
. Thus
\langle f\rangle=\langle f^{\prime}\rangle
where
f^{\prime}
is the restriction of
f
to
\prod_{1\leq l\leq L}\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}
. Consequently we have

\displaystyle\Big\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial x}H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta)\Big\rangle=\begin{cases}\Big\langle u^{\prime}\big(S_{i,t}(\theta)\big)\cdot\frac{\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{d}}\Big\rangle&:x=s_{i},i\leq n-1\\
\Big\langle u^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}(\theta)\big)\cdot\Big(\frac{v\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{d}}+(1-v)x\Big)\Big\rangle&:x=s_{n}\\
\Big\langle u^{\prime}\big(S_{i,t}(\theta)\big)\cdot\frac{\theta_{j}}{\sqrt{d}}\Big\rangle&:x=g_{i,j},i\leq n-1,j\leq d-1\\
\Big\langle u^{\prime}\big(S_{i,t}(\theta)\big)\cdot\theta_{d}\sqrt{\frac{t}{d}}\Big\rangle&:x=g_{i,d},i\leq n-1\\
\Big\langle u^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}(\theta)\big)\cdot\theta_{j}\sqrt{\frac{v}{d}}\Big\rangle&:x=g_{n,j},j\leq d-1\\
\Big\langle u^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}(\theta)\big)\cdot\theta_{d}\sqrt{\frac{vt}{d}}\Big\rangle&:x=g_{n,d}\\
\Big\langle u^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}(\theta)\big)\cdot\sqrt{(1-v)q_{n,d}}\Big\rangle&:x=Z\\
\Big\langle\theta_{d}\sqrt{(1-t)r}\Big\rangle&:x=Y\,.\end{cases}

An analogous derivation applies also for
\Big\langle f\frac{\partial}{\partial x}H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta^{l})\Big\rangle
.

Now consider
x=W^{l}
for some
1\leq l\leq L
. We remark that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial W^{l}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W^{l}}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\big(H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta)\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad=-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W^{l}}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\big(H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta)\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W^{l}}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\big(H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta)\big)\cdot u^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}^{l}(\theta^{l})\big)\frac{\partial}{\partial W^{l}}u\big(S_{n,t,v}^{l}(\theta^{l})\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad=-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W^{l}}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\big(H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta)\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}_{W^{l}}\Big[\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}\exp\big(H_{t,v,n,d}(\theta)\big)\cdot u^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}^{l}(\theta^{l})\big)\sqrt{(1-v)(\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d})}\Big]\,,

where the last step follows from the precense of the constraint
\mathbf{1}\{\theta_{1}^{l}/\sqrt{d}\in I\}
and analogous logic as above. Since
f=R_{1,1}
or
R_{1,2}
does not depend on the
W^{l}
, it follows from an identical derivation as above that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial W^{l}}\langle f\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq l\leq L}-\langle f\rangle+\langle f\rangle+\sqrt{(1-v)(\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d})}\Big(\Big\langle fu^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}^{l}(\theta^{l})\big)\Big\rangle-\langle f\rangle\Big\langle u^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}^{l}(\theta^{l})\big)\Big\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq l\leq L}\sqrt{(1-v)(\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d})}\Big(\Big\langle fu^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}^{l}(\theta^{l})\big)\Big\rangle-\langle f\rangle\Big\langle u^{\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}^{l}(\theta^{l})\big)\Big\rangle\Big)\,.

Note
\langle\dot{f}\rangle=0
. Thus for any
1\leq l\leq L
,

\displaystyle\big\langle\dot{f}u^{\prime}(S^{l})\big\rangle=\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{u}^{\prime}(S^{l})\big\rangle\,,\,\big\langle\dot{f}\theta^{l}_{d}\big\rangle=\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{\theta^{l}_{d}}\big\rangle\,,

and for any
1\leq l\leq L
and any
1\leq j\leq d
,

\displaystyle\big\langle\dot{f}\theta^{l}_{j}u^{\prime}(S^{l})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{\theta^{l}_{j}}u^{\prime}(S^{l})\big\rangle+\big\langle\theta^{l}_{j}\big\rangle\big\langle\dot{f}u^{\prime}(S^{l})\big\rangle=\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{\theta^{l}_{j}}u^{\prime}(S^{l})\big\rangle+\big\langle\theta^{l}_{j}\big\rangle\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{u}^{\prime}(S^{l})\big\rangle\,.

Since
t,v\in[0,1]^{2}
,
L\leq 2
for
f=R_{1,1},R_{1,2}
, and as
f=R_{1,1},R_{1,2}
are symmetric in
\theta^{1},\theta^{2}
, combining all of the displays above implies that

\displaystyle\|\nabla\|^{2}\leq K\Big(\textsc{(I)}+\textsc{(II)}+\textsc{(III)}\Big)\,

where

(I)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{\theta^{1}_{j}}u^{\prime}\big(S^{1}_{n,t,v}(\theta^{1})\big)\big\rangle^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i\leq n-1,1\leq j\leq d}\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{\theta^{1}_{j}}u^{\prime}\big(S_{i,t}(\theta^{1})\big)\big\rangle^{2}\,,

(II)

\displaystyle:=\Big(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}\langle\theta^{1}_{j}\rangle^{2}\Big)\Big(\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{u}^{\prime}\big(S^{1}_{n,t,v}(\theta^{1})\big)\big\rangle^{2}+\sum_{i\leq n-1}\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{u}^{\prime}\big(S_{i,t}(\theta^{1})\big)\big\rangle^{2}\Big)\,,

(III)

\displaystyle:=\max\big\{x^{2},q_{n,d},r,\rho_{n,d}-q_{n,d}\big\}\Big(\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{u}^{\prime}\big(S^{1}_{n,t,v}(\theta^{1})\big)\big\rangle^{2}+\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{\theta^{1}_{d}}\big\rangle^{2}\Big)\,.

Note
|x|\leq 1
and that
r\leq K(D)
. Moreover by (
D.17
), we have
|q_{n,d}|,|\rho_{n,d}|\leq K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)
. Also remark that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}\langle\theta^{1}_{j}\rangle^{2}\leq\frac{1}{d}\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}\langle(\theta^{1}_{j})^{2}\rangle=\frac{1}{d}\big\langle\|\theta^{1}\|^{2}\big\rangle\,.

Next, for
S^{1}
either
S^{1}_{n,t,v}(\theta^{1})
or
S_{i,t}(\theta^{1})
, we observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}\Big\langle\dot{f}\dot{\theta^{1}_{j}}u^{\prime}\big(S^{1}(\theta^{1})\big)\Big\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}\Big\langle\dot{f}(\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\dot{f}(\theta^{L+1},\ldots,\theta^{2L})u^{\prime}\big(S^{1}(\theta^{1})\big)u^{\prime}\big(S^{1}(\theta^{L+1})\big)\dot{\theta^{1}_{j}}\dot{\theta^{L+1}_{j}}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\Big\langle\dot{f}(\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\dot{f}(\theta^{L+1},\ldots,\theta^{2L})u^{\prime}\big(S^{1}(\theta^{1})\big)u^{\prime}\big(S^{1}(\theta^{L+1})\big)\cdot\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)^{T}\big(\theta^{L+1}-\langle\theta^{L+1}\rangle\big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\Big\|\Big\langle\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)\dot{f}u^{\prime}(S^{1})\Big\rangle\Big\|^{2}\,.

Analogously, we have

\displaystyle\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{u}^{\prime}\big(S^{1}_{n,t,v}(\theta^{1})\big)\big\rangle^{2}+\sum_{i\leq n-1}\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{u}^{\prime}\big(S_{i,t}(\theta^{1})\big)\big\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\Big\langle\dot{f}(\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})\dot{f}(\theta^{L+1},\ldots,\theta^{2L})\cdot\Big(u\big(S^{1}(\theta^{1})\big)-\big\langle u\big(S^{1}(\theta^{1})\big)\big\rangle\Big)^{T}\Big(u\big(S^{1}(\theta^{L+1})\big)-\big\langle u\big(S^{1}(\theta^{L+1})\big)\big\rangle\Big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=\Big\|\Big\langle\big(u\big(S^{1}(\theta^{1})\big)-\big\langle u\big(S^{1}(\theta^{1})\big)\big\rangle\big)\dot{f}\Big\rangle\Big\|^{2}\,.

Using these observations, the Lemma follows from combining terms in the above expressions
\textsc{(I)},\textsc{(II)},\textsc{(III)}
.
∎

We now aim to upper bound the expressions appearing in the bound from Lemma
D.10
.

Lemma D.11
.

For any function
f
of
\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L}
mapping to
\mathbb{R}
(possibly depending on the disorder), we have

\Big\|\Big\langle\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)f\Big\rangle\Big\|^{2}\leq K\langle f^{2}\rangle\,,

and consequently we also have
\big\langle f\dot{\theta^{1}_{d}}\big\rangle^{2}\leq K\langle f^{2}\rangle
, where
K
depends on
\beta_{0},\beta_{1}
.

Proof.

Consider any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\Big|\big\langle\Big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\Big)f\big\rangle^{T}y\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big\langle f\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)^{T}y\Big\rangle\leq\big\langle f^{2}\big\rangle^{1/2}\Big\langle\Big(\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)^{T}y\Big)^{2}\Big\rangle^{1/2}\,.

By Theorem
B.3
applied with the function
(\theta^{1})^{T}y
of
\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L}
which is
\|y\|
-Lipschitz,

\displaystyle\Big\langle\Big(\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)^{T}y\Big)^{2}\Big\rangle\leq K(\beta_{0},\beta_{1})\|y\|^{2}\,.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)f\Big\rangle^{T}y\Big|

\displaystyle\leq K(\beta_{0},\beta_{1})\big\langle f^{2}\big\rangle^{1/2}\|y\|\,,

and since
y
is arbitrary the first part of the Lemma follows. The second part of this Lemma follows upon observing that analogously as in the proof of Lemma
D.10
, we have

\displaystyle\big\langle f\dot{\theta^{1}_{d}}\big\rangle^{2}\leq\sum_{1\leq j\leq d}\big\langle f\dot{\theta^{1}_{j}}\big\rangle^{2}=\Big\|\Big\langle\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)f\Big\rangle\Big\|^{2}\,.

Applying the first part of this Lemma then finishes the proof.
∎

To bound the other term in Lemma
D.10
, define the matrix
M
as follows: for all
1\leq i\leq n
and
1\leq j\leq d
, we let

\displaystyle(M)_{i,j}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}s_{i}&:1\leq i\leq n-1,j=1\\
g_{i,j}&:1\leq i\leq n-1,2\leq j\leq d-1\\
\sqrt{t}g_{i,d}&:1\leq i\leq n-1,j=d\\
vs_{n}&:i=n,j=1\\
\sqrt{v}g_{n,j}&:i=n,2\leq j\leq d-1\\
\sqrt{vt}g_{n,d}&:i=n,j=d\,.\end{cases}

(D.25)

\displaystyle B^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\|M\|_{\textsc{op}}\,.

(D.26)

We now control
B^{\prime}
as follows:

Lemma D.12
.

We have
B^{\prime 2}\leq Kd
with probability at least
1-K\exp(-d)
, where
K
depends on
\lambda
.

Proof.

Note
B^{\prime 2}=\|MM^{T}\|_{\textsc{op}}
. Consider any unit vector
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Note for all
1\leq j\leq d
that

\displaystyle\big(M^{T}y\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}s_{i}y_{i}+vs_{n}y_{n}&:j=1\\
\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{i,j}y_{i}+\sqrt{v}g_{n,j}y_{n}&:2\leq j\leq d-1\\
\sqrt{t}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{i,d}y_{i}+\sqrt{tv}g_{n,d}y_{n}&:j=d\,.\end{cases}

(D.27)

Let
\bar{M}
be the
n\times d-1
matrix comprising of the last
d-1
columns of
M
; note this matrix does not involve the
s_{i}
, which we handle in a separate step. Observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=2}^{d-1}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{i,j}y_{i}+\sqrt{v}g_{n,j}y_{n}\Big)^{2}+\Big(\sqrt{t}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}g_{i,d}y_{i}+\sqrt{tv}g_{n,d}y_{n}\Big)^{2}=\|\bar{M}^{T}y\|^{2}\leq\|\bar{M}\|_{\textsc{op}}^{2}\,.

(D.28)

By concentration of operator norm for matrices with i.i.d. sub-Gaussian entries, e.g. Theorem 4.4.5 of
[
Ver18
]
, as
t,v\in[0,1]^{2}
we have
\|\bar{M}\|_{\textsc{op}}\leq K\sqrt{d}
with probability at least
1-K\exp(-d)
.
Also, as
v\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}s_{i}y_{i}+vs_{n}y_{n}\Big)^{2}\leq\|y\|^{2}\|s\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}^{2}\,.

(D.29)

As each
s_{i}^{2}
is
K_{\textsc{signal}}
sub-Exponential and
n\leq 2d
, the latter is at most
K(K_{\textsc{signal}})d
with probability at least
1-K\exp(-d)
by Bernstein’s Inequality. Combining (
D.27
) with the aforementioned high-probability bounds for (
D.28
) and (
D.29
), the Lemma follows.
∎

Next note for any sequences
(x_{j})_{j\leq d}
,
(y_{i})_{i\leq n}
, we have

\sum_{i\leq n,j\leq d}M_{i,j}x_{j}y_{i}\leq B^{\prime}\Big(\sum_{j\leq d}x_{j}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\sum_{i\leq n}y_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\,.

Now applying the above inequality for
x_{j}=\sum_{i\leq n}M_{i,j}y_{i}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{j\leq d}\Big(\sum_{i\leq n}M_{i,j}y_{i}\Big)^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\leq B^{\prime}\Big(\sum_{i\leq n}y_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\,.

(D.30)

Lemma D.13
.

Consider any sequence
y=(y_{i})_{i\leq n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then the function
f(\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L})=\big\langle u^{1}(\theta^{1}),y\big\rangle
is Lipschitz with constant
B^{\prime}D\|y\|/\sqrt{d}
.

Proof.

Clearly
f
only depends on
\theta^{1}
among
\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L}
. Note for all
1\leq j\leq d
that by construction of
M_{i,j}
,

\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta^{1}_{j}}\big\langle u^{1}(\theta^{1}),y\big\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\Big(\sum_{1\leq i\leq n-1}y_{i}u^{\prime\prime}\big(S_{i,t}(\theta^{1})\big)M_{i,j}+y_{n}u^{\prime\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}(\theta^{1})\big)M_{n,j}\Big)\,.

Applying (
D.30
) for the sequence
\Big(y_{1}u^{\prime\prime}\big(S_{1,t}(\theta^{1})_{,}\ldots,y_{n-1}u^{\prime\prime}\big(S_{n-1,t}(\theta^{1})\big),y_{n}u^{\prime\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}(\theta^{1})\big)\Big)
and recalling
|u^{\prime\prime}|\leq D
now gives that

\Big(\sum_{j\leq d}\Big(\frac{\partial f}{\partial\theta^{1}_{j}}\Big)^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\leq\frac{B^{\prime}}{\sqrt{d}}\Big(\sum_{i\leq n-1}y_{i}^{2}u^{\prime\prime}\big(S_{i,t}(\theta^{1})\big)^{2}+y_{n}^{2}u^{\prime\prime}\big(S_{n,t,v}(\theta^{1})\big)^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\leq\frac{B^{\prime}D\|y\|}{\sqrt{d}}\,.

Since
f
only depends on
\theta^{1}
, the conclusion follows.
∎

Lemma D.14
.

For any function
f
of
\theta^{1},\ldots,\theta^{L}
mapping to
\mathbb{R}
(possibly depending on the disorder), we have

\Big\|\Big\langle\big(u^{1}(\theta^{1})-\big\langle u^{1}(\theta^{1})\big\rangle\big)f\Big\rangle\Big\|^{2}\leq\frac{KB^{\prime 2}}{d}\langle f^{2}\rangle\,,

where
K
depends on
D,\beta
.

Proof.

Consider any vector
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. By Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\Big\langle\big(u^{1}(\theta^{1})-\big\langle u^{1}(\theta^{1})\big\rangle\big)f\Big\rangle^{T}y

\displaystyle=\Big\langle f\big(u^{1}(\theta^{1})-\big\langle u^{1}(\theta^{1})\big\rangle\big)^{T}y\Big\rangle\leq\langle f^{2}\rangle^{1/2}\Big\langle\Big(\big(u^{1}(\theta^{1})-\big\langle u^{1}(\theta^{1})\big\rangle\big)^{T}y\Big)^{2}\Big\rangle^{1/2}\,.

Consider the function
\theta\rightarrow\big\langle u(\theta),y\big\rangle
. By Lemma
D.13
, this function is Lipschitz with constant
B^{\prime}D\|y\|/\sqrt{d}
.
Applying Theorem
B.3
, we obtain

\Big\langle\Big(\big(u^{1}(\theta^{1})-\big\langle u^{1}(\theta^{1})\big\rangle\big)^{T}y\Big)^{2}\Big\rangle\leq\frac{K(\beta)B^{\prime 2}D^{2}\|y\|^{2}}{d}\,.

Since
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
was arbitrary, the desired conclusion follows.
∎

Corollary D.15
.

For
f=R_{1,1},R_{1,2}
, defining
\nabla
as in Lemma
D.10
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\|^{2}\leq K\Bigg(\frac{B^{\star}}{d^{2}}+\Big(1+\frac{B^{\star}}{d}\Big)\frac{B^{\star}B^{\prime 2}}{d^{3}}\Bigg)\,,

where
K
depends on
D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\lambda
and also on
\kappa
for the interpolators.

Proof.

Applying the bound from Lemma
D.10
, it remains to upper bound

(I)

\displaystyle:=\big\langle\dot{f}\dot{\theta_{d}^{1}}\big\rangle^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\big\|\langle\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)\dot{f}u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{n,t,v})\rangle\big\|^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i\leq n-1}\big\|\langle\big(\theta^{1}-\langle\theta^{1}\rangle\big)\dot{f}u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{i,t})\rangle\big\|^{2}\,,

(II)

\displaystyle:=\Big(1+\frac{1}{d}\big\langle\|\theta^{1}\|^{2}\big\rangle\Big)\Big\|\Big\langle\big(u^{1}(\theta^{1})-\big\langle u^{1}(\theta^{1})\big\rangle\big)\dot{f}\Big\rangle\Big\|^{2}\,.

First, we upper bound
(I)
. To this end, applying Lemma
D.11
with the function
\dot{f}
or
\dot{f}u^{\prime}(S^{1})
where
S^{1}=S^{1}_{i,t}
or
S^{1}_{n,t,v}
, we have

\displaystyle\textsc{(I)}\leq K(\beta_{0},\beta_{1})\Big(\langle\dot{f}^{2}\rangle+\frac{1}{d}\langle\dot{f}^{2}u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{n,t,v})^{2}\rangle+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i\leq n-1}\langle\dot{f}^{2}u^{\prime}(S^{1}_{i,t})^{2}\rangle\Big)\leq\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)B^{\star}}{d^{2}}\,,

where we use
|u^{\prime}|\leq D
, Proposition
D.5
to control
\langle\dot{f}^{2}\rangle
, and that
n/d\leq\alpha_{0}=2
.

Next, we upper bound
(II)
. First, by (
D.18
),

\displaystyle\Big(1+\frac{1}{d}\big\langle\|\theta^{1}\|^{2}\big\rangle\Big)\leq 1+\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)B^{\star}}{d}\,.

By Lemma
D.14
and Proposition
D.5
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big\langle\big(u^{1}(\theta^{1})-\big\langle u^{1}(\theta^{1})\big\rangle\big)\dot{f}\Big\rangle\Big\|^{2}\leq\frac{K(D,\beta)B^{\prime 2}}{d}\langle\dot{f}^{2}\rangle\leq\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)B^{\star}B^{\prime 2}}{d^{3}}\,.

This yields an upper bound on
(II)
, and combining the upper bounds on
(I)
and
(II)
establishes Corollary
D.15
.
∎

We now have done the necessary preparation to prove Proposition
D.6
.

Proof of Proposition
D.6
.

For this proof, we let
f=\langle R_{1,1}\rangle
or
\langle R_{1,2}\rangle
.
Similarly as the proof of Theorem
B.2
, we consider the space
\mathcal{S}=\mathbb{R}^{nd+L+2}
where the first
n
coordinates correspond to the
s_{j}
, the next
(n-1)d
coordinates correspond to
g_{i,j}
, the next
L
coordinates correspond to
W^{L}
, and the last two coordinates correspond to
Z
and
Y
.
We endow
\mathcal{S}
with the product measure
\gamma
where the measure on each coordinate is given by law of each corresponding part of the disorder, that is,
s_{j},g_{i,j},W^{L},Z
or
Y
. Thus integration w.r.t.
\gamma
corresponds to taking expectation w.r.t. the disorder.

Consider the set

\displaystyle C:=\{x\in\mathcal{S}\,:\,B^{\star}\leq K(\lambda)d\,,\,B^{\prime 2}\leq K(\lambda)d\}\,,

where by (
D.15
) and Lemma
D.12
,
K(\lambda)
has been chosen large enough so that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(C^{c})\leq K(\lambda)\exp(-d)\,,

where probability here is w.r.t.
\gamma
.
Moreover,
C
is a convex subset of
\mathcal{S}
, because
B^{\star}
is a convex function of the disorder, the operator norm of a matrix is a convex function of its entries, and the entries of
M
are an affine transformation of the disorder.

Next, let
\gamma^{\prime}
be a probability measure defined on
C
with density proportional to that of
\gamma
. By Assumption
1
,
\gamma^{\prime}
is
\min\{1,\beta_{\textsc{signal}}\}
strongly log-concave. By Corollary
D.15
,
f
is Lipschitz with constant
\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)}{\sqrt{d}}
on
C
. Let
m=\int f\mathrm{d}\gamma^{\prime}
. Since
C
is convex and since
\gamma^{\prime}
is supported on
C
, Theorem
B.3
gives that for all
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\int\big(f-m)^{2k}\mathrm{d}\gamma^{\prime}\leq\Big(\frac{K(D,\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\kappa,\lambda)k}{d}\Big)^{k}\,.

Thus by definition of
\gamma^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[(f-m)^{2k}\mathbf{1}\{C\}\big]
